Patrologia Latina · Vol. 113 ·

LIBER DEUTERONOMII

One book of the Glossa ordinaria — the whole gloss, book by book.

PL 113, coll. 445–506  ·  Latin from the Migne printing  ·  New English translation  ·  column numbers follow the original plates, not the Garnier reprint

Anselm of Laon and his school

Anselmus Laudunensis et schola

twelfth century

The masters of the cathedral school of Laon, gathered around Anselm (c. 1050–1117), to whom the making of the Glossa ordinaria on the Bible is traditionally credited. The gloss is a school's work rather than one man's: it draws its comment from the Fathers — Rabanus, Isidore, Bede, Jerome, Gregory — and names them as it goes, and it grew by accretion over generations. Migne prints it under the name of Walafrid Strabo, an ascription now known to be mistaken.

Hebraice ELLEH HADDEBARIM ( אלה הדברים ), id est Haec sunt verba.

In Hebrew ELLEH HADDEBARIM ( אלה הדברים ), that is These are the words.

PRAEFATIO.

445D

Fasciculus myrrhae delectus mihi. Myrrha, arbor aromatica, quinque cubitis tantum in altum excrescit, 446cujus cortex et radix et folia et omnia amara sunt. Myrrha amara est et cadaveribus apponitur, ut arceat putredinem vermium. Haec est lex vetus in 447Aquinque libris comprehensa; amara, id est, nemini parcens, unde et Marath designata est; apposita cadaveribus, id est inventa pro peccatoribus, ut corruptionem criminum ab eis arceret. Hujus fasciculus est Deuteronomius, id est ramusculorum plurium in unum collectio. Terminata enim lege in quatuor voluminibus, iste quintus additus est, diffuse dicta in unum breviter colligens, quaedam etiam praetermissa supplens. Additus autem est illis ut numero legali satisfaceret. Quinarius enim numerus est legis, quaternarius Evangelii. Unde et refectio quinque millium hominum in Evangelio ad patres Veteris Testamenti spectat: refectio quatuor millium ad patres novi; illis enim promissa sunt et data terrena, quae quinque sensibus subsunt: illis quadrata, 447Bid est, soliditas terrae viventium. Deservit etiam liber iste Evangelio in nomine, in explanationis serie, in sui dignitate. Dicitur enim Hebraice Elleh Haddebarim, id est, haec verba, Graece δευτερονόμιον, quod est Latine secunda lex: Non ut quidam dixerunt, altera post praedictam, unde in praedictis dicebatur: Locutus est Dominus; hic autem, Locutus est Moyses; sed secunda, id est secundo iterata, hoc nomine Evangelium praefigurans, quod successit legi, cui nullum succedet. Cum etiam multa breviter colligit et supplet quaedam, formam Evangelii praefigurat: hoc est enim abbreviatum verbum quod fecit Dominus super terram, et supplet imperfecta legis; unde: Non veni solvere legem, sed adimplere (Matth. V). Cum autem dicitur 447Choc opus adeo dignum, quod manibus regis gestari jubeatur, dignitatem Evangelii aperit, quod ordinatum est in manu Mediatoris. Cum ergo sic serviat Evangelio, merito est fasciculus dilectus Christo, non tantum pro se, quantum quia ostendit rotam in medio rotae. Intentio est recapitulare praedicta triplici fine, ut arctius memoriae commendetur, ut utilitas praedictorum inculcetur. Quod enim utile est, saepe dicere, nec pigrum esse debet, ut ait Apostolus (Phil. III), nec molestum audire, et ut duritia Judaeorum condemnetur, qui toties dicta non intellexerunt. Modus agendi: Praemittit quasi prologum, quo ostendit quis scripserit, et quid, etc. Post memorat grave delictum pro quo perierunt in deserto, ut hoc timore excitet ad audiendum. Post separat 447Dcivitates refugii. Post recapitulat dicta et facta bona et mala supplet. Post jubet hunc librum poni in arca, et septimo anno legi omni Israeli in scenophegia. Post subdit canticum: Audite, etc., in memoriam murmuris eorum et poenae, obedientiae et praemii. Et post benedictiones tribuum. Ultimo de morte Moysi.

PREFACE.

445D

A bundle of myrrh, beloved to me. [Vulg. Cant. 1:12 dilectus meus mihi, "my beloved is to me"] Myrrh, an aromatic tree, grows up in height only five cubits, 446whose bark and root and leaves and all parts are bitter. Myrrh is bitter, and is laid upon corpses that it may keep off the rottenness of worms. This is the old law comprised in 447Afive books: bitter, that is, sparing no one, whence also it was signified by Marath; laid upon corpses, that is, devised for sinners, that it might keep off from them the corruption of crimes. The bundle of this law is Deuteronomy, that is, a gathering of many little branches into one. For the law having been completed in four volumes, this fifth was added, gathering briefly into one the things said at large, and supplying also certain things passed over. And it was added to them that it might satisfy the number of the law. For five is the number of the law, four of the Gospel. Whence also the feeding of the five thousand men in the Gospel looks to the fathers of the Old Testament, the feeding of the four thousand to the fathers of the New; for to those were promised and given earthly things, which lie under the five senses; to those, the four-square, 447Bthat is, the solidity of the land of the living. This book serves the Gospel also in its name, in the order of its exposition, in its own dignity. For in Hebrew it is called Elleh Haddebarim, that is, "these words"; in Greek δευτερονόμιον, which in Latin is "the second law": not, as some have said, another after the one aforesaid, whence in the aforesaid books it was said: The Lord spoke; but here, Moses spoke; but second, that is, repeated a second time, prefiguring by this name the Gospel, which succeeded the law, and to which nothing shall succeed. Since also it gathers many things briefly and supplies certain things, it prefigures the form of the Gospel: for this is the abridged word which the Lord made upon the earth, and it supplies the imperfect things of the law; whence: I came not to destroy the law, but to fulfil (Matth. V). And when 447Cthis work is said to be so worthy that it is commanded to be borne in the hands of the king, it discloses the dignity of the Gospel, which was ordained in the hand of the Mediator. Since therefore it thus serves the Gospel, deservedly is it a bundle beloved by Christ, not so much for its own sake as because it shows the wheel in the midst of the wheel. The intention is to recapitulate the aforesaid with a threefold end: that it may be the more closely committed to memory; that the usefulness of the aforesaid may be impressed. For to say often what is useful ought neither to be irksome, as the Apostle says (Phil. III), nor troublesome to hear; and that the hardness of the Jews may be condemned, who so many times did not understand the things said. The manner of proceeding: he sets before it what is as it were a prologue, in which he shows who wrote, and what, etc. Afterward he calls to mind the grave offence for which they perished in the desert, that by this fear he may rouse them to hearing. Afterward he sets apart 447Dthe cities of refuge. Afterward he recapitulates things said and done, and supplies things good and evil. Afterward he commands this book to be placed in the ark, and in the seventh year to be read to all Israel at the feast of tabernacles. Afterward he subjoins the canticle: Hear ye, etc., in memory of their murmuring and of its penalty, of obedience and of its reward. And after that, the blessings of the tribes. Last, of the death of Moses.

CAPUT PRIMUM.

VERS. 1.-- Haec sunt verba, etc. Praemittit legislator titulum quasi prologum, in quo ostendit nomen auctoris, et quid fecerit, et ubi, et quando. Sicut paulo post in glossa distinguetur, et terminatur ut quibusdam videtur, ibi: Quadragesimo, etc., vel 448Ainferius, ibi: Deus noster, etc. Sed ad eorum evidentiam quae in prologo, et eorum quae sequuntur, tangenda est aliquantulum veritas historiae. Ab exitu filiorum Israel de Aegypto usque quo pervenerunt ad campestria Moab supra Jordanem: fluxerunt quadraginta duo anni, in quibus diversis temporibus quadraginta duas mansiones fecerunt. Quando nubes levabatur, castra movebantur, et cum stabat, figebantur et manebant in eodem loco quousque iterum levabatur nubes. Prima mansio fuit in Ramesse, ultima in campestribus Moab. A Ramesse usque ad montem Sina, spatio quadraginta septem dierum duodecim mansiones fecerunt et hoc primo anno, et ibi fere totum annum steterunt. Ibi lex data est, ibi Genesis scriptus, Exodus, Numeri scripti sunt, et 448Bmagna pars hujus. Inde pedem moventes anno secundo pervenerunt in Cadesbarne et in spatio illo fecerunt viginti mansiones; de Cadesbarne miserunt exploratores, qui revertentes nuntiaverunt terram bonam, sed robustos et gigantes homines invenerunt, et territi volentes redire in terram Aegypti, unde iratus Dominus. Abierunt retro et lustraverunt montem Seir per triginta octo annos, ibi mortui et prostrati sunt murmurantes. In quadragesimo anno pervenerunt ad Cadesbarne, unde retro abierunt per desertum, et in illo spatio novem mansiones iterum fecerunt. Undecim vero dies Moyses antequam veniret ad Cadesbarne, legem solo verbo recapitulavit, et de Cadesbarne usque ad campestria Moab, et illo spatio undecim dierum scripto voluit 448Ccommendare. Ecce de primo anno duodecim, de secundo una et viginti, de reliquis novem, et ita quadraginta duae mansiones.

Haec fuit causa hujus libri, ut scilicet quae facta fuerant, breviter collecta, arctius tenerentur in memoria, facile enim retinentur quae breviter dicuntur.

(RAB. in Deut. ) Haec sunt verba, etc. Principium Deuteronomii titulus esse videtur totius operis, quia personam indicat auctoris, et quid fecerit, et locum, et tempus. Nomen quoque libri gratiam demonstrat Novi Testamenti. Deuteronomium namque secunda lex interpretatur, et Evangelium significat, quod Veteris Testamenti sacramenta spiritualiter explanat. 448DSicut liber Deuteronomii repetitio est quatuor librorum legis: illi enim proprias continent in se causas, iste replicat universa, habet et ipse propria sacramenta.

(ID.) Haec sunt verba. Hoc de se tanquam de alio, etc., usque ad ut nemo possit dubitare Moysen ista scripsisse.

Locutus est. Moyses lex vetus est, quae trans Jordanem, id est ante baptismum, Israeliticum docuit populum. In Jordane enim significatur baptismus in quo baptizatus est Christus: in solitudine campestri, id est in sterilitate carnalis populi, qui fructum virtutum non attulit.

In solitudine, sterilitate plebis, cui dicitur: Relinquetur 449Avobis domus vestra deserta (Matth. XXXI; Luc. XIII).

Contra mare. Quia generatio prava et perversa ablutionem peccatorum in sanguine Christi credere noluit.

Inter Pharan. Pharan auctus; Thophel, insulsitas; Laban, dealbatio; Haseroth, atria. Auctus est enim populus numero hominum, doctrina legis et prophetarum; sed insulsus fuit, quia sal sapientiae non habuit, dealbatus vero legis caeremoniis variisque munditiis, quas ex littera legis se habere credidit. Qui bene per atria significatur, tanquam per divinas Scripturas ad introitum fidei paratus, unde multi in adventu Christi facilius susceperunt, quem ante praedictum agnoverunt, unde Philippus: Quem scripsit 449BMoyses in lege, et prophetae invenimus Jesum filium Joseph a Nazareth (Joan. I). Quatuor hi montes, quatuor Evangelia significant, quibus Ecclesia circumdatur.

VERS. 2.-- Undecim diebus. Lex per Moysen undecim diebus explanata, Judaicae plebis transgressionem in mandatis Decalogi significat:

Undecim dies sunt undecim apostoli per quos (Juda reprobato) Evangelium Christi praedicatum est in mundo.

De Horeb. Qui Hebraice Choreb ( ); incipiens per Seir, id est pilosum et sordidum vitiis populum transiens venit ad Cadesbarne: qui commutatus vel electus vel nobilis interpretatur, significans litteralem legis sensum in spiritalem per Evangelium commutatum. 449COmnia enim in figura contingebant illis, scripta ad correctionem nostram, in quos fines saeculorum devenerunt (I Cor. X). Lex enim non est soluta, sed adimpleta, nec unum iota aut unus apex praeteribit donec omnia fiant (Matth. V).

VERS. 3.-- Quadragesimo anno, undecimo mense, prima, etc. (ISID., in Deut., tom. V.) Quadraginta anni, etc., usque ad sine corruptione manebunt.

Quadragenarius ex denario et quaternario constat, et legis Evangelii impletionem significat, quae fit in Ecclesia, decem namque ad legem pertinent, quatuor vero ad Evangelium.

Undecimo mense, etc. Sicut in undecima expressa est legis transgressio, sic in prima die pervigil ad 449Ddocendum legislatoris intentio; qui ex quo inobediens populus legem transgredi non timuit, statim, praedicando et corrigendo, voluntatem Domini insinuare studuit, et eos ad meliora trahere vel provocare verbis et exemplis non cessavit.

VERS. 4.-- Postquam percussit Sehon, etc. Notandum quod, interfectis duobus Amorrhaeorum regibus, lex recapitulatur et populus instruitur, quia, remotis scandalis et superatis vitiorum turmis, competenter doctrinae insistitur et fidei lumen digne desiderantibus aperitur.

Debemus prius in nobis vitia occidere et sic mandata divina populis tradere.

Sehon regem Amorrhaeorum qui habitavit in Hesebon, 450Aetc. (ISID.) In his regibus, etc., usque ad sed foris manentes semper operibus insistant pravis.

VERS. 5.-- In terra Moab. Exlex siquidem populus fuit, qui legem spiritalem non habuit.

VERS. 6.-- Dominus Deus noster. In libro Numerorum de profectione filiorum Israel de deserto Sinai narratur, sed praeceptum Domini ad Moysen de eodem transitu non dicitur; sed de nube quae tabernaculum tegebat, et quando proficiscendum erat recedebat: et ad locum figendorum castrorum praecedebat. Sic enim scriptum est: Anno secundo, mense secundo, vigesima die mensis, elevata est nubes de tabernaculo foederis, et recubuit in solitudine Pharan (Num. X). Sed manifestum est transitum illum Dei praecepto esse dispositum, licet ibi non sit commemoratum. 450BUnde alibi in libro Numeri ad imperium Domini figebant tentoria, et ad imperium illius deponebant (Num. IX).

Sufficit vobis, etc. Praecipitur Moysi ut recedat a monte in quo legem acceperat, quia doctor non semper debet instare contemplationi, sed aliquando descendat ut sui et proximorum curam gerat, unde, Sive mente excedimus Deo, sive sobrii sumus vobis.

VERS. 9.-- Dixique vobis in illo tempore, etc. De hac divisione potestatum in Exodo antequam venirent ad montem Sinai, scriptum est quod Jethro sacerdos Madian cognatus Moysi hoc ipsi suggesserit (Exod. XVIII). Unde merito quaeritur quomodo haec sibi modo, et illo tempore conveniant? Sed quod hic dicitur dixisse Moyses in illo tempore: Non possum 450Csolus sustinere vos, etc., non illud tempus significat, quo de Horeb proficiscebantur, sed totum tempus quo profecti de Aegypto in solitudine morabantur, et Moyses non ex suo cognati sui consilio hoc populo suo persuasit.

Illo tempore. Omnia faciunt sancti in tempore opportuno, sic Deus quoque ubi venit plenitudo temporis, misit Filium suum (Gal. IV).

VERS. 11.-- Dominus Deus patrum vestrorum addat, etc. Docet doctores de profectu subditorum gaudere, gratias agere et semper meliora optare.

Ad hunc numerum, etc. Diffinitum apud Deum. Novit qui sunt ejus qui numerat multitudinem stellarum (II Tim. II).

(RAB.) Vide quam observandum sit quod alibi Scriptura 450Ddicit: Fili, ne in multis sint actus tui (Eccli. XI). Verba quoque Jethro consilium dantis considera, ait enim: Audi me, et consilium dabo tibi, et erit Deus tecum (Exod. XVIII). Videtur significari nimis intentum animum actionibus humanis, Deo quodammodo vacuari, quo tanto fit plenior, quanto in superna et aeterna liberius extenditur.

VERS. 13.-- Date ex vobis. Date vos, non ego; quasi, nolo vobis imperare, sed obedire.

Ponam eos. Nota, subditis electionem tribuit, positionem sibi retinuit, ne plebs contemnat aut oderit, si non licuit habere quem voluit.

Tribunos. Millenarios, Graece χιλιάρχους, qui de spe scilicet aeterni praemii docent subjectos.

451AEt centuriones. Centuriones, Graece ἐκατοντάρχους : qui coelestia mandata perfectis tradunt. Jure praeponuntur, qui centenario numero, id est, perfectione praediti: quasi centum regunt, dum ad perfectionem perducunt.

Et quinquagenarios. Graece πεντεκοσιάρχους : qui opera poenitentiae annuntiant.

Decanos, δεκάρχους : qui denarii perfectione vineae denarium exspectantes, ejusdem denarii multos praeparant participes.

VERS. 17.-- Quia Dei judicium est; non hominum, quia judicando Dei vicarii estis, qui personam non accipit.

Quod si difficile. Majora quaeque et difficilia discernere et judicare debent majores, parva et facilia 451Bminores.

VERS. 19.-- Cumque venissemus in Cadesbarne, etc. Quod secundo anno egressionis suae populus in deserto Pharan, quod est Cades, venerit; ubi reversis exploratoribus cum fructibus terrae, et quae viderant nuntiantibus, murmuraverunt contra Moysen et Aaron, et ideo longi itineris taedio et mortalitate affecti interierint: hic locus manifestum dabit indicium. Ubi notandum quod quadraginta duae mansiones, quas ab Aegypto usque ad Jordanem habuerunt, trium tantum congruere videntur curriculis annorum: primi scilicet, secundi, et quadragesimi egressionis de Aegypto. Primus certa distinctione continet duodecim, primam Ramesse, quintodecimo die primi mensis ingressam, ultimam solitudinem Sinai, 451Cprima die tertii mensis additam; et per undecim continuos menses construendi tabernaculi, et docendae legis gratia, minime relictam. Quarum duodecim mansionum novem tantum in Exodo exprimuntur nominatim, tres in vocabulo deserti Sin (quod dicitur esse inter Elim et Sinai) indiscrete significantur. Secundus annus complectitur mansiones viginti et unam. Quibus in ordine historiae cunctis indifferenter sub nomine solitudinis Pharan comprehensis, prima tantum et secunda, et ultima, id est, Sepulcra concupiscentiae, Haseroth, et Cades, distinguuntur nomine. Sed in catalogo mansionum pariter omnes quot in numero fiunt, vel quo nomine dictae ostenditur. Prima mansio, id est, Sepulcra concupiscentiae, secundo mense ejusdem secundi anni 451Dsecunda vicesima die mensis introita est. Anno enim secundo (ut Scriptura dicit) mense secundo vicesima die mensis moverunt castra de deserto Sinai, et recubuit in solitudine Pharan nubes, profectique sunt de monte Domini viam trium dierum, donec venirent ad locum mansionis, quae merito populi carnes concupiscentis sepulcrorum concupiscentiae nomen accepit. Ultimam harum, id est, Cades, quoto die vel mense ejusdem anni ingressi sunt, non dicitur: quae tamen in solitudine Pharan sita est, quia eodem anno adita fuerit, non tacetur. Scriptum est enim: Populus non est motus de illo loco donec revocata est Maria. Profectusque est de Haseroth fixis tentoriis in deserto Pharan, ubi locutus est Dominus ad Moysen 452Adicens: Mitte viros gui considerent terram Chanaan (Num. XII, XIII). Quod ne proxima post Haseroth mansione jussum factumque putetur, sed in ultima eorum, quae sub nomine Pharan continetur, impletum, infra scriptum est: Reversique exploratores terrae post quadraginta dies omni regione circumita, venerunt ad Moysen in Pharan, quod est Cades. In Deuteronomio quoque dicit Moyses populo: Cum venissetis in Cadesbarne, dixi vobis: Venistis ad montem Amorrhaei, etc., et dixistis: Mitte viros qui considerent terram, etc. (Deut. I). Quod autem eamdem mansionem secundo anno egressionis adierunt, a quo tamen peccato murmurationis reverti, et diu per desertum errare, et passim cadere meruerunt, testatur Moyses in sequentibus, dicens: Sedistis in Cadesbarne multo tempore, profectique inde venimus 452Bin solitudinem quae ducit ad mare Rubrum, sicut dixerat mihi Dominus, et circumivimus montem Seir longo tempore, et intra tempus quo ambulavimus de Cadesbarne usque ad torrentem Zareth octavo tricesimo anno fuit, donec consumeretur omnis generatio bellatorum. Zareth autem non est nomen alicujus mansionis de quadraginta duabus, sed torrentis ad quem (sicut in libro Numerorum legitur) transgressa octava decima mansione nomine Jeabarim venerunt, quem relinquentes, inquit, castrametati sunt contra Arnon, quae est in deserto, et prominet in finibus Amorrhaei, quod quadragesimo anno gestum fuisse non latet, qui ultimus per desertum longissimae viae mansiones continet decem: quarum prima magno labore 452Crepetita, eadem scilicet ipsa Cades deserti Sin, quam ante annos octo et triginta culpa exigente post se reversi reliquerunt, de qua scriptum est: Venerunt filii Israel in desertum Sin mense primo, et mansit populus in Cades, et mortua est ibi Maria et sepulta in eodem loco. Cumque indigeret aqua populus, coierunt adversum Moysen et Aaron, etc., usque ad hoc, Haec est aqua contradictionis, etc. (Num. XX). Notandum quod eadem Cades, et in deserto Pharan, et in deserto Sin facta est, sicut Scriptura refert, unde conjicimus partem deserti Pharan ubi Cades est, specialiter Sin appellari: sin autem non est ipsa Cades, quam mox transgresso mari Rubro inter Elim et Sinai pertransierunt, sed alia prorsus, et aliis apud Hebraeos scripta litteris. Secunda vero ejusdem 452Dquadragesimi anni mansio est mons Hor, in quo occubuit Aaron primo die quinti mensis: ultima, campestria Moab super Jordanem contra Jericho, ubi Deuteronomium meditantes, manserunt, donec mortuo Moyse, Josue duce, primo die decimi mensis Jordanem transierunt. Fiunt ergo mansiones primi anni duodecim, secundi viginti et una, ultimi et ipsa vicesima prima quae est Cades, et aliae novem, simul scilicet omnes quadraginta duae.

VERS. 22.-- Et accessistis ad me. In libro Numeri scriptum est, Dominum dixisse Moysi, ut mitteret viros, qui considerarent terram: hic vero populus dixisse legitur: Mittamus viros qui considerent terram, sed populus hoc prius Moysi suggessit, Moyses 453Aad Dominum retulit, secundum cujus praeceptum postea exploratores misit.

VERS. 23.-- Misi ex vobis. (ISID.) Duodecim exploratores missi ad explorandam uberem terram, etc., usque ad quia nec Christus sine lege, nec lex sine Christo.

Duodecim. Hi doctores Ecclesiae significant, quorum alii prava docendo populum pervertunt: alii recta praedicando proficiunt.

VERS. 33.-- Nocte ostendens. Nox peccatoris, dies vita justi. In columna ignis malorum damnatio, quibus terribilis apparebit Dominus in judicio: in columna nubis levis et blanda visio, qua consolabuntur electi.

Die per columnam. In aestu hujus saeculi obumbrans, 453Bet sustentans fortitudine, et propitiatione suae carnis.

VERS. 34.-- Juravit. Non est Deus passibilis, nec vitio iracundiae subjectus. Sed per haec charitas Moysi erga populum manifestatur, et Dei bonitas, quae omnem sensum superat.

VERS. 35.-- Non videbit quispiam. Simile dicitur de illis, qui invitati ad coenam excusaverunt se: Nemo virorum illorum qui vocati sunt gustabit coenam meam (Luc. XIV).

Pessima generatio non possidet terram bonam, quia Christi non credit incarnationem.

VERS. 37.-- Nec miranda. Hic dicit Moyses, propter populum, iratum sibi Dominum dixisse, quod in terram promissionis non ingrederetur, cum in 453Clibro Numeri legatur: Dixit Dominus ad Moysen et Aaron: Quia non credidistis mihi, ut sanctificaretis me coram filiis Israel, non introducetis hos populos in terram quam dabo eis. Sed sententia sequens videtur solvere quaestionem: Haec est, inquit, aqua contradictionis, ubi jurgati sunt filii Israel contra Dominum, etc. (Num. XX). Jurgium enim et rebellio populi causa fuit irae et vindictae, tam in populum quam in Moysen et Aaron.

Nec tu ingrederis. (ISID.) Videtur in Maria, etc., usque ad cum tanquam montis eminentia Christus excelsus agnoscitur.

VERS. 41.-- Cumque instructi. Haec sententia humanam percutit superbiam, quae de suis viribus praesumit, nec in Dei confidit potentia. Amorrhaei 453Denim, id est, amaricantes, hostes sunt spiritales, qui habitant in montibus superbiae, et praesumptuosos necant, de Seir usque Horma, id est a squalore vitiorum usque ad damnationem poenarum: Seir namque pilosus, Horma interpretatur anathema. Quisquis enim vitiorum sordibus non metuit pollui, hunc necesse est, pro meritis, ignibus gehennae tradi. Daemones namque volunt nos implicare cupiditatibus terrenis, et si perseveramus in peccatis, et in montem superbiae ascendere non desistimus, ignitis jaculis concidunt et perducunt ad anathema perpetuum. Ideo non secundum Pelagianistas de nostra praesumamus potentia, sed infirmitatem nostram considerantes, Domino per omnia obsequamur, ut 454Asuperatis hostibus, coelestis patriae gaudia promissa consequamur.

Cavendum est ne murmurantes contra Dominum ascendamus in montem superbiae, putantes nos propriis viribus salvari posse, ne continuo tradamur Amorrhaeis, id est daemonibus.

CHAPTER ONE.

VERS. 1.-- These are the words, etc. The lawgiver sets before it a title as it were a prologue, in which he shows the name of the author, and what he did, and where, and when. As will be distinguished a little later in the gloss; and it ends, as it seems to some, there: In the fortieth, etc., or 448Alower down, there: Our God, etc. But for the making plain of the things which are in the prologue, and of those which follow, the truth of the history must be touched upon a little. From the going out of the children of Israel from Egypt until they came to the plains of Moab above the Jordan there flowed forty-two years, in which at divers times they made forty-two stations. When the cloud was lifted up, the camp was moved; and when it stood still, they pitched and remained in the same place until the cloud was lifted up again. The first station was in Ramesse, the last in the plains of Moab. From Ramesse as far as Mount Sina, in the space of forty-seven days, they made twelve stations, and this in the first year; and there they stayed almost the whole year. There the law was given; there Genesis was written, Exodus and Numbers were written, and 448Ba great part of this book. Moving their foot thence, in the second year they came into Cadesbarne, and in that space they made twenty stations; from Cadesbarne they sent scouts, who on returning reported the land good, but they found strong men and giants, and being terrified they wished to return into the land of Egypt, whereat the Lord was angered. They went back and ranged about Mount Seir for thirty-eight years; there the murmurers died and were laid low. In the fortieth year they came to Cadesbarne, whence they had gone back through the desert, and in that space they again made nine stations. But for eleven days, before he came to Cadesbarne, Moses recapitulated the law by word alone; and from Cadesbarne as far as the plains of Moab, and in that space of eleven days, he willed to commit it 448Cto writing. Behold: from the first year twelve, from the second one and twenty, from the rest nine; and so forty-two stations.

This was the cause of this book: namely, that the things which had been done, being briefly gathered, might be the more closely held in memory; for the things that are briefly said are easily retained.

(RAB. in Deut. ) These are the words, etc. The beginning of Deuteronomy seems to be the title of the whole work, because it indicates the person of the author, and what he did, and the place, and the time. The name of the book also shows forth the grace of the New Testament. For Deuteronomy is interpreted "the second law," and signifies the Gospel, which expounds spiritually the mysteries of the Old Testament. 448DJust as the book of Deuteronomy is a repetition of the four books of the law: for those contain in themselves their own matters, this one repeats them all, and itself also has its own mysteries.

(ID.) These are the words. This of himself as of another, etc., as far as that no one can doubt that Moses wrote these things.

He spoke. Moses is the old law, which beyond the Jordan, that is, before baptism, taught the Israelite people. For by the Jordan is signified baptism, in which Christ was baptized: in the wilderness of the plain, that is, in the barrenness of the carnal people, which brought forth no fruit of virtues.

In the wilderness, the barrenness of the people, to whom it is said: Your house shall be left 449Ato you desolate (Matth. XXXI; Luc. XIII).

Over against the sea. Because the depraved and perverse generation would not believe in the washing away of sins in the blood of Christ.

Between Pharan. Pharan, increased; Thophel, insipidity; Laban, whitening; Haseroth, courts. For the people was increased in the number of men, in the doctrine of the law and of the prophets; but it was insipid, because it had not the salt of wisdom; whitened indeed by the ceremonies of the law and by divers cleannesses, which it believed itself to have from the letter of the law. And it is well signified by the courts, as being made ready through the divine Scriptures for the entrance of faith; whence many at the coming of Christ received him the more easily, whom they had known as foretold beforehand; whence Philip: Whom 449BMoses wrote of in the law, and the prophets, we have found: Jesus the son of Joseph of Nazareth (Joan. I). These four mountains signify the four Gospels, with which the Church is encompassed.

VERS. 2.-- Eleven days. The law expounded by Moses in eleven days signifies the transgression of the Jewish people in the commandments of the Decalogue:

The eleven days are the eleven apostles, through whom (Judas being reprobate) the Gospel of Christ was preached in the world.

From Horeb. Which in Hebrew is Choreb ( ) [our source prints an empty parenthesis here, and the word Migne sets in it has been lost in the digitization. Three Hebrew words were lost in the same way in the Glossa on the Song of Songs (8944), the companion volume in this same recension, and recovered only from the plate; recovery here would require the same.]; beginning through Seir, that is, passing through the people hairy and foul with vices, he came to Cadesbarne, which is interpreted changed, or chosen, or noble, signifying the literal sense of the law changed into the spiritual through the Gospel. 449CFor all these things happened to them in figure, and were written for our correction, upon whom the ends of the world are come (I Cor. X). For the law is not loosed, but fulfilled, nor shall one iota or one tittle pass away till all be done (Matth. V).

VERS. 3.-- In the fortieth year, in the eleventh month, on the first day, etc. (ISID., in Deut., tom. V.) Forty years, etc., as far as they shall remain without corruption.

The number forty consists of ten and four, and signifies the fulfilment of the law, of the Gospel, which comes to pass in the Church; for ten belong to the law, and four to the Gospel.

In the eleventh month, etc. As in the eleventh is expressed the transgression of the law, so in the first day the lawgiver's intention, ever watchful to 449Dteach; who, from the time when the disobedient people feared not to transgress the law, straightway, by preaching and correcting, was zealous to instil the will of the Lord, and ceased not to draw or to provoke them to better things by words and by examples.

VERS. 4.-- After he had slain Sehon, etc. It is to be noted that, the two kings of the Amorrhites being slain, the law is recapitulated and the people instructed; because, scandals being removed and the troops of the vices overcome, one fittingly applies oneself to doctrine, and the light of faith is opened to them that worthily desire it.

We ought first to slay the vices in ourselves, and so to deliver the divine commandments to the peoples.

Sehon king of the Amorrhites who dwelt in Hesebon, 450Aetc. (ISID.) In these kings, etc., as far as but, remaining without, let them ever apply themselves to depraved works.

VERS. 5.-- In the land of Moab. For that people was lawless, which had not the spiritual law.

VERS. 6.-- The Lord our God. In the book of Numbers the setting out of the children of Israel from the desert of Sinai is narrated, but the command of the Lord to Moses concerning that same passage is not spoken of; but rather of the cloud which covered the tabernacle, and which withdrew when they were to set out, and went before them to the place where the camp was to be pitched. For thus it is written: In the second year, in the second month, on the twentieth day of the month, the cloud was lifted up from the tabernacle of the covenant, and rested in the wilderness of Pharan (Num. X). But it is manifest that that passage was ordered by the command of God, although it is not there recorded. 450BWhence elsewhere in the book of Numbers, at the command of the Lord they pitched their tents, and at his command they took them down (Num. IX).

It is enough for you, etc. Moses is commanded to withdraw from the mountain on which he had received the law, because a teacher ought not always to be intent upon contemplation, but should sometimes come down, that he may take care of himself and of his neighbours; whence, Whether we be transported in mind, it is to God; or whether we be sober, it is for you.

VERS. 9.-- And I said to you at that time, etc. Concerning this division of powers it is written in Exodus, before they came to Mount Sinai, that Jethro the priest of Madian, the kinsman of Moses, suggested this to him (Exod. XVIII). Whence it is rightly asked: how do these things agree together, both now and at that time? But that which Moses is here said to have said at that time, I am not able 450Calone to bear you, etc., does not signify that time in which they were setting out from Horeb, but the whole time in which, having gone out from Egypt, they were tarrying in the wilderness; and Moses urged this upon his people not from his own, from his kinsman's counsel.

At that time. The saints do all things in the fitting season; so God also, when the fulness of time came, sent his Son (Gal. IV).

VERS. 11.-- The Lord God of your fathers add, etc. He teaches teachers to rejoice at the progress of their subjects, to give thanks, and always to wish better things.

To this number, etc. Determined with God. He knoweth who are his, who numbereth the multitude of the stars (II Tim. II).

(RAB.) See how carefully that is to be observed which elsewhere Scripture 450Dsays: Son, let not thy acts be in many things (Eccli. XI). Consider also the words of Jethro giving counsel, for he says: Hear me, and I will give thee counsel, and God will be with thee (Exod. XVIII). It seems to be signified that a mind too intent upon human actions is in some manner emptied of God, of whom it becomes the fuller the more freely it is stretched out toward things above and eternal.

VERS. 13.-- Give from among you. Give ye, not I: as if, I will not command you, but obey.

I will appoint them. Note: he granted the choice to his subjects, the appointing he kept for himself, lest the people should despise or hate, if it were not permitted them to have whom they wished.

Tribunes. Commanders of a thousand, in Greek χιλιάρχους, who teach their subjects, namely, of the hope of the eternal reward.

451AAnd centurions. Centurions, in Greek ἐκατοντάρχους: who deliver the heavenly commandments to the perfect. Rightly are they set over others, who are endowed with the number of a hundred, that is, with perfection: they rule as it were a hundred, while they lead men through to perfection.

And officers of fifty. In Greek πεντεκοσιάρχους: who announce the works of penance.

Deans, δεκάρχους: who, awaiting through the perfection of the denary the denary of the vineyard, make ready many to be partakers of that same denary.

VERS. 17.-- Because it is the judgment of God; not of men, because in judging you are the vicars of God, who accepteth not the person.

But if anything be difficult. All greater and difficult matters the greater ought to discern and to judge; small and easy ones 451Bthe lesser.

VERS. 19.-- And when we had come to Cadesbarne, etc. That in the second year of their going out the people came into the desert of Pharan, which is Cades, where, the scouts having returned with the fruits of the land and reporting what they had seen, they murmured against Moses and Aaron, and therefore, afflicted with the weariness of a long journey and with mortality, perished: this passage will give a manifest indication. Where it is to be noted that the forty-two stations which they had from Egypt as far as the Jordan seem to fit the courses of three years only: namely, the first, the second, and the fortieth from the going out of Egypt. The first, by a sure division, contains twelve: the first Ramesse, entered on the fifteenth day of the first month; the last the wilderness of Sinai, 451Cadded on the first day of the third month, and by no means left for eleven continuous months, for the sake of building the tabernacle and of teaching the law. Of which twelve stations only nine are expressed by name in Exodus; three are signified without distinction under the name of the desert of Sin (which is said to be between Elim and Sinai). The second year comprises twenty-one stations. All of which, in the order of the history, being included indifferently under the name of the wilderness of Pharan, only the first and the second and the last, that is, the Graves of Lust, Haseroth, and Cades, are distinguished by name. But in the catalogue of the stations it is shown alike of them all how many they are in number, and by what name they are called. The first station, that is, the Graves of Lust, was entered in the second month of that same second year, 451Don the two and twentieth day of the month. For in the second year (as the Scripture says), in the second month, on the twentieth day of the month, they moved the camp from the desert of Sinai, and the cloud rested in the wilderness of Pharan; and they set forward from the mount of the Lord a journey of three days, until they came to the place of the station which, by the desert of a people lusting after flesh, received the name of the Graves of Lust. On what day or in what month of that same year they entered the last of these, that is, Cades, is not said; yet that it is situated in the wilderness of Pharan, and that it was reached in that same year, is not passed over in silence. For it is written: The people was not moved from that place until Mary was called back. And they set forward from Haseroth and pitched their tents in the desert of Pharan, where the Lord spoke to Moses 452Asaying: Send men gui who may view the land of Chanaan (Num. XII, XIII). And lest this be thought to have been commanded and done at the station next after Haseroth, but rather to have been fulfilled at the last of those which are contained under the name of Pharan, it is written below: And the scouts of the land, returning after forty days, having gone round the whole region, came to Moses in Pharan, which is Cades. In Deuteronomy also Moses says to the people: When you had come to Cadesbarne, I said to you: You are come to the mountain of the Amorrhite, etc., and you said: Send men who may view the land, etc. (Deut. I). And that they came to that same station in the second year of the going out, from which nevertheless, by the sin of murmuring, they deserved to turn back, and long to wander through the desert, and to fall on every side, Moses testifies in what follows, saying: You sat in Cadesbarne a long time, and setting forward thence we came 452Binto the wilderness which leads to the Red Sea, as the Lord had said to me; and we went round Mount Seir a long time, and within the time in which we walked from Cadesbarne as far as the torrent Zareth was in the eight and thirtieth year, until the whole generation of the men of war was consumed. But Zareth is not the name of any station of the forty-two, but of a torrent to which (as is read in the book of Numbers) they came when the eighteenth station, by name Jeabarim, had been passed; leaving which, he says, they encamped over against Arnon, which is in the desert and juts out in the borders of the Amorrhite; and it is not hidden that this was done in the fortieth year, which last year contains ten stations of a very long way through the desert: of which the first was regained with great labour, 452Cnamely that very same Cades of the desert of Sin, which eight and thirty years before, their fault requiring it, they left behind them when they had turned back; of which it is written: The children of Israel came into the desert of Sin in the first month, and the people abode in Cades; and Mary died there and was buried in the same place. And when the people wanted water, they came together against Moses and Aaron, etc., as far as this, This is the water of contradiction, etc. (Num. XX). It is to be noted that the same Cades came to be both in the desert of Pharan and in the desert of Sin, as the Scripture relates; whence we conjecture that the part of the desert of Pharan where Cades is, is specially called Sin: but Sin is not that same Cades which, soon after the Red Sea was crossed, they passed through between Elim and Sinai, but altogether another, and written with other letters among the Hebrews. But the second station of that same 452Dfortieth year is Mount Hor, in which Aaron died on the first day of the fifth month; the last, the plains of Moab above the Jordan over against Jericho, where, meditating upon Deuteronomy, they remained until, Moses being dead, under Josue their leader, on the first day of the tenth month they crossed the Jordan. There are therefore twelve stations of the first year, twenty-one of the second, and of the last that same twenty-first which is Cades, and nine others; namely, all together forty-two.

VERS. 22.-- And you came near to me. In the book of Numbers it is written that the Lord said to Moses that he should send men who might view the land; but here the people is read to have said: Let us send men who may view the land; but the people first suggested this to Moses, Moses 453Areferred it to the Lord, according to whose command he afterward sent the scouts.

VERS. 23.-- I sent from among you. (ISID.) Twelve scouts sent to explore the rich land, etc., as far as because neither Christ without the law, nor the law without Christ.

Twelve. These signify the teachers of the Church, of whom some, by teaching depraved things, pervert the people; others, by preaching right things, make progress.

VERS. 33.-- Showing by night. Night is the sinner's, day the life of the just. In the pillar of fire, the damnation of the wicked, to whom the Lord will appear terrible in the judgment; in the pillar of cloud, the light and gentle vision wherewith the elect shall be consoled.

By day through the pillar. Overshadowing in the heat of this world, 453Band sustaining by the strength and by the propitiation of his flesh.

VERS. 34.-- He swore. God is not passible, nor subject to the vice of anger. But by these things the charity of Moses toward the people is made manifest, and the goodness of God, which surpasses all understanding.

VERS. 35.-- Not one shall see. The like is said of those who, being invited to the supper, excused themselves: None of those men that were invited shall taste of my supper (Luc. XIV).

The worst generation possesses not the good land, because it believes not the incarnation of Christ.

VERS. 37.-- Nor is it to be wondered at. Here Moses says that the Lord, being angered with him on account of the people, said that he should not enter into the land of promise, whereas in 453Cthe book of Numbers it is read: The Lord said to Moses and Aaron: Because you have not believed me, to sanctify me before the children of Israel, you shall not bring these peoples into the land which I will give them. But the sentence that follows seems to resolve the question: This is, he says, the water of contradiction, where the children of Israel strove against the Lord, etc. (Num. XX). For the strife and the rebellion of the people was the cause of the anger and of the vengeance, both upon the people and upon Moses and Aaron.

Neither shalt thou enter. (ISID.) It seems in Mary, etc., as far as when Christ the exalted is acknowledged as it were in the eminence of a mountain.

VERS. 41.-- And when, being armed. This sentence strikes at human pride, which presumes upon its own strength, and trusts not in the power of God. For the Amorrhites, 453Dthat is, the embittering, are spiritual enemies, who dwell in the mountains of pride and slay the presumptuous, from Seir even to Horma, that is, from the squalor of vices even to the damnation of punishments: for Seir is interpreted hairy, Horma anathema. For whosoever fears not to be defiled with the filth of vices, he must needs, according to his deserts, be delivered to the fires of Gehenna. For the demons wish to entangle us in earthly desires; and if we persevere in sins, and cease not to ascend into the mountain of pride, they cut us down with fiery darts and lead us through to perpetual anathema. Therefore let us not, after the manner of the Pelagianists, presume upon our own power; but, considering our infirmity, let us obey the Lord in all things, that, 454Aour enemies being overcome, we may attain the promised joys of the heavenly fatherland.

We must beware lest, murmuring against the Lord, we ascend into the mountain of pride, thinking that we can be saved by our own strength, lest we be straightway delivered to the Amorrhites, that is, to the demons.

CAPUT II.

VERS. 1.-- Et circuivimus montem Seir, etc. Seir est mons in terra Edom, in quo habitavit Esau in regione Gabalena, ubi habitavit antea Chorraeus quem interfecit Chodorlahomor; qui ab Esau piloso et hispido, Seir, id est pilosi, nomen accepit. Chorraei sicut caeterae gentes, quas superaverunt, qui de patriarcharum semine descenderunt, malignos spiritus 454Bvel vitia exprimunt, quae illi exsuperare possunt, qui sanctorum Patrum vestigiis et doctrinis insistunt, et spiritalem militiam sub rege Christo agunt, de qua dicitur: Non est nobis colluctatio adversus carnem et sanguinem, sed adversus principes et potestates, etc. (Ephes. VI).

VERS. 9.-- Filiis Loth tradidi. Ar metropolis est Moabitarum, super ripam torrentis Arnon: olim possessa a gente veterrima Emim, postea retenta a filiis Loth, id est Moabitis, subversis accolis. Haec ostenditur hodie in vertice montis, torrens vero per abrupta descendens, in mare Mortuum fluit. Ar interpretatur suscitavit; Emim, id est terribiles: hi sunt daemones, qui lites suscitant, et contentiones, et tela nequitiae parant contra fideles. Enacim quoque 454Cde stirpe Enac procreati, et gigantibus proceritate aequati, secundum nomen suum significant daemones, nam monile collo sublimi interpretantur. Superbiae enim fastu elati omnem militiae suae paraturam ascribunt propriae virtuti, et ideo illis facile cedunt, qui in virtute Dei confidunt, et cum Psalmista dicunt: Fortitudo mea et laus mea Dominus, et factus est mihi in salutem (Psal. CXVII).

VERS. 14.-- De Cadesbarne. (HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam.) Cabesbarne est locus in deserto, qui conjungitur civitati Petrae in Arabia: ubi occubuit Maria, et Moyses rupe percussa aquam sitienti populo dedit. Monstratur etiam usque hodie ibidem sepulcrum Mariae, sed et principes Amalec ibi caesi sunt a Chodorlahomor.

454DVERS. 26.-- Misi ergo nuntios, etc. Sehon diabolum significat, quod est germen inutile, vel arbor infructuosa, vel elatus. Israel populum Christianum, qui per viam mundi hujus cupit transire, et ad coelestia regna pervenire. Hic mittit ad Sehon verbis pacificis, promittens se non habitaturum in terra ejus, sed tantum transiturum, et regali via incessurum. Hoc quisque promisit, cum primum ad aquam baptismi pervenit, et prima fidei signacula suscepit. Abrenuntiavit enim diabolo et pompis ejus, et operibus ejus, et omni servitio, et voluptatibus ejus. Hoc est quod dicitur: Quia Israel non declinabit in agrum ejus, vel in vineam ejus, et aquam de lacu ejus non potabit (Num. XXI), id est, disciplinam vel astrologicae 455Avel magicae, vel cujuslibet doctrinae, quae contra pietatem est, amplius non sumet: sed bibet de fontibus Salvatoris, non derelinquet fontem aquae vivae, ut bibat de fonte Sehon, sed incedet via regia, quae est Christus, qui dicit: Ego sum via, veritas, et vita (Joan. XIV), et rex, secundum illud, Deus, judicium tuum regi da (Psal. LXXI). Non declinabit in agrum, neque in vineam ejus, ad opera scilicet, vel ad pravos sensus ejus. Amorrhaei loquentes interpretantur, vel amaricantes, quia Deum ad amaritudinem provocant, et in verbis tantum confidunt, ut poetae, astrologi, philosophi, qui inania loquuntur; regnum autem Dei non est in sermone, sed virtute. Volunt fideles cum pace transire, et pacifici esse etiam cum inimicis pacis: sed eo magis principem 455Bmundi offendunt, quo se cum illo nolle morari dicunt, nec aliquid ejus contingere. Inde exacerbatus magis persequitur, pericula suscitat, cruciatus intentat, congregat populum suum, exit confligere adversum Israel: populus ejus sunt principes, et judices mundi, cunctique nequitiae ministri, qui persequuntur populum Dei: sed et ipse venit in Jasa, quod interpretatur mandati impletio. Qui enim ad expletionem mandatorum Dei venerit, Sehon vel exercitum ejus saevientem non timebit. Mandata enim Dei complere, est diabolum, et exercitum ejus superare. Sic enim conterit Deus Satan sub pedibus nostris, et dat nobis potentiam calcandi super serpentes et scorpiones, et super omnem virtutem inimici.

455CVERS. 30.-- Quia induraverat. Longa patientia, non corrigendo, unde in Exodo: Ego induravi cor Pharaonis (Exod. X); et Psalmista: Convertit cor eorum, ut odirent populum ejus (Psal. CIV). Causa vero indurationis additur, ut traderetur in manus tuas, id est ut contereretur a te; quod non fieret nisi resisteret, nec resisteret nisi induratus esset. Hujus rei justitiam si quaesierimus, inscrutabilia sunt judicia ejus, sed iniquitas non est apud Deum.

VERS. 33.-- Percussimusque eum. Qui sunt veri Israelitae, non carne Judaei, sed spiritu, habitant in omnibus civitatibus Amorrhaeorum, cum in omni orbe terrarum Christi Ecclesiae propagantur, et unusquisque nostrum civitas fuit regis Sehon: quia regnabat in nobis stultitia, superbia, impietas, et omnia quae 455Ddiabolus suggerit. Sed ubi victus est fortis, et vasa ejus direpta, facti sumus civitates Israel, haereditas sanctorum, excisa prius arbore infructuosa, et dejecto elato rege, et regnante in nobis mansueto, qui dicit: Discite a me, quia mitis sum et humilis corde, etc. (Matth. XI). Nomina quoque locorum conveniunt: Aroer namque sublevans interpretatur; Arnon, acervus tristitiae; Galaad, acervus testimonii. Ab Aroer ergo, quae est super ripam torrentis Arnon, etc. Quia omnis persona cujuslibet dignitatis fastu superbiae vel pompa confidens mundana, cum persecutoribus qui variis tribulationibus Christianos angustiant; usque ad haereticorum conciliabula, qui testimoniis Scripturarum depravatis contra Ecclesiam 456Apugnant, sed resistere non valent, traduntur victi in manus sanctorum cum habitaculis suis.

CHAPTER II.

VERS. 1.-- And we went round Mount Seir, etc. Seir is a mountain in the land of Edom, in which Esau dwelt, in the region of Gabalena, where formerly dwelt the Chorrhite whom Chodorlahomor slew; and it received its name Seir, that is, of the hairy one, from Esau, who was hairy and shaggy. The Chorrhites, like the other nations which they overcame, who were descended from the seed of the patriarchs, set forth malignant spirits 454Bor vices, which they are able to overcome who follow the footsteps and the teachings of the holy Fathers, and wage the spiritual warfare under Christ the king; of which it is said: Our wrestling is not against flesh and blood, but against principalities and powers, etc. (Ephes. VI).

VERS. 9.-- I have given to the children of Loth. Ar is the metropolis of the Moabites, upon the bank of the torrent Arnon: once possessed by the most ancient nation of the Emim, afterward held by the children of Loth, that is, by the Moabites, the former inhabitants being overthrown. This is shown to this day upon the top of a mountain; but the torrent, descending through steep places, flows into the Dead Sea. Ar is interpreted he stirred up; Emim, that is, the terrible: these are the demons, who stir up quarrels and contentions, and make ready the darts of wickedness against the faithful. The Enacim also, 454Cbegotten of the stock of Enac, and matched with the giants in stature, signify according to their name the demons; for they are interpreted a necklace upon a lofty neck. For, lifted up with the arrogance of pride, they ascribe all the equipment of their warfare to their own strength, and therefore they easily give way to those who trust in the strength of God, and say with the Psalmist: The Lord is my strength and my praise, and he is become my salvation (Psal. CXVII).

VERS. 14.-- Of Cadesbarne. (HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam.) Cabesbarne is a place in the desert which adjoins the city of Petra in Arabia, where Mary died, and Moses, the rock being struck, gave water to the thirsting people. The sepulchre of Mary is also shown in that same place to this day; and there too the princes of Amalec were slain by Chodorlahomor.

454DVERS. 26.-- I sent therefore messengers, etc. Sehon signifies the devil, which is a useless shoot, or an unfruitful tree, or the lifted up. Israel, the Christian people, which desires to pass by the way of this world and to come to the heavenly kingdoms. This people sends to Sehon with peaceable words, promising that it will not dwell in his land, but only pass through, and will go by the royal way. This every man promised when first he came to the water of baptism and received the first seals of faith. For he renounced the devil and his pomps, and his works, and all his service, and his pleasures. This is what is said: That Israel will not turn aside into his field, or into his vineyard, and will not drink water out of his well (Num. XXI), that is, it will no more take up the discipline either of astrology 455Aor of magic, or of any doctrine whatsoever that is against godliness; but it will drink of the fountains of the Saviour; it will not forsake the fountain of living water to drink of the fountain of Sehon, but will go by the royal way, which is Christ, who says: I am the way, the truth, and the life (Joan. XIV), and king, according to that word, Give to the king thy judgment, O God (Psal. LXXI). It will not turn aside into his field, nor into his vineyard, that is, to his works, or to his depraved meanings. The Amorrhites are interpreted speakers, or the embittering, because they provoke God to bitterness, and trust in words only, like the poets, the astrologers, the philosophers, who speak vain things; but the kingdom of God is not in speech, but in power. The faithful wish to pass through in peace, and to be peaceable even with the enemies of peace; but so much the more do they offend the prince 455Bof the world, in that they say that they will not tarry with him, nor touch anything of his. Thereupon, being the more exasperated, he persecutes them the more, stirs up perils, threatens torments, gathers his people, goes out to fight against Israel: his people are the princes and judges of the world, and all the ministers of wickedness, who persecute the people of God; but he himself also comes into Jasa, which is interpreted the fulfilment of the commandment. For he who has come to the fulfilling of the commandments of God will not fear Sehon or his raging army. For to fulfil the commandments of God is to overcome the devil and his army. For thus God crushes Satan under our feet, and gives us the power of treading upon serpents and scorpions, and upon all the strength of the enemy.

455CVERS. 30.-- Because he had hardened. By long patience, by not correcting; whence in Exodus: I have hardened the heart of Pharao (Exod. X); and the Psalmist: He turned their heart to hate his people (Psal. CIV). But the cause of the hardening is added, that he might be delivered into thy hands, that is, that he might be crushed by thee; which would not come to pass unless he resisted, nor would he resist unless he had been hardened. If we seek the justice of this matter, his judgments are inscrutable, but there is no iniquity with God.

VERS. 33.-- And we slew him. They who are true Israelites, Jews not in the flesh but in the spirit, dwell in all the cities of the Amorrhites, since throughout the whole world the Churches of Christ are spread abroad; and every one of us was a city of king Sehon, because there reigned in us folly, pride, impiety, and all things which 455Dthe devil suggests. But when the strong man was overcome and his vessels plundered, we were made cities of Israel, the inheritance of the saints, the unfruitful tree being first cut down, and the lifted-up king cast down, and the meek one reigning in us, who says: Learn of me, because I am meek and humble of heart, etc. (Matth. XI). The names of the places also agree: for Aroer is interpreted lifting up; Arnon, a heap of sadness; Galaad, a heap of testimony. From Aroer therefore, which is upon the bank of the torrent Arnon, etc. Because every person of whatsoever dignity, trusting in the arrogance of pride or in worldly pomp, together with the persecutors who straiten Christians with divers tribulations, even to the conventicles of the heretics, who fight against the Church 456Awith the testimonies of the Scriptures depraved, but are not able to resist, are delivered up, overcome, into the hands of the saints, with their dwellings.

CAPUT III.

VERS. 1.-- Itaque conversi. Ad Og, qui est rex Basan, nec legatos dignantur mittere, nec transitum per terram ejus petere: continuo confligunt cum eo, superantes eum et populum suum. Basan interpretatur turpitudo: merito ergo nec legati mittuntur ad gentem istam, nec transitus petitur; nullus enim transitus debet nobis esse, vel ascensus ad turpitudinem, quae statim expugnanda, et omnino cavenda est. Og autem interpretatur conclusio; carnalia significat, quorum desiderio anima excluditur, et separatur a Deo. Adversus haec sic est bellandum, ut 456Bnullum relinquatur vitium. Nullum enim oportet relinqui in regno turpitudinis; debent enim turpia resecari, ut honesta et religiosa possint aedificari. In regno Sehon vel Moab non hoc praecipitur. Forte enim ex illis aliquid nobis opus est, quo multiplex praesentis vitae usus eget: alioquin deberemus exire de hoc mundo. De Basan vero, id est de turpitudine, nihil relinquimus, quia nihilo indigemus. Honestum etiam esse non potest, quod turpe est.

VERS. 4.-- Sexaginta. Superiorem sensum confirmat. Sexagenarius quippe numerus, sicut senarius, perfectionem significat. Bene autem sexaginta urbes memorat vastatas in regione Og regis Basan, id est diaboli, qui est rex turpitudinis; quia perfecte omnium vitiorum delectationem destruendam esse significat. 456CArgob , id est maledicta sublimitas, terrenam superbiam significat, quae in filiis irae regnat: destructis urbibus, viros ac mulieres ac parvulos dicit interfectos, jumenta et spolia direpta, quia, munitione diaboli superata, quidquid in carne nostra rebelle, quidquid lascivum senserimus cum parvulis cogitationibus, zelo Dei est conterendum; quidquid autem in simplicitate puri sensus, vel membrorum ornatu, servitio Dei aptum inveniemus, reservare debemus.

VERS. 12.-- Terramque. (GREG., lib. XXVII Moral., cap. 10.) Sunt multi in Ecclesia, etc., usque ad vana scilicet desideria et irrationabiles animi motus.

Ruben et Gad. (RAB.) Ruben primogenitus Jacob, etc., usque ad sed cum ipsis mulieribus et infantibus 456Dcontendunt transire et ad patriam pervenire.

VERS. 28.-- Praecipe Josue, etc. Magna dispensatione Dei factum est, ne Moses, qui est typus legis, introduceret populum in terram promissionis, quia lex neminem ad perfectum adduxit; Josue autem typus Christi, qui salvator interpretatur. (Christus enim populum suum salvavit), convenienter dux et princeps in terram promissionis introduxit

CHAPTER III.

VERS. 1.-- And so, turning. To Og, who is king of Basan, they deign neither to send legates nor to ask a passage through his land: straightway they fight with him, overcoming him and his people. Basan is interpreted baseness: rightly therefore are no legates sent to that nation, nor is a passage asked; for we ought to have no passage, nor ascent, to baseness, which must at once be stormed and altogether shunned. But Og is interpreted a closing in; it signifies carnal things, by the desire whereof the soul is shut out and separated from God. Against these we must so wage war that 456Bno vice be left. For nothing ought to be left in the kingdom of baseness; for base things must be cut away, that honourable and religious things may be built up. In the kingdom of Sehon or of Moab this is not commanded. For perhaps we have need of somewhat from them, whereof the manifold use of the present life stands in want: otherwise we should have to go out of this world. But of Basan, that is, of baseness, we leave nothing, because we have need of nothing. Nor, moreover, can that be honourable which is base.

VERS. 4.-- Sixty. It confirms the foregoing sense. For the number sixty, like the number six, signifies perfection. And well does he record sixty cities laid waste in the region of Og king of Basan, that is, of the devil, who is the king of baseness; because he signifies that the delight of all vices is to be perfectly destroyed. 456CArgob, that is, accursed loftiness, signifies earthly pride, which reigns in the children of wrath: the cities being destroyed, he says that the men and the women and the little ones were slain, the beasts and the spoils plundered; because, the fortification of the devil being overcome, whatsoever we shall have felt rebellious in our flesh, whatsoever wanton, together with the little thoughts, is to be crushed with the zeal of God; but whatsoever we shall find fit for the service of God, in the simplicity of a pure understanding or in the adornment of the members, we ought to reserve.

VERS. 12.-- And the land. (GREG., lib. XXVII Moral., cap. 10.) There are many in the Church, etc., as far as namely, vain desires and the irrational motions of the mind.

Ruben and Gad. (RAB.) Ruben the firstborn of Jacob, etc., as far as but with those same women and children 456Dthey strive to pass over and to come to the fatherland.

VERS. 28.-- Command Josue, etc. By a great dispensation of God it came to pass that Moses, who is a type of the law, should not bring the people into the land of promise, because the law brought no one to perfection; but Josue is a type of Christ, who is interpreted saviour. (For Christ saved his people), fittingly, as leader and prince, brought them into the land of promise

CAPUT IV.

VERS. 1.-- Et nunc, Israel, audi praecepta et judicia. Non addetis. Docet normam aequitatis, et omnem haeresim percutit. Vetat enim rectae doctrinae aliquid pravae intentionis addere, vel aliquid veritatis 457Asubtrahere: via enim regia incedendum est, non ad dexteram vel ad sinistram; unde: Quia rectae viae Domini, et justi ambulabunt in eis (Ose. XIV). Similiter ait Joannes: Si quis apposuerit ad haec, apponet Deus super illum plagas scriptas in libro isto; si quis diminuerit, auferet Deus partem ejus de libro vitae (Apoc. XXII).

VERS. 7.-- Nec est alia, etc. Non est alia natio tam grandis, dignitate, scilicet donorum, non multitudine populorum. Nec enim gens Judaeorum numero vincebat alias gentes aut potentia, sed patrum praerogativa et dignatione divina.

VERS. 9.-- Quae viderunt. Visus inter omnes sensus obtinet principatum: ideo pro omni sensu ponitur.

VERS. 16.-- Sculptam similitudinem. (AUG., quaest. 457B4 in Deut.) Forsitan his duobus vocabulis unam rem significavit, etc., usque ad quod de hominibus usitatum est.

VERS. 18.-- Qui sub. (AUG., quaest. 5.) An aquam terram intelligi voluit, etc., usque ad et animalia terrena habere non posset.

VERS. 19.-- Quae creavit. (AUG., quaest. 6.) Non ita dictum est, etc., usque ad sed non cultum quem habent aliae gentes.

VERS. 23.-- Sculptam similitudinem. (AUG., q. 7.) Hic generaliter loquens similitudinem posuit, imaginem tacuit, quia si sit nulla similitudo, nec imago.

VERS. 26.-- Testes invoco hodie coelum, etc. Μετονυμικῶς per id quod continet id quod continetur significat.

457CVERS. 30.-- Novissimo autem. Conversionem Israelis ad fidem Christi praenuntiat, ut postquam plenitudo gentium intraverit, omnis Israel salvus fiat.

VERS. 32.-- A summo coelo. (AUG., quaest. 8.) Cur non dixerit similiter a summo terrae, etc., usque ad cum hoc de novissima electorum congregatione dicatur.

CHAPTER IV.

VERS. 1.-- And now, O Israel, hear the precepts and the judgments. You shall not add. He teaches the rule of equity, and strikes at every heresy. For he forbids to add anything of depraved intent to right doctrine, or to take away anything 457Aof the truth: for we must go by the royal way, not to the right hand or to the left; whence: For the ways of the Lord are right, and the just shall walk in them (Ose. XIV). In like manner John says: If any man shall add to these things, God shall add unto him the plagues written in this book; if any man shall take away, God shall take away his part out of the book of life (Apoc. XXII).

VERS. 7.-- Nor is there any other, etc. There is not another nation so great, in dignity, namely, of gifts, not in the multitude of peoples. For neither did the nation of the Jews surpass other nations in number or in power, but by the prerogative of the fathers and by the divine favour.

VERS. 9.-- Which they have seen. Sight holds the chief place among all the senses: therefore it is put for every sense.

VERS. 16.-- A graven likeness. (AUG., quaest. 457B4 in Deut.) Perhaps by these two words he signified one thing, etc., as far as which is customary of men.

VERS. 18.-- That are under. (AUG., quaest. 5.) Whether he wished the water to be understood as earth, etc., as far as and could not have earthly animals.

VERS. 19.-- Which he created. (AUG., quaest. 6.) It is not so said, etc., as far as but not the worship which other nations have.

VERS. 23.-- A graven likeness. (AUG., q. 7.) Here, speaking generally, he set down likeness and kept silence about image, because if there be no likeness, neither is there an image.

VERS. 26.-- I call to witness this day heaven, etc. Μετονυμικῶς, by that which contains it signifies that which is contained.

457CVERS. 30.-- But in the last time. He foretells the conversion of Israel to the faith of Christ, that after the fulness of the Gentiles shall have entered in, all Israel may be saved.

VERS. 32.-- From the highest part of heaven. (AUG., quaest. 8.) Why he did not likewise say, from the highest part of the earth, etc., as far as since this is said of the last gathering of the elect.

CAPUT V.

VERS. 1.-- Audi, Israel, etc. (AUG., quaest. 9 in Deut.) An ideo dictum est, etc., usque ad et videre, et audire, et memoria retinere?

VERS. 4.-- Facie ad faciem, etc. (AUG.) Paulo ante admonere curavit, etc., usque ad quamvis aliqui hoc speraverint.

VERS. 5.-- Inter vos et Deum. (ID., quaest. 10.) Tanquam 457DDominus esset in loco, etc., usque ad audiens de medio ignis vocem Dei dicentis decalogum legis.

VERS. 9.-- Reddens iniquitatem, etc. (ISID.) Sunt qui ita edisserant, etc., usque ad sed sententiam diu differat.

His qui oderunt me, et faciens misericordiam in multa millia diligentibus me, etc. His scilicet, qui haereditaria impietate Dominum oderunt. Perversum est enim peccata patrum filiis non peccantibus imputari, cum per Ezechielem dictum sit: Filius non portabit iniquitatem, etc. (Ezech. XVIII).

VERS. 11.-- Non usurpabis nomen Domini Dei frustra, etc. Primum mandatum ad Patrem pertinet quod dicitur in sequentibus: Audi, Israel, Dominus 458ADeus tuus unus est (Deut. VI); ut hoc scilicet audiens, unum Deum Patrem colas, non multos deos suscipias. Secundum pertinet ad Filium, de quo hic dicitur: Non assumes nomen Dei tui in vanum, id est, non aestimes creaturam esse Dei Filium. Omnis enim creatura subjecta est vanitati, sed credas eum aequalem Patri, Deum deorum, Deum apud Deum, per quem omnia facta sunt. Tertium ad Spiritum, cujus dono requies aeterna promittitur: quia enim Spiritus sanctus dicitur septiformis, ideo septimum diem sanctificavit Deus. In aliis enim diebus operum sanctificatio non nominatur, nisi in sabbato, in quo requievit Deus. Recte ergo hoc mandatum pertinet ad Spiritum: tum propter sanctificationis nomen; tum propter aeternam requiem, ad donum sancti 458BSpiritus pertinentem. Dicitur enim sic: Memento ut diem sabbati sanctifices: sex diebus operaberis, etc. In opere sex dierum, etc., videtur sex millium annorum operatio signari, in septimo tempus aeternae quietis, in quo post bona opera quies aeterna promittitur. Quidquid ergo agimus, si propter futuram requiem facimus, sabbatum observamus.

VERS. 16.-- Honora patrem tuum et matrem, sicut praecepit tibi. Post tria praecepta succedunt septem quae ad proximum pertinent. Inter septem primum, sed in ordine omnium quartum est: Honora patrem tuum, etc. A parentibus enim homo oculos aperit, et vita haec ab eorum dilectione exordium sumit: unde hoc mandatum, maximum et primum dicitur in altera scilicet tabula. Jubemur ergo parentes honorare, et 458Cofficium pietatis et reverentiam eis exhibere; qui enim hoc parentibus non facit, quomodo faciet aliis? Quintum: Non maechaberis. Id est praeter legitimam uxorem nulli misceberis. Sextum: Non occides. Occidit etiam qui fame vel nuditate proximum mori videt, nec subvenit in quantum potest. Septimum: Non furtum facies. In quo omnem rapinam prohibet. Octavum: Non falsum testimonium dices. Crimen mendacii et falsitatis abominatur. Nonum: Non concupisces uxorem proximi tui. In quo etiam adulterinam cogitationem interdicit. Decimum: Non concupisces rem proximi tui. In quo ambitionem et concupiscentiam percutit. Primum ergo prohibet subreptionem; secundum, errorem; tertium 458Dsaeculi amorem; quartum, impietatem; quintum, fornicationem; sextum, crudelitatem; septimum, rapacitatem; octavum, falsitatem; nonum, cogitationem adulterii; decimum, cupiditatem mundi. Et notandum, quod sicut decem plagis percutiuntur Aegyptii, sic decem praeceptis regitur populus Dei, et daemones occiduntur.

VERS. 22.-- In duabus tabulis lapideis quas tradidit mihi. (ISID.) Duo Testamenta, etc., usque ad sensibile scilicet et intelligibile.

VERS. 29.-- Quis det talem eos habere mentem? Vult Deus intelligi gratia sua hoc beneficium concedi, ut in omnibus sit justitia Dei ex fide, non quasi propria ex lege, unde: Auferam eis cor lapideum, et dabo cor carneum (Ezech. XXXVI), id est, 459Asensatum, caro enim sensum habet, lapis vero non habet; et alibi: Idoneos nos fecit ministros Novi Testamenti, non littera sed spiritu.

CHAPTER V.

VERS. 1.-- Hear, O Israel, etc. (AUG., quaest. 9 in Deut.) Whether it was said for this reason, etc., as far as both to see, and to hear, and to retain in memory?

VERS. 4.-- Face to face, etc. (AUG.) A little before he took care to admonish, etc., as far as although some have hoped this.

VERS. 5.-- Between you and God. (ID., quaest. 10.) As though 457Dthe Lord were in a place, etc., as far as hearing out of the midst of the fire the voice of God speaking the decalogue of the law.

VERS. 9.-- Repaying the iniquity, etc. (ISID.) There are those who so expound it, etc., as far as but long defers the sentence.

To them that hate me, and showing mercy unto many thousands to them that love me, etc. To those, namely, who by hereditary impiety hate the Lord. For it is perverse that the sins of the fathers should be imputed to sons who do not sin, since it was said through Ezechiel: The son shall not bear the iniquity, etc. (Ezech. XVIII).

VERS. 11.-- Thou shalt not usurp the name of the Lord God in vain, etc. The first commandment pertains to the Father, which is spoken in what follows: Hear, O Israel, the Lord 458Athy God is one (Deut. VI); that, hearing this, thou mayest worship one God the Father, and not take to thyself many gods. The second pertains to the Son, of whom it is here said: Thou shalt not take the name of thy God in vain, that is, esteem not the Son of God to be a creature. For every creature is subject to vanity; but believe thou him equal to the Father, God of gods, God with God, through whom all things were made. The third to the Spirit, by whose gift eternal rest is promised: for because the Holy Spirit is called sevenfold, therefore God sanctified the seventh day. For on the other days no sanctification of the works is named, save on the sabbath, in which God rested. Rightly therefore does this commandment pertain to the Spirit: both because of the name of sanctification, and because of the eternal rest, which pertains to the gift of the Holy 458BSpirit. For it is said thus: Remember that thou keep holy the sabbath day: six days shalt thou labour, etc. In the work of six days, etc., there seems to be signified the working of six thousand years; in the seventh, the time of eternal rest, in which after good works eternal rest is promised. Whatsoever therefore we do, if we do it for the sake of the rest to come, we keep the sabbath.

VERS. 16.-- Honour thy father and thy mother, as he hath commanded thee. After the three precepts follow seven which pertain to the neighbour. Among the seven it is the first, but in the order of them all the fourth: Honour thy father, etc. For from his parents a man opens his eyes, and this life takes its beginning from love of them: whence this commandment is called the greatest and the first, namely, on the second table. We are bidden therefore to honour our parents, and 458Cto show them the duty of piety and reverence; for he who does not this to his parents, how shall he do it to others? The fifth: Thou shalt not commit adultery. That is, thou shalt be joined to none besides thy lawful wife. The sixth: Thou shalt not kill. He also kills who sees his neighbour dying of hunger or of nakedness, and does not come to his aid so far as he can. The seventh: Thou shalt not steal. In which he forbids all rapine. The eighth: Thou shalt not bear false witness. He abominates the crime of lying and of falsehood. The ninth: Thou shalt not covet thy neighbour's wife. In which he interdicts also the adulterous thought. The tenth: Thou shalt not covet thy neighbour's goods. In which he strikes at ambition and concupiscence. The first therefore forbids stealthy deception; the second, error; the third, 458Dthe love of the world; the fourth, impiety; the fifth, fornication; the sixth, cruelty; the seventh, rapacity; the eighth, falsehood; the ninth, the thought of adultery; the tenth, the desire of the world. And it is to be noted that, as the Egyptians are struck with ten plagues, so the people of God is ruled by ten precepts, and the demons are slain.

VERS. 22.-- On two tables of stone, which he delivered to me. (ISID.) The two Testaments, etc., as far as namely, the sensible and the intelligible.

VERS. 29.-- Who shall grant them to have such a mind? God wills it to be understood that this benefit is granted by his grace, that in all things there may be the justice of God which is of faith, not as it were one's own which is of the law; whence: I will take away from them the heart of stone, and will give them a heart of flesh (Ezech. XXXVI), that is, 459Aone having sense; for flesh has sense, but stone has not; and elsewhere: He hath made us fit ministers of the New Testament, not in the letter, but in the spirit.

CAPUT VI.

VERS. 5.-- Diliges Dominum. (AUG., lib. I de Doctrina Christiana, c. 26, 27, 28.) Charitas non auferetur, etc., usque ad qui pro locorum et temporum vel quarumlibet rerum opportunitate magis nobis conjuncti sunt.

(GREG., lib. VII Moral., cap. 10.) Diliges Dominum Deum tuum, etc. Per amorem Dei gignitur amor proximi, etc., usque ad in bonis enim non agnoscitur amicus, et in malis non absconditur inimicus.

Triplicis naturae est anima: ideo jubetur diligere 459Btripliciter. Non vult Deus amorem suum partiri, ut simul diligamus Deum, et aurum, vel uxorem, vel filios. Nemo potest duobus dominis servire (Matth. VI), ipse super omnia diligendus, ejus dilectio praeponitur.

VERS. 8.-- Et ligabis ea quasi signum. Hoc Pharisaei male interpretantes, in membranulis decalogum (id est, decem verba) scribebant, et ligata in fronte portabant. Quod usque hodie faciunt Babylonii, ut putentur religiosi. Unde: Dilatant enim phylacteria sua, et magnificant fimbrias (Matth. XXIII).

VERS. 13.-- Per nomen illius jurabis. (AUG., quaest. 12.) Permittuntur jurare per nomen Dei, etc., usque ad qui autem omnino non jurat, longe a perjurio est.

459CVERS. 16.-- Non tentabis Dominum Deum. Deum tentat, qui habens quod faciat, sine ratione se committit periculo, experiens utrum possit liberari a Deo.

CHAPTER VI.

VERS. 5.-- Thou shalt love the Lord. (AUG., lib. I de Doctrina Christiana, c. 26, 27, 28.) Charity shall not be taken away, etc., as far as who by the opportunity of places and of times, or of any things whatsoever, are more closely joined to us.

(GREG., lib. VII Moral., cap. 10.) Thou shalt love the Lord thy God, etc. Through the love of God is begotten the love of the neighbour, etc., as far as for in good things a friend is not known, and in evil things an enemy is not hidden.

The soul is of a threefold nature: therefore it is bidden to love 459Bin a threefold manner. God wills not that his love be divided, so that we should love at once God, and gold, or wife, or children. No man can serve two masters (Matth. VI); he himself is to be loved above all things, his love is set first.

VERS. 8.-- And thou shalt bind them as a sign. The Pharisees, misinterpreting this, wrote the decalogue (that is, the ten words) upon little parchments, and carried them bound upon the forehead. Which the Babylonians do to this day, that they may be thought religious. Whence: For they make broad their phylacteries, and enlarge their fringes (Matth. XXIII).

VERS. 13.-- Thou shalt swear by his name. (AUG., quaest. 12.) They are permitted to swear by the name of God, etc., as far as but he who swears not at all is far from perjury.

459CVERS. 16.-- Thou shalt not tempt the Lord God. He tempts God who, having something that he might do, commits himself to danger without reason, trying whether he can be delivered by God.

CAPUT VII.

VERS. 1.-- Gentes multas. Septem gentes sunt septem principalia vitia, quae per Dei gratiam spiritalis miles exsuperans exterminare jubetur. Majoris numeri dicuntur, quia plura sunt vitia quam virtutes. In catalogo dicuntur septem nationes, in expugnatione sine numero ponuntur; sed, cum deleverit gentes multas coram te. Numerosior enim est, quam Israel, populus carnalium passionum, qui multiplicatur de septenario fomite vitiorum. De gastrimargia, comessatio, ebrietas; de fornicatione, turpiloquia, 459Dscurrilitas, ludicra, et stultiloquia; de philargyria, mendacium, fraus, furta, perjuria, turpis lucri appetitus, falsa testimonia, violentia, inhumanitas, et rapacitas; de ira homicidium, clamor et indignatio; de tristitia, rancor, pusillanimitas, amaritudo, et desperatio, et pigritia; de acedia, otiositas, somnolentia, importunitas, inquietudo mentis et corporis, instabilitas, verbositas, et curiositas; de cenodoxia, contentiones, haereses, jactantia, et praesumptio novitatum; de superbia, contemptus, invidia, inobedientia, blasphemia, murmuratio, detractio, et alia plura. Haec cum majoris numeri sint quam virtutes, devictis tamen principalibus omnes quiescunt, et perpetua nece delentur. Quod autem hae pestes robustiores 460Asint, in tentatione sentimus: fortius enim militat in membris nostris carnalium delectatio passionum, quam studia virtutum, quae summa cordis et corporis contritione acquiruntur. Harum gentium regiones possidemus, cum Israelis exercitu, id est virtutibus vitia superamus.
