Ut in corde nostro locum fornicationis castitas obtineat; locum furoris, patientia; locum tristitiae, salutaris laetitia; acediae locum incolat fortitudo; locum superbiae, humilitas, sic singulis vitiis expulsis loco eorum regnent filii Israel, id est, animae Deum videntes. Cum vero Abrahae de futuris loqueretur Deus, non septem sed decem gentes numerasse legitur, quorum terra semini ejus promittitur, abjectis scilicet idololatriis gentium, blasphemia Judaeorum, errore haereticorum, 460Bquibus ante notitiam Dei et gratiam baptismi, in spirituali Aegypto commorantur, subjecti sunt. Sed qui inde egreditur, et per gratiam Dei ad eremum spiritualem pervenit, ab impugnatione trium gentium liberatur, contra septem congreditur.

Haetheum et Gergezaeum, etc. (HIERON. de Nom. Hebr., tom. III.) Hethaeum, etc., usque ad id est, calcatum sive praesepe eorum.

Septem gentes. Abrahae permissum est quod decem delerentur: tunc enim decem erant, sed cum filii Israel transirent ad Jordanem, jam tres deletae erant. Filii enim Loth deleverunt gigantes; filii Esau Horraeos; Cappadocesve partem Hevaeorum.

Et robustiores. Spiritales nequitiae subtiliores sunt, 460Cet plus valent ad decipiendum, quam infirmitas hominum ad sustinendum.

VERS. 14.-- Sterilis. Quaedam steriles apud Judaeos fuerunt, sed adeo paucae, ut ad comparationem fecundarum viderentur nullae.

VERS. 20.-- Insuper et crabrones, etc. Non legitur alicubi in historia, quod crabrones vel vespas in hostes eorum Deus praemiserit. Sed per animalia aculeata timoris compunctiones designat, quibus hostes territos fugabat.

VERS. 22.-- Non poteris, etc. Cavendum esse ostendit, ne forte, expulsis peccatis a carne nostra, insurgat spiritalis bestia, id est jactantia, superbia, vel vana gloria, quae difficilius exstirpantur quam carnalia vitia. Loth enim, qui in Sodomis continuit, 460Din monte fornicatione pollutus est (Gen. XIX). Expulso vitio statim virtus plantetur ne immundus spiritus redeat, et domum vacantem inveniat. (Matth. XII).

(RAB., GREG.) Quibusdam majora bona Deus dat, nec minora concedit, ut semper animus eorum habeat unde se reprehendat, et dum perfecti esse appetunt nec possunt, minime se extollant in his quae habent, qui per se parva non habere valent. Hinc est quod fortes et potentes adversarios exstinxit Philisthaeos; et Chananaeos diu reservavit, ut in eis erudiretur Israel habens contra quos pugnaret, ne de majoribus devictis sese extolleret. Eadem ergo mens pollet ex virtute, lassescit ex infirmitate, ut 461Adum quaerit quod habere non valet, quod habet humiliter servet. Superna quoque regio in civibus suis ex parte damna pertulit, ex parte fortiter stetit: ut electi angeli dum alios per superbiam cecidisse conspicerent, tanto robustius quanto humilius starent. Sic in unaquaque anima agitur, ut maxima lucra conferant, aliquando minima damnant.

VERS. 26.-- Anathema. Ἀπὸ τοῦ ἀναθήναι, id est, a sursum ponendo, vel suspendendo, sicut donaria in templis suspendebantur, et ab eis qui dabant alienabantur.

CHAPTER VII.

VERS. 1.-- Many nations. The seven nations are the seven principal vices, which the spiritual soldier, overcoming them by the grace of God, is bidden to exterminate. They are said to be of a greater number, because the vices are more than the virtues. In the catalogue seven nations are named; in the storming of them they are set down without number; but, when he shall have destroyed many nations before thee. For more numerous than Israel is the people of carnal passions, which is multiplied out of the sevenfold tinder of the vices. Of gastrimargia: revelling, drunkenness; of fornication: filthy speech, 459Dscurrility, jests, and foolish talk; of philargyria: lying, fraud, thefts, perjuries, the appetite of base gain, false testimonies, violence, inhumanity, and rapacity; of anger: murder, clamour, and indignation; of sadness: rancour, pusillanimity, bitterness, and despair, and laziness; of acedia: idleness, drowsiness, importunity, unquietness of mind and body, instability, wordiness, and curiosity; of cenodoxia: contentions, heresies, boasting, and the presumption of novelties; of pride: contempt, envy, disobedience, blasphemy, murmuring, detraction, and many more. Although these are of a greater number than the virtues, yet, when the principal ones have been overcome, they all fall quiet and are blotted out by a perpetual slaying. But that these plagues are the stronger, we feel in temptation: for the delight of carnal passions wars more strongly in our members 460Athan do the pursuits of the virtues, which are acquired by the utmost contrition of heart and body. We possess the regions of these nations when with the army of Israel, that is, with the virtues, we overcome the vices. So that in our heart chastity may hold the place of fornication; patience the place of fury; saving gladness the place of sadness; that fortitude may inhabit the place of acedia; humility the place of pride: so that, each several vice being cast out, the children of Israel may reign in their place, that is, souls seeing God. But when God was speaking to Abraham of things to come, he is read to have numbered not seven but ten nations, whose land is promised to his seed — when, that is, the idolatries of the Gentiles, the blasphemy of the Jews, and the error of the heretics have been cast away, 460Bto which they are subject who, before the knowledge of God and the grace of baptism, tarry in spiritual Egypt. But he who goes out thence, and by the grace of God attains to the spiritual desert, is set free from the assault of three nations, and closes in battle against seven.

The Haethite and the Gergezite, etc. (HIERON. de Nom. Hebr., tom. III.) The Hethite, etc., as far as that is, trodden down, or their stall.

Seven nations. To Abraham it was granted that ten should be destroyed: for then there were ten, but when the children of Israel were passing over to the Jordan, three had already been destroyed. For the sons of Lot destroyed the giants; the sons of Esau the Horrites; or the Cappadocians a part of the Hevites.

And stronger. The spiritual wickednesses are subtler, 460Cand avail more for deceiving than the weakness of men for enduring.

VERS. 14.-- Barren. There were certain barren women among the Jews, but so few that, by comparison with the fruitful, they seemed none at all.

VERS. 20.-- Moreover also hornets, etc. It is nowhere read in the history that God sent hornets or wasps before them against their enemies. But by stinging animals he designates the prickings of fear, by which he put their terrified enemies to flight.

VERS. 22.-- Thou shalt not be able, etc. He shows that care must be taken lest perchance, when sins have been driven out of our flesh, a spiritual beast rise up, that is, boasting, pride, or vainglory, which are rooted out with more difficulty than carnal vices. For Lot, who contained himself in Sodom, 460Dwas defiled with fornication upon the mountain (Gen. XIX). When a vice has been driven out, let a virtue at once be planted, lest the unclean spirit return and find the house empty. (Matth. XII).

(RAB., GREG.) To some God gives the greater goods, and does not grant the lesser, that their mind may always have whence to reprove itself, and that, while they long to be perfect and cannot, they may in no wise extol themselves in the things they have, who of themselves are unable to have small things. Hence it is that he destroyed the strong and mighty adversaries, the Philistines, and long kept back the Canaanites, that in them Israel might be schooled, having those against whom he should fight, lest he should extol himself over the greater ones he had overcome. The same mind therefore is strong from virtue and grows weary from weakness, so that, 461Awhile it seeks what it is unable to have, it may humbly keep what it has. The region above too suffered losses in part among its citizens, and in part stood bravely: so that the elect angels, while they beheld others fallen through pride, might stand the more stoutly the more humbly. So it is wrought in each several soul, that they may confer the greatest gains; sometimes they condemn the least [damnant; read damna, "the smallest losses"].

VERS. 26.-- Anathema. Ἀπὸ τοῦ ἀναθήναι, that is, from setting above, or from hanging up, as offerings were hung up in the temples, and were alienated from those who gave them.

CAPUT VIII.

VERS. 2.-- Quadraginta annis. Ideo enim per quadraginta annos permisit eos affligi, ut cognoscerent 461Bquantae fragilitatis essent.

Ut affligeret te. (AUG., quaest. 13.) Hic apertius dictum est, etc., usque ad quod si dixisset, intelligendum erat, cognita faceret.

VERS. 3.-- Manna. Carnem Christi, qua refecti ad veram promissionis terram veniamus. Quadraginta annis manna comedimus, id est, toto tempore nostro, quod quatuor vicissitudinibus volvitur, Dominici corporis sacramento pascimur.

Non in solo pane vivit homo, sed in omni, etc. Spiritalis cibus commendatur, unde animae in aeternum victurae satiantur. Manna de coelo datum carnem Christi significat, qui ait: Ego sum panis vitae, hic est panis qui de coelo descendit (Joan. VI), quia Verbum caro factum est, et habitavit in nobis. Qui manducat 461Chanc carnem, habet vitam aeternam.

Non in solo pane, etc. Significat geminam naturae nostrae substantiam. Homo enim ex anima et corpore constat: corpus, quod ex terra est, terreno alitur cibo; anima vero vivit de verbo Dei; spiritum vero hominis vivificat spiritus Dei, si tamen homo est in corpore Ecclesiae, id est Christi, et in fide Filii Dei. Ideo addit: Vestimentum ejus vetustate non deficere; quia novi hominis conversatio in novitate vitae debet permanere, cujus pes non est subtritus, quia gressus operum ejus servatur illaesus: Quia novit Dominus viam justorum, et iter impiorum peribit (Psal. I).

VERS. 11.-- Judicia. Quae per Moysen post legem datam diversis temporibus populo praecepta sunt, 461Dquae in Exodo justificationes appellantur.

Et caeremonias. Circumcisionem, ritus sacrificiorum. Unde Ambrosius super Epistolam ad Romanos: Triplex (inquit) lex est, prima pars de sacramento divinitatis; secunda congruit legi naturali, quae peccatum interdicit; tertia festorum legis, id est, sabbata, neomeniae, et circumcisio.

VERS. 15.-- Ac dipsas. Genus aspidis quae Latine dicitur situla, quemcunque momorderit, siti perit; adeo parva ut cum calcatur non videatur. Cujus venenum ante exstinguit, quam sentiatur, nec tristitiam sentit moriturus.

Mystice. Varii serpentes haereses sunt inter se quidem diversae, sed omnes mortiferae.

CHAPTER VIII.

VERS. 2.-- Forty years. For he therefore suffered them to be afflicted through forty years, that they might know 461Bof how great frailty they were.

That he might afflict thee. (AUG., quaest. 13.) Here it is said more openly, etc., as far as and if he had said this, it was to be understood, he would make them known.

VERS. 3.-- Manna. The flesh of Christ, refreshed by which we may come to the true land of promise. For forty years we eat manna, that is, through our whole time, which is rolled round in four changes, we are fed with the sacrament of the Lord's body.

Not in bread alone doth man live, but in every, etc. Spiritual food is commended, whence souls that are to live for ever are filled. The manna given from heaven signifies the flesh of Christ, who says: I am the bread of life; this is the bread which came down from heaven (Joan. VI), because the Word was made flesh and dwelt among us. He that eateth 461Cthis flesh hath life everlasting.

Not in bread alone, etc. It signifies the twofold substance of our nature. For man consists of soul and body: the body, which is of the earth, is nourished with earthly food; but the soul lives of the word of God; and the Spirit of God quickens the spirit of man, if indeed the man is in the body of the Church, that is, of Christ, and in the faith of the Son of God. Therefore he adds: That his garment failed not by age; because the conversation of the new man ought to abide in newness of life, whose foot is not worn away, because the step of his works is kept unhurt: For the Lord knoweth the way of the just, and the way of the wicked shall perish (Psal. I).

VERS. 11.-- Judgments. Those which were enjoined upon the people by Moses at divers times after the law was given, 461Dwhich in Exodus are called justifications.

And ceremonies. Circumcision, the rites of sacrifices. Whence Ambrose on the Epistle to the Romans: Threefold (says he) is the law: the first part is of the sacrament of the Godhead; the second agrees with the natural law, which forbids sin; the third is of the feasts of the law, that is, sabbaths, new moons, and circumcision.

VERS. 15.-- And the dipsas. A kind of asp which in Latin is called situla: whomsoever it has bitten perishes of thirst; so small that when it is trodden upon it is not seen. Its venom extinguishes before it is felt, nor does he who is to die feel any sadness.

Mystically. The divers serpents are heresies, differing indeed among themselves, but all deadly.

CAPUT IX.

462A

VERS. 5.-- Neque enim, etc. (AUG., quaest. 14.) Isti sunt qui non meruerunt perire in deserto, etc., usque ad ubi illi exacerbaverunt Dominum, qui merito suo in terram promissionis introducti non sunt.

VERS. 10.-- Deditque, etc. (AUG., quaest. 15.) In Exodo legitur quod tabulas Moysi Dominus dederit, etc., usque ad durat in saeculum.

CHAPTER IX.

462A

VERS. 5.-- For neither, etc. (AUG., quaest. 14.) These are they who did not deserve to perish in the desert, etc., as far as where those provoked the Lord who by their own desert were not brought into the land of promise.

VERS. 10.-- And he gave, etc. (AUG., quaest. 15.) In Exodus it is read that the Lord gave the tables to Moses, etc., as far as it endures unto the age.

CAPUT X.

VERS. 4.-- Scripsitque in. (AUG. quaest. 14 in Deut.) In Exodo Moyses, etc., usque ad ipse autem adjuvat bonae voluntatis arbitrium.

VERS. 6.-- In Mosera, etc. In libro Numeri in 462Bmonte Hor mortuus scribitur, sed forsitan locus est binomius. Beroth enim filiorum Jacan locus est in deserto, sicut Mosera, ubi obiit Aaron, sicut in libris locorum legitur. Et ostenditur usque hodie in decimo lapide urbis Petrae in montis vertice

VERS. 8.-- Tribum Levi. Nisi per hanc significaretur omne regale sacerdotium, quod pertinet ad Novum Testamentum, non auderet homo dicere, qui de ea tribu non erat: Pars mea Dominus (Psal. LXXII); et alibi: Dominus pars haereditatis meae, etc. (Ibid., XV.)

VERS. 14.-- Coelum coeli, etc. Quidam dicunt tres esse coelos, aereum, aethereum, et sidereum. Nonnulli septem, primum aereum, secundum aethereum, tertium olympium, quartum igneum, quintum firmamentum, 462Csextum aqueum, septimum angelorum.

VERS. 16.-- Circumcidite, etc. Spiritalem circumcisionem significat, id est mentis custodiam, et veram abstinentiam. Quasi, cum luxuriam carnis exstinxeritis, etiam mentis superflua resecato. Circumcidendi sunt omnes sensus. Unde Moyses se incircumcisum labiis conqueritur; et Stephanus Judaeis: Incircumcisi, inquit, corde et auribus, etc. (Act. VII). Vere ergo circumcisus est, qui obturat aures ne audiat sanguinem, et claudit oculos, ne videat malum; qui non delinquit in lingua, cujus cor non gravatur crapula et ebrietate, qui lavat inter innocentes manus suas, et ab omni via mala prohibet pedes suos; qui castigat corpus suum et servituti 462Dsubjicit, et omni custodia servat cor suum. Bina quoque circumcisione indigemus, ne ex his quaeramus vanam gloriam, ne foris mundi, et intus immundi simus, et simulatae sanctitatis poenas sustineamus, sicut Sichimitae qui patriarcharum circumcisionem imitati sunt, non Dei amore, sed ob causam luxuriae: ideo nihil mercedis acquisiverunt, sed die tertia intra civitatis suae ruinas perierunt. Tales enim in tempore resurrectionis evacuatis, quibus confidebant, virtutibus, in aeternum moriuntur.

VERS. 22.-- Descenderunt patres tui in Aegyptum. Descenditur in Aegyptum: Facilis est descensus Averni. In Jerusalem ascenditur, quia arcta est via quae ducit ad vitam, et tendit in ardua virtus

CHAPTER X.

VERS. 4.-- And he wrote in. (AUG. quaest. 14 in Deut.) In Exodus Moses, etc., as far as but he himself helps the choice of a good will.

VERS. 6.-- In Mosera, etc. In the book of Numbers he is written to have died upon 462Bmount Hor, but perhaps the place is of two names. For Beroth of the children of Jacan is a place in the desert, as is Mosera, where Aaron died, as is read in the books of places. And it is shown unto this day at the tenth milestone from the city of Petra, upon the top of the mountain

VERS. 8.-- The tribe of Levi. Unless by this were signified the whole royal priesthood which pertains to the New Testament, a man who was not of that tribe would not dare to say: The Lord is my portion (Psal. LXXII); and elsewhere: The Lord is the portion of my inheritance, etc. (Ibid., XV.)

VERS. 14.-- The heaven of heaven, etc. Some say that there are three heavens: the airy, the ethereal, and the starry. Not a few say seven: the first airy, the second ethereal, the third olympian, the fourth fiery, the fifth the firmament, 462Cthe sixth watery, the seventh of the angels.

VERS. 16.-- Circumcise, etc. It signifies spiritual circumcision, that is, the keeping of the mind and true abstinence. As if to say: when you have extinguished the wantonness of the flesh, cut away also the superfluities of the mind. All the senses are to be circumcised. Whence Moses complains that he is uncircumcised in his lips; and Stephen to the Jews: Uncircumcised, he says, in heart and ears, etc. (Act. VII). Truly therefore he is circumcised who stoppeth his ears that he may not hear blood, and shutteth his eyes that he may not see evil; who does not offend with his tongue, whose heart is not weighed down with surfeiting and drunkenness, who washeth his hands among the innocent, and keepeth back his feet from every evil way; who chastiseth his body and bringeth it 462Dinto subjection, and with all watchfulness keepeth his heart. We need a twofold circumcision also, lest out of these things we seek vainglory, lest we be clean without and unclean within, and undergo the penalties of a feigned holiness, like the Sichemites, who imitated the circumcision of the patriarchs, not for the love of God, but for the cause of wantonness: therefore they gained nothing of reward, but on the third day perished within the ruins of their own city. For such men, at the time of the resurrection, when the virtues in which they trusted have been emptied out, die for ever.

VERS. 22.-- Thy fathers went down into Egypt. There is a going down into Egypt: Easy is the descent of Avernus. There is a going up into Jerusalem, because narrow is the way that leadeth to life, and virtue makes for the steep

CAPUT XI.

463A

VERS. 10.-- De qua existi. Mente, duce Moyse, id est docente lege terrenas voluptates despicere, et terram promissionis duce Jesu intrare.

In hortorum. Quia carnales quique praesentem felicitatem et copiam rerum temporalium appetunt sibi multiplicare.

VERS. 12.-- A principio anni. Ab initio scilicet bonae voluntatis usque ad perfectionem boni operis cursum vitae dirigit. Deus enim est qui operatur in nobis velle et perficere pro bona voluntate (Philip. II).

VERS. 14.-- Temporaneam, etc. Quia electis suis priore tempore legis intellectum contulit; serotinam pluviam tribuit, quia ultimo tempore incarnationis suae mysterium patefecit nobis.

463BEt serotinam, etc. Quia novissima hora est, cum praedicatio ejus ad nos pervenit, qui dicit: Elevatio manuum mearum sacrificium vespertinum (Psal. CXL).

VERS. 20.-- Scribes ea super postes. (AUG., quaest. 17.) Hoc secundum litteram an hyperbolica commendatio est, sicut multa dicuntur?

VERS. 29.-- Montem Garizim. Garizim interpretatur Divisio vel Advena; Hebal, Vorago vetus interpretatur. Qui enim a vitiis se separant, et se advenas et peregrinos in hoc mundo judicant, etiam aeternam merentur benedictionem. Qui vero in voraginem veteris vitae se praecipitant, carnalibus desideriis servientes, aeternae maledictioni subjacebunt

CHAPTER XI.

463A

VERS. 10.-- Out of which thou camest. In mind, with Moses for leader, that is, with the law teaching us to despise earthly pleasures and to enter the land of promise with Jesus for leader.

Of gardens. Because all carnal men desire to multiply for themselves present happiness and an abundance of temporal things.

VERS. 12.-- From the beginning of the year. That is, from the beginning of a good will unto the perfection of good work he directs the course of life. For it is God who worketh in us to will and to accomplish, according to good will (Philip. II).

VERS. 14.-- The early, etc. Because in the former time he bestowed upon his elect the understanding of the law; he granted the latter rain, because in the last time he laid open to us the mystery of his incarnation.

463BAnd the latter, etc. Because it is the last hour when his preaching reaches us, who says: The lifting up of my hands, an evening sacrifice (Psal. CXL).

VERS. 20.-- Thou shalt write them upon the door posts. (AUG., quaest. 17.) Is this according to the letter, or is it a hyperbolical commendation, as many things are said?

VERS. 29.-- Mount Garizim. Garizim is interpreted Division or Stranger; Hebal is interpreted Old Gulf. For they who separate themselves from vices, and judge themselves strangers and pilgrims in this world, merit also the eternal blessing. But they who cast themselves headlong into the gulf of the old life, serving carnal desires, shall lie under the eternal curse

CAPUT XII.

463C

VERS. 6.-- Decimas et primitias, etc. (AUG., quaest. 18.) Quaerendum est quomodo jubeat decimationes omnium fructuum et primitiva omnium pecorum non manducari, nisi in civitate ubi templum erat, cum eas Levitis dari in lege praeceperit.

VERS. 13.-- Cave ne offeras. Occasionem amputat idolis sacrificandi. Ideo enim praecipit in templo et ad altare Domini hostias offerri, ut ubi integra est religio, ibi cultus congruus exhibeatur. Mystice autem innuit nullum munus acceptabile esse Deo extra catholicam Ecclesiam, hic enim est locus quem Deus elegit, ut poneret nomen suum 463Dibi, et habitet in eo.

VERS. 16.-- Absque esu. Homicidium prohibet. Sanguis pro anima ponitur, quia sedes animae, id est humanae vitae, in sanguine est.

VERS. 17.-- In oppidis. Id est, meritis tuis non assignabis, quae divina largitate perceperis, non viribus tuis, sed Deo, cujus munere hoc habueris (Gal. VI).

VERS. 23.-- Hoc solum cave. Ad litteram apostoli hoc praeceperunt: Et in sanguine prohibetur homicidium, et odium fratrum. Qui enim odit fratrem suum homicida est (I Joan. III).

Sanguis enim eorum. Quia aliquod vitale est in sanguine, et per ipsum maxime in hac carne vivitur, 464Aqui per omnes venas diffunditur: ipsum animam vocat, id est, corporis vitam, quae morte finitur. Mystice autem innuitur, ut carnales homines a pristina conversatione discedentes, in unitate fidei et bonorum operum sociantes, eorum errores in usum nostrum non convertamus; sed effundamus, id est, per confessionem prolatos terrenae fragilitati deputemus, et quasi indignos viris spiritualibus abjiciamus.

CHAPTER XII.

463C

VERS. 6.-- Tithes and firstfruits, etc. (AUG., quaest. 18.) It is to be asked how he commands that the tithings of all fruits and the firstlings of all cattle be not eaten save in the city where the temple was, whereas in the law he had commanded them to be given to the Levites.

VERS. 13.-- Beware lest thou offer. He cuts off the occasion of sacrificing to idols. For he therefore commands that victims be offered in the temple and at the altar of the Lord, that where religion is whole, there a fitting worship may be shown. But mystically he intimates that no gift is acceptable to God outside the catholic Church; for this is the place which God chose, that he might put his name 463Dthere, and dwell in it.

VERS. 16.-- Without eating. He forbids murder. The blood is put for the soul, because the seat of the soul, that is, of human life, is in the blood.

VERS. 17.-- In thy towns. That is, thou shalt not assign to thy own merits the things thou hast received by the divine bounty, not to thy own strength, but to God, by whose gift thou hast had this (Gal. VI).

VERS. 23.-- This only beware. According to the letter the apostles commanded this: and in the blood both murder and the hatred of brethren are forbidden. For he that hateth his brother is a murderer (I Joan. III).

For their blood. Because there is something vital in the blood, and by it chiefly life is lived in this flesh, 464Awhich is diffused through all the veins: this he calls the soul, that is, the life of the body, which is ended by death. But mystically it is intimated that, when carnal men depart from their former manner of life and join themselves in the unity of faith and of good works, we should not turn their errors to our own use, but should pour them out, that is, should reckon them, brought forth by confession, to earthly frailty, and cast them away as unworthy of spiritual men.

CAPUT XIII.

VERS. 1.-- Si surrexerit. Vult intelligi, ea quae a divinis non secundum Deum dicuntur, etiamsi evenerint, non esse sic accipienda, ut fiant quae ab eis praedicuntur, nec praeter potestatem suam ostendit esse, quae ita contingunt. Sed quare permittat 464Bsupponit, ad cognoscendum scilicet quantum diligant Deum servientes ipsis, non illi qui omnia novit.

CHAPTER XIII.

VERS. 1.-- If there rise up. He would have it understood that the things which are spoken by diviners not according to God, even if they come to pass, are not so to be received that the things foretold by them should come about; nor does he show that the things which so happen are beyond his own power. But he subjoins why he permits it, 464Bnamely, for the knowing how much they that serve them love God — not for him who knows all things.

CAPUT XIV.

VERS. 1.-- Non vos incidetis. Usque hodie multae gentes incidunt se in funeribus mortuorum. Unde: Non vos contristemini sicut et caeteri, qui spem non habent. Haec autem facere, eorum est qui desperant de resurrectione.

Nec facietis. Capilli capitis vestri numerati sunt (Matth. X), id est cogitationes, quas non oportet ab honestate capitis auferre et diabolo offerre, sicut quidam corporales capillos diabolo offerunt.

VERS. 3.-- Ne comedatis. Animalia immunda 464Csunt, sed quae significando exprimunt, mores, scilicet hominum, et actus, et voluntates.

VERS. 5.-- Camelopardalum. Qui albis maculis respersus, capite camelo similis, collo equino, pedibus bubalo, per caetera pardus: in Aethiopia nascitur.

Omne animal quod in duas. Illos recipit Ecclesia et membris suis incorporat, qui ungulam findunt, qui discretionem boni et mali habere sciunt, et qui ruminant, id est meditantur in lege die ac nocte.

VERS. 6.-- Quod in duas partes. Qui scilicet duo testamenta suscipiens firmo gradu justitiae et innocentiae sese constituit

VERS. 7.-- Camelum. Judaeum qui liquat culicem, et glutit camelum, qui litteram ruminat, sed non 464Dhabet fissam ungulam, quia a spiritu litteram non dividit, hi sunt scribae et Pharisaei.

Leporem. Per choerogyllum et leporem multitudinem significat Judaeorum, utrumque enim animal debile et infirmum.

Quia ruminant. Ruminant Judaei verba legis, sed ungulam non dividunt, quia in Patrem et Filium non credunt, nec duo testamenta recipiunt. Quidam autem haeretici ungulam findunt, sed doctrinam veritatis in ore non ruminant (Levit. XI).

VERS. 9.-- Haec comedetis ex omnibus quae moventur in aquis. Per haec gentiles intelliguntur, qui in aquis baptismi renascuntur.

Quae habent pinnulas. Meditantes legem vitae sublimis 465Aet coelestis; quia ignorantia divinae legis diu in gentibus fuerat, pinnulas et squamas adjungit: sicut enim pisces squamati sunt et pinnati, sic quibus est ignorantia temporalis, cito additur cognitio sublimis et vitae coelestis.

VERS. 12.-- Et gryphem. Quae est quadrupes, capite et alis aquilae similis, reliquo corpore leoni, et abundat in Hyperboreis montibus, equis maxime infensa et hominibus.

VERS. 15.-- Struthionem. Qui a terra non levatur, sed saecularibus negotiis implicatur.

Noctuam. Quae in nocte acute videt, et non in die. Sicut qui scientia legis gloriantur, et lucem Evangelii non capiunt.

Larum. Qui ut avis volat, ut aquatile natat, qui 465Bscilicet circumcisionem veneratur et baptisma. Talibus Paulus ait: Si circumcidimini, Christus vobis non prodest (Gal. V).

Accipitrem, etc. Qui mansueti videntur, sed potentibus et avaris sociantur.

VERS. 16.-- Cygnum et ibin. Hae longi colli esse, et cibum de profundo terrae trahere dicuntur: nos autem debemus quaerere panem qui de coelo descendit, nec esse solliciti quid manducemus (Joan. VI Matth. VI)

VERS. 17.-- Nycticoracem. Quae nocte repit et operatur, significans eos qui noctis operibus dedicantur, quae sunt fornicatio, immunditia, idolorum cultus et similia.

VERS. 18.-- Upupam. Quae luctum amat. Saeculi 465Cautem tristitia mortem operatur: Nos vero debemus in Domino gaudere, in omnibus gratias agere (II Cor. VII; I Thes. V).

Vespertilionem, etc. Quae circa terram volat, et pennis ambulat. Nostra autem conversatio in coelis debet esse (Phil. III).

VERS. 21.-- Quidquid autem. Mystice praecipitur ecclesiastico viro, ut se in sanctitate servet, et omnem peccati immunditiam devitet, peregrinum, id est, gentilem in suis sordibus remanere permittat.

Peregrino qui intra portas tuas est. Quasi paganis et haereticis immunda relinque. Hinc daemones in Evangelio permittuntur ire in gregem porcorum (Matth. VIII), et dicitur: Sine mortuos sepelire mortuos suos 465D(Luc. IX).

In lacte, mediocribus disciplinis, sed austerioribus, ut restaurarent amara quae perdiderunt dulcia.

VERS. 22.-- Decimam. Quasi, bona quae facis secundum divinae legis praecepta facias, et Domino separes, ut ad ejus laudem omnia facias.

VERS. 23.-- Primogenita. Id est boni operis initia, quae decimanda sunt, id est, Domino consecranda.

VERS. 24.-- Portare. Quasi, qui longe sunt a perfectione sanctorum, nec possunt ascendere culmen virtutum, nec contemplationi vacare: quod possunt, agant humiliter, et devote, et faciant sibi amicos de mammona iniquitatis, et thesauros in 466Acoelis, ut recipiantur in aeterna tabernacula, ubi epulandum et laetandum cum amicis, id est sanctis et electis Dei.

VERS. 26.-- Et emes ex eadem pecunia, etc. Spiritualiter praecipitur, ut semper bonis operibus insistamus, quatenus post hanc vitam ad domum nostram, id est ad coelestem patriam revertamur.

VERS. 28.-- Aliam decimam. Extra illam, scilicet quam manducas cum tuis et Levitis, in loco quem elegerit Dominus.

VERS. 29.-- Venietque Levites. Manifeste ostendit hanc decimam non esse ei qui offert communicandam, sed tantum illis quibus praecepit erogari, in quibus praecipue Leviten posuit.

CHAPTER XIV.

VERS. 1.-- You shall not cut yourselves. Unto this day many nations cut themselves at the funerals of the dead. Whence: Be not you sorrowful, even as the rest, who have not hope. But to do these things belongs to them who despair of the resurrection.

Neither shall you make. The hairs of your head are numbered (Matth. X), that is, the thoughts, which ought not to be taken away from the comeliness of the head and offered to the devil, as certain men offer the bodily hairs to the devil.

VERS. 3.-- Eat not. The animals are unclean, 464Cbut what they express by signifying are the manners, namely, of men, and their acts, and their wills.

VERS. 5.-- The camelopard. Which, sprinkled with white spots, is like a camel in the head, like a horse in the neck, like a buffalo in the feet, and for the rest a leopard: it is born in Ethiopia.

Every beast that into two. The Church receives and incorporates into her own members those who divide the hoof, who know how to have the discernment of good and evil, and who chew the cud, that is, who meditate in the law day and night.

VERS. 6.-- That into two parts. He, namely, who, taking up the two testaments, establishes himself with a firm step of justice and innocence

VERS. 7.-- The camel. The Jew, who strains out the gnat and swallows the camel, who chews the letter, but has not 464Da cloven hoof, because he does not divide the letter from the spirit: these are the scribes and Pharisees.

The hare. By the choerogyllus and the hare he signifies the multitude of the Jews, for each animal is weak and feeble.

Because they chew the cud. The Jews chew the words of the law, but they do not divide the hoof, because they do not believe in the Father and the Son, nor do they receive the two testaments. But certain heretics divide the hoof, yet do not chew the doctrine of truth in their mouth (Levit. XI).

VERS. 9.-- These shall you eat of all that move in the waters. By these are understood the Gentiles, who are born again in the waters of baptism.

Which have little fins. Meditating the law of the sublime 465Aand heavenly life; because ignorance of the divine law had long been among the Gentiles, he adds little fins and scales: for as fishes are scaled and finned, so to those whose ignorance is but temporal there is quickly added the knowledge of the sublime and of the heavenly life.

VERS. 12.-- And the griffin. Which is a four-footed beast, like an eagle in head and wings, like a lion in the rest of its body, and it abounds in the Hyperborean mountains, most hostile to horses and to men.

VERS. 15.-- The ostrich. Which is not lifted up from the earth, but is entangled in secular business.

The night owl. Which sees sharply in the night, and not in the day. Like those who glory in the knowledge of the law, and do not take in the light of the Gospel.

The gull. Which flies as a bird and swims as a water creature: he, namely, who 465Bvenerates both circumcision and baptism. To such Paul says: If you be circumcised, Christ shall profit you nothing (Gal. V).

The hawk, etc. They who seem meek, but ally themselves with the mighty and the greedy.

VERS. 16.-- The swan and the ibis. These are said to be of a long neck, and to draw their food from the deep of the earth: but we ought to seek the bread which came down from heaven, and not to be solicitous what we shall eat (Joan. VI Matth. VI)

VERS. 17.-- The night raven. Which creeps and works by night, signifying those who are given over to the works of night, which are fornication, uncleanness, the worship of idols, and the like.

VERS. 18.-- The hoopoe. Which loves mourning. But the sorrow 465Cof the world worketh death: But we ought to rejoice in the Lord, and in all things to give thanks (II Cor. VII; I Thes. V).

The bat, etc. Which flies about the earth, and walks with its wings. But our conversation ought to be in heaven (Phil. III).

VERS. 21.-- But whatsoever. Mystically it is enjoined upon the ecclesiastical man that he keep himself in holiness, and shun all uncleanness of sin, and suffer the stranger, that is, the Gentile, to remain in his own filth.

To the stranger that is within thy gates. As if to say: leave unclean things to pagans and heretics. Hence in the Gospel the demons are permitted to go into the herd of swine (Matth. VIII), and it is said: Let the dead bury their own dead 465D(Luc. IX).

In milk, by middling disciplines, but by more austere ones, that by bitter things they might restore the sweet things which they lost.

VERS. 22.-- The tithe. As if to say: the good things which thou dost, do according to the precepts of the divine law, and set them apart for the Lord, that thou mayest do all things unto his praise.

VERS. 23.-- The firstborn. That is, the beginnings of good work, which are to be tithed, that is, to be consecrated to the Lord.

VERS. 24.-- To carry. As if to say: they who are far from the perfection of the saints, and can neither ascend the summit of the virtues nor be free for contemplation — what they can, let them do humbly and devoutly, and let them make themselves friends of the mammon of iniquity, and treasures in 466Aheaven, that they may be received into the eternal tabernacles, where there is feasting and rejoicing with friends, that is, with the saints and elect of God.

VERS. 26.-- And thou shalt buy with the same money, etc. Spiritually it is enjoined that we should always press upon good works, that after this life we may return to our own house, that is, to the heavenly fatherland.

VERS. 28.-- Another tithe. Besides that one, namely, which thou eatest with thine own and with the Levites, in the place which the Lord shall choose.

VERS. 29.-- And the Levite shall come. He plainly shows that this tithe is not to be shared with him who offers it, but only with those to whom he commanded it to be paid out, among whom he set the Levite chiefly.

CAPUT XV.

466B

VERS. 1.-- Septimo anno. In hoc capite pietas et dilectio commendatur, ut proximis misereamur, et praemium a Domino exspectemus. Tempore enim suo metemus non deficientes, si, dum tempus habemus, operemur bonum ad omnes, maxime autem ad domesticos fidei (I Tim. V); unde: Dimittite et dimittetur vobis, date et dabitur vobis (Luc. VI).

Septimo anno. Quia in septiformi Spiritu remissio est. Omnes pene legis solemnitates in septenario numero sunt: sabbatum enim in die septima est; et Pentecoste finitis septem hebdomadibus; septimus quoque annus celebratur; et jubilaeus septies septem annis succedit.

466CVERS. 3-- A peregrino. Licet sint omnes proximi nostri, maxime tamen illis misericordia impendenda est, qui Christi sunt nobiscum membra. Qui enim habet substantiam mundi, et fratri indigenti non impendit, quomodo manet in eo charitas Dei? (I Joan. III.)

VERS. 6.-- Foenerabis. Quasi: ditior eris omnibus gentibus. Ecclesia quoque abundat spiritalibus divitiis: ipsa docet, nec habet necesse doceri.

VERS. 9.-- Cave. (AUG., quaest. 21.) LXX: Attende tibi ipsi, ne fiat verbum occultum, etc., usque ad si crudeliter cogitat illo tempore dandum non esse, quo dandum est?

VERS. 12.-- Cum tibi venditus, etc. (ISID.) Si 466DHebraeus puer in servitutem devenerit, etc., usque ad scilicet peccatis in aeternum serviemus.

Cum tibi venditus, etc. Hebraeus transitor qui ad aeternam scilicet patriam tendit, et de vitiis ad virtutes, de terrenis ad coelestia transit; si talis se tibi vendiderit, id est magisterio tuo se addixerit, docebis eum, ut in praesenti vita studeat operari, ut ad requiem pervenire possit. Hoc est, sex annis serviet tibi, septimo dimittes eum liberum, nec vacuum abire patieris; quasi: aeterna praemia et veram libertatem promittes ei in alia vita. Si autem dixerit, Nolo egredi, eo quod diligat te et domum tuam, id est si bona quae fecit non pro aeterna mercede, sed pro humana laude, quasi diligens praesentem 467Avitam fecerit, perforabis aurem ejus subula, id est mentem ejus terrore judicii; compunges in janua domus, quia judicium erit in hujus vitae egressu et alterius ingressu, et serviet tibi usque in aeternum, id est docebis eum si saeculum plus amaverit quam Deum, aeternis suppliciis mancipandum.

(GREG., hom. 3 in Ezech.) Cum activa vita et contemplativa sit nobis ex Dei gratia, etc., usque ad ubi subula recusantis libertatem aurem perfodi praecipitur.

VERS. 19.-- De primogenitis. (AUG., quaest. 23.) Primogenita dicit, etc., usque ad quia solus de substantia Patris, et aequalis et coaeternus.

Sanctificabis Domino Deo tuo. Non tibi, sed omnium largitori: Quia omne datum optimum desursum 467Best (Jac. I). Quod autem leve in nostra cogitatione, vel maculosum in nostra conversatione, vel claudum in operum progressione, vel caecum ignorantia mentis aut aliqua parte deforme vel debile, non debemus Deo immolare, id est, imputare, sed nobis.

Non operaberis. (GREG., lib. VIII Moral., cap. 34.) In primogenito bovis arare, etc., usque ad deceptus animus non deprehendat quae in eis sunt mala.

VERS. 16.-- Sin autem habuerit. Sunt quaedam opera quae licet minus placeant Deo, prosunt tamen proximo; et si Deo offerri non possunt, comedi possunt. Cum enim Deo minus placeant pro fuscata intentione quam Deus intuetur, proximis tamen exemplo opitulantur. Quasi caprea et cervo. Caprea saltatim 467Cincedit, cervus inimicus est serpenti, et aspera et spinosa transiliens, montana transcendit, et ideo in bonam partem accipiuntur.

CHAPTER XV.

466B

VERS. 1.-- In the seventh year. In this chapter piety and love are commended, that we may have mercy upon our neighbours and look for a reward from the Lord. For in due time we shall reap, not failing, if, while we have time, we work good toward all, but most of all toward the household of the faith (I Tim. V); whence: Forgive, and you shall be forgiven; give, and it shall be given to you (Luc. VI).

In the seventh year. Because in the sevenfold Spirit is remission. Almost all the solemnities of the law are in the number seven: for the sabbath is on the seventh day; and Pentecost when seven weeks are finished; the seventh year also is kept; and the jubilee follows after seven times seven years.

466CVERS. 3-- Of the stranger. Although all are our neighbours, yet mercy is chiefly to be bestowed upon those who with us are members of Christ. For he that hath the substance of this world, and bestoweth not upon his needy brother, how doth the charity of God abide in him? (I Joan. III.)

VERS. 6.-- Thou shalt lend. As if to say: thou shalt be richer than all nations. The Church too abounds in spiritual riches: she herself teaches, and has no need to be taught.

VERS. 9.-- Beware. (AUG., quaest. 21.) The LXX: Attend to thyself, lest there be a hidden word, etc., as far as if he cruelly thinks that it is not to be given at that time at which it is to be given?

VERS. 12.-- When there shall be sold to thee, etc. (ISID.) If 466Da Hebrew boy shall have come into servitude, etc., as far as namely, we shall serve sins for ever.

When there shall be sold to thee, etc. The Hebrew is a passer-over, who, namely, tends toward the eternal fatherland, and passes over from vices to virtues, from earthly things to heavenly; if such a one sell himself to thee, that is, make himself over to thy teaching, thou shalt teach him to be zealous to work in the present life, that he may be able to come to rest. That is: six years he shall serve thee, in the seventh thou shalt let him go free, nor shalt thou suffer him to go away empty; as if to say: thou shalt promise him eternal rewards and true liberty in the other life. But if he shall say, I will not go out, because he loveth thee and thy house — that is, if he did the good things he did not for an eternal reward, but for human praise, as one loving the present 467Alife — thou shalt bore his ear with an awl, that is, his mind with the terror of judgment; thou shalt prick him at the door of the house, because the judgment will be at the going out of this life and the entering of the other, and he shall serve thee unto eternity, that is, thou shalt teach him that, if he has loved the world more than God, he is to be made over to eternal punishments.

(GREG., hom. 3 in Ezech.) Whereas the active life and the contemplative are ours of God's grace, etc., as far as where with an awl the ear of him that refuses liberty is commanded to be bored through.

VERS. 19.-- Of the firstlings. (AUG., quaest. 23.) He says firstborn, etc., as far as because he alone is of the substance of the Father, and equal and coeternal.

Thou shalt sanctify to the Lord thy God. Not to thyself, but to the bestower of all things: Because every best gift is from above 467B(Jac. I). But whatever is light in our thought, or spotted in our manner of life, or lame in the going forward of our works, or blind through ignorance of mind, or in any part deformed or feeble, we ought not to immolate, that is, to impute, to God, but to ourselves.

Thou shalt not work. (GREG., lib. VIII Moral., cap. 34.) To plough with the firstling of the ox, etc., as far as the deceived mind may not perceive the evils that are in them.

VERS. 16.-- But if he shall have. There are certain works which, although they please God less, yet profit the neighbour; and if they cannot be offered to God, they can be eaten. For although they please God less because of the darkened intention which God beholds, yet they help the neighbours by example. As the roe and the hart. The roe goes 467Cby leaping; the hart is the enemy of the serpent, and, leaping over rough and thorny places, it climbs the mountains; and therefore they are taken in a good part.

CAPUT XVI.

VERS. 1.-- Observa mensem novarum frugum. Quia in eo manipuli spicarum, id est primitiae, per sacerdotem offerebantur Domino.

Mensem primum, scilicet Hebraeorum qui apud eos nisan apud Graecos quoque ξάνθικος, apud Latinos Aprilis dicitur, in quo agnus paschalis occidebatur.

Eduxit te. Mystice. Nos quoque de Aegypto hujus mundi liberati, novum hominem induti, in novitate 467Dvitae ambulemus, primitias bonorum operum consecrantes, et phase, id est, transitum bonum celebrantes, ut, relicta Aegypto, ad terram promissionis veniamus.

VERS. 2.-- Et de bobus in loco. (AUG., quaest. 24.) Boves addit, cum de ove tantum praeceperit, quam jussit accipi ex ovibus et haedis vel capris, vel propter Christum, qui secundum carnem ex justis fuit et peccatoribus. Non enim ait ex ovibus vel capris, sed ex ovibus et capris, licet proprie non intelligatur ovis ex capris, ne Judaei dicerent subaudiendum esse caprum. Quare ergo additi sunt boves? an propter alia sacrificia, quae diebus azymorum sunt immolanda?

468AVERS. 3.-- Panem fermentatum. Corruptam doctrinam vel veterem conversationem, unde: Epulemur in azymis, non in fermento veteri, etc. (I Cor. V).

Septem. Toto tempore praesentis vitae. Sicut illi septem diebus azyma comedebant, sic nos pure et simpliciter septem diebus hujus vitae conversemur, quibus quotidie agnus occiditur, et pascha celebratur, si innovati nihil ex veteri corruptione retinemus. Quid enim est aliud fermentum, quam naturae corruptio? Fermentatum enim recedit a naturali dulcedine, et corrumpitur adulterino rancore.

VERS. 4.-- Non apparebit. Epulemur non in fermento veteri, neque in fermento malitiae et nequitiae, etc. (I Cor. V), quasi: relinquite veterem hominem, et induite novum.

468BNon remanebit. In hac vita omnia sacramenta Dominicae incarnationis perscrutanda, et cuncta praecepta ejus implenda sunt (Ephes. IV), antequam mane, id est finis saeculi, adveniat.

VERS. 5.-- Non poteris. In una domo agnus paschalis editur, et in uno loco phase immolatur, id est in catholica Ecclesia: extra illam non recte pascha celebratur. Vetat celebrare mysteria Christi in conventiculis haereticorum.

VERS. 6.-- Vespere ad solis. Novissima aetate mundi, quando verus Sol in passione occubuit, et Aegyptiacam captivitatem solvit, cum pascha nostrum immolatus est Christus.

VERS. 7.-- Vades in tabernacula. Peracto servitio Domini mane resurrectionis transibimus in aeterna 468Ctabernacula, ut quiescamus ab omni labore, laetantes de percepta retributione.

VERS. 8.-- Die septima. Aeterna requie, quam septenarius significat: propter sabbatum ubi nullus labor, nulla operandi ratio.

VERS. 9.-- Septem hebdomadas. In Levitico plenius. Numerabis ergo ab altero die sabbati septem hebdomadas plenas ad alteram diem explorationis hebdomadae septimae.

(AUG., quaest. 25.) Septem hebdomadas, etc. Si ab universo populo haec Pentecoste jussa est observari, nunquid omnes credendum est uno die falcem jussos mittere in messem? Si autem sibi quisque observat istam quinquagesimam, dinumerans ab illo die quo falcem mittit, non una est universo populo. Illa vero 468Duna est quae computatur ab immolatione paschae usque in diem datae legis in Sina.

Nos quoque celebramus Pentecosten, id est, adventum Spiritus sancti, qui est digitus Dei, quo scripta est lex in tabulis.

VERS. 13.-- Solemnitatem. Qui ut tabernaculo, praesenti mundo utitur, festivitatem agere, et laetari in eo potest, propter futuri exspectationem: Quia si terrena domus hujus habitationis dissolvatur, habebit domum non manufactam in coelis (I Cor. V).

Quando. Consummata perfectione omnium bonorum et multarum collectione virtutum, laetandum et exsultandum esse cum amicis significat. Cum exsultabit quisque de percepta retributione qui hic largus 469Afuerit in munere, tunc vere tabernaculorum solemnitas agetur, non mutabilium scilicet, sed aeternorum, unde: Ut recipiant vos in aeterna tabernacula (Luc. XVI).

VERS. 16.-- Masculinum. Non femineum; nihil enim molle, fragile et infirmum in nostra conversatione debet esse: fortia quaeque a nobis exigit Deus (Exod. XXIII, XXXIV; Eccli. XXXV). Si autem in fide Trinitatis Patris et Filii et Spiritus sancti aeternae mercedis respectu tempore praesentis vitae bonis operibus insudamus, masculum in conspectu Domini statuimus.

In omnibus. Ut advenientes undique statim in introitu, paratos inveniant, a quibus justa judicia accipiant.

469BVERS. 19.-- Oculos sapientum. Apud homines, non apud Deum, quibus dicitur: Vae qui sapientes estis in oculis vestris (Isa. V).

VERS. 20.-- Juste. (GREG., lib. X Moral., cap. 19.) Injuste quod justum est exsequitur, qui ad defensionem justitiae non virtutis aemulatione, sed amore praemii temporalis excitatur, et justitiam quam praetendit, vendere non veretur. Juste ergo justum exsequi est in assertione justitiae ipsam justitiam quaerere: Sunt viae quae videntur hominibus justae, etc. (Prov. XIV). Et: Vae qui justificatis vos coram hominibus (Luc. XVI).

VERS. 21.-- Non plantabis. Occasionem idololatriae amputat. Solebant enim gentes immolare, et in delubris statuas adorare.

469CLucum. (ISID.) Nemus, frondentes arbores infructuosae, etc., usque ad nec permittit fictionem falsitatis vel erroris veritati catholicae sociare.

CHAPTER XVI.

VERS. 1.-- Observe the month of the new corn. Because in it the handfuls of ears, that is, the firstfruits, were offered to the Lord by the priest.

The first month, namely of the Hebrews, which among them is called nisan, among the Greeks also ξάνθικος, among the Latins April, in which the paschal lamb was slain.

He brought thee out. Mystically. Let us too, delivered from the Egypt of this world and clothed with the new man, walk in newness 467Dof life, consecrating the firstfruits of good works, and celebrating the phase, that is, a good passing over, that, Egypt being left behind, we may come to the land of promise.

VERS. 2.-- And of oxen, in the place. (AUG., quaest. 24.) He adds oxen, although he had given command concerning the sheep only, which he bade be taken from the sheep and the kids or the goats — or because of Christ, who according to the flesh was of the just and of sinners. For he does not say, of the sheep or of the goats, but, of the sheep and of the goats, although properly a sheep is not understood to be of the goats, lest the Jews should say that a goat was to be understood. Why then are oxen added? is it because of the other sacrifices, which are to be immolated on the days of the unleavened bread?

468AVERS. 3.-- Leavened bread. Corrupt doctrine, or the old manner of life; whence: Let us feast in the unleavened bread, not in the old leaven, etc. (I Cor. V).

Seven. Through the whole time of the present life. As they ate unleavened bread for seven days, so let us live purely and simply through the seven days of this life, in which daily the lamb is slain and the pasch is celebrated, if, being renewed, we retain nothing of the old corruption. For what else is leaven than the corruption of nature? For what is leavened departs from its natural sweetness, and is corrupted with an adulterine rancidness.

VERS. 4.-- It shall not appear. Let us feast not in the old leaven, nor in the leaven of malice and wickedness, etc. (I Cor. V); as if to say: leave the old man, and put on the new.

468BThere shall not remain. In this life all the sacraments of the Lord's incarnation are to be searched out, and all his precepts are to be fulfilled (Ephes. IV), before the morning, that is, the end of the world, comes.

VERS. 5.-- Thou shalt not be able. In one house the paschal lamb is eaten, and in one place the phase is immolated, that is, in the catholic Church: outside her the pasch is not rightly celebrated. He forbids the mysteries of Christ to be celebrated in the conventicles of heretics.

VERS. 6.-- In the evening at the sun's. In the last age of the world, when the true Sun went down in the passion and loosed the Egyptian captivity, when Christ our pasch was immolated.

VERS. 7.-- Thou shalt go into the tabernacles. The service of the Lord being accomplished, in the morning of the resurrection we shall pass over into the eternal 468Ctabernacles, that we may rest from all labour, rejoicing over the retribution received.

VERS. 8.-- On the seventh day. By the eternal rest, which the number seven signifies: because of the sabbath, where there is no labour, no reckoning of working.

VERS. 9.-- Seven weeks. More fully in Leviticus. Thou shalt number therefore from the morrow after the sabbath seven full weeks, unto the morrow of the searching out of the seventh week.

(AUG., quaest. 25.) Seven weeks, etc. If this Pentecost was commanded to be observed by the whole people, is it to be believed that all were bidden to put the sickle into the harvest on one day? But if each man observes that fiftieth day for himself, numbering from that day on which he puts in the sickle, it is not one for the whole people. But that one 468Dis one which is reckoned from the immolation of the pasch unto the day of the giving of the law on Sina.

We too celebrate Pentecost, that is, the coming of the Holy Spirit, who is the finger of God, by whom the law was written on the tables.

VERS. 13.-- The solemnity. He who uses the present world as a tabernacle can keep festival and rejoice in it, because of the expectation of what is to come: Because if the earthly house of this our habitation be dissolved, he shall have a house not made with hands in heaven (I Cor. V).

When. He signifies that, the perfection of all good things being consummated and many virtues gathered together, there is to be rejoicing and exulting with friends. When each one who here has been bountiful 469Ain giving shall exult over the retribution received, then truly shall the solemnity of tabernacles be kept — not of changeable ones, that is, but of eternal ones; whence: That they may receive you into eternal tabernacles (Luc. XVI).

VERS. 16.-- The male. Not the female; for nothing soft, frail, or feeble ought to be in our manner of life: God requires of us all things strong (Exod. XXIII, XXXIV; Eccli. XXXV). But if, in the faith of the Trinity, of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, with regard for the eternal reward, we sweat at good works in the time of the present life, we set a male in the sight of the Lord.

In all. That they who come from every side may at once, at the entrance, find men prepared, from whom they may receive just judgments.

469BVERS. 19.-- The eyes of the wise. Among men, not with God; to whom it is said: Woe to you that are wise in your own eyes (Isa. V).

VERS. 20.-- Justly. (GREG., lib. X Moral., cap. 19.) He unjustly pursues what is just who is stirred to the defence of justice not by zeal of virtue, but by love of a temporal reward, and does not fear to sell the justice he puts forward. Justly therefore to pursue the just is, in the asserting of justice, to seek justice itself: There are ways which seem to men just, etc. (Prov. XIV). And: Woe to you who justify yourselves before men (Luc. XVI).

VERS. 21.-- Thou shalt not plant. He cuts off the occasion of idolatry. For the nations were wont to immolate, and to adore statues in their shrines.

469CA grove. (ISID.) A wood, leafy unfruitful trees, etc., as far as nor does it permit the feigning of falsehood or of error to be joined to catholic truth.

CAPUT XVII.

VERS. 2.-- Cumque reperti fuerint. Lex errorem et idololatriam maxime detestatur: maximum enim scelus est honorem Creatoris impendere creaturae; quod faciunt gentiles, vel mali catholici, qui, licet ab Ecclesia non recedant, tamen magis inveniuntur per diversa vitia servire diabolo, quam per bona opera militare Deo. Hos lapidibus jubet obrui quos irrevocabilis sententia damnandos esse praevidit.

VERS. 6.-- In ore duorum aut trium testium. Attestatione scilicet legis prophetarum et Evangelii, secundum 469Dillud: Sermo quem locutus est, ipse judicabit eum in novissimo die (Joan. XII). In titulo quoque crucis tres linguae testantur Christum esse regem Judaeorum: Hebraica, Graeca et Latina (Joan. XIX).

Nemo occidatur. Tanta concordia est Veteris ac Novi Testamenti, ut neutrum sine altero sit.

In humano judicio primum divinae Scripturae auctoritas requirenda est.

VERS. 81.-- Si difficile. Sacerdotes Ecclesiae Dei instruit, ut judicia ecclesiastica secundum potestatem sibi a Deo datam reverenter agant, et juste decernant, non ad libitum suum, sed secundum legis decretum, ne suscipiendo personam mutent sententiam.

470AVERS. 11.-- Juxta. Nota, non dicitur tibi, ut obedias, nisi juxta legem docuerint, unde: Supra cathedram Moysi sederunt scribae et pharisaei (Matth. XXIII).

VERS. 12.-- Sacerdotis imperio. Christi qui est sacerdos in aeternum secundum ordinem Melchisedech, qui vicarios sibi substituit, quibus ait: Qui vos audit, me audit; et qui vos spernit, me spernit, etc. (Luc. X). Jure ergo damnationis sustinet sententiam, qui contemnit divinitatis potentiam.

VERS. 14.-- Cum ingressus fueris terram, etc. (AUG., quaest. 26.) Quaeri potest cur displicuit populus Deo, cum regem desideravit, cum hic inveniatur esse permissus? Sed intelligendum est merito non fuisse secundum voluntatem Dei, quia hoc fieri non praecepit, sed desiderantibus permisit. Verumtamen praecepit 470Bne fieret alienus, sed frater ex eodem populo indigena non alienigena. Quod autem ait: Non poteris: intelligendum est, non debebis.

Constituam super. Habitatores terrae constituunt sibi regem contra Dei sententiam.

VERS. 17.-- Non habebit, etc. (AUG., quaest. 27.) Manifestum est Salomonem hoc praeceptum transisse, etc., usque ad perveniat ad alienigenas.

Neque argenti. Nolite thesaurizare vobis thesauros in terra (Matth. VI).

VERS. 19.-- Legetque. Nota quanta assiduitate legere debent sacerdotes, cum assidue legant reges. Lectio ipsa lux est et vita, unde: Verba, quae ego loquor vobis, spiritus et vita sunt (Joan. VI).

VERS. 20.-- Superbiam. Quasi, sciat se esse fratrem. 470CUnum patrem Deum omnes habemus, cui dicimus: Pater noster, qui es in coelis, etc. (Luc. XI).

Superbiam. Benedictio est regnare super Israel, scilicet regnando facere Israel, scilicet Deum videntes.

CHAPTER XVII.

VERS. 2.-- And when there shall be found. The law above all detests error and idolatry: for it is the greatest wickedness to pay to a creature the honour of the Creator; which the Gentiles do, or bad catholics, who, although they do not withdraw from the Church, yet are found rather to serve the devil through divers vices than to war for God through good works. These he bids to be overwhelmed with stones, whom an irrevocable sentence has foreseen to be condemned.

VERS. 6.-- In the mouth of two or three witnesses. By the attestation, namely, of the law, of the prophets, and of the Gospel, according to 469Dthat: The word which he hath spoken, the same shall judge him in the last day (Joan. XII). In the title of the cross too three tongues bear witness that Christ is the king of the Jews: the Hebrew, the Greek, and the Latin (Joan. XIX).

Let no man be put to death. So great is the concord of the Old and the New Testament, that neither is without the other.

In human judgment the authority of Holy Scripture is first to be sought.

VERS. 81.-- If difficult. He instructs the priests of the Church of God, that they may conduct ecclesiastical judgments reverently according to the power given them by God, and may decree justly, not at their own pleasure, but according to the decree of the law, lest by accepting a person they change the sentence.

470AVERS. 11.-- According to. Note, it is not said to thee that thou shouldst obey, unless they have taught according to the law; whence: Upon the chair of Moses have sitten the scribes and the Pharisees (Matth. XXIII).

VERS. 12.-- At the command of the priest. Of Christ, who is a priest for ever according to the order of Melchisedech, who put vicars in his own stead, to whom he says: He that heareth you heareth me; and he that despiseth you despiseth me, etc. (Luc. X). By right therefore he undergoes the sentence of damnation who contemns the power of the Godhead.

VERS. 14.-- When thou shalt have entered into the land, etc. (AUG., quaest. 26.) It can be asked: why did the people displease God when it desired a king, seeing that here it is found to have been permitted? But it is to be understood that deservedly it was not according to the will of God, because he did not command this to be done, but permitted it to them that desired it. Nevertheless he commanded 470Bthat no stranger should be made king, but a brother out of that same people, a native, not one of another race. But as to what he says: Thou shalt not be able: it is to be understood, thou oughtest not.

I will set over. The inhabitants of the land set a king over themselves against the sentence of God.

VERS. 17.-- He shall not have, etc. (AUG., quaest. 27.) It is manifest that Solomon transgressed this precept, etc., as far as it may come to men of another race.

Nor of silver. Lay not up to yourselves treasures on earth (Matth. VI).

VERS. 19.-- And he shall read. Note with how great assiduity priests ought to read, since kings read assiduously. Reading itself is light and life; whence: The words which I speak to you are spirit and life (Joan. VI).

VERS. 20.-- Pride. As if to say: let him know that he is a brother. 470CWe all have one Father, God, to whom we say: Our Father, who art in heaven, etc. (Luc. XI).

Pride. It is a blessing to reign over Israel, that is, by reigning to make Israel, that is, men seeing God.

CAPUT XVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Non habebunt. Et in Veteri et in Novo Testamento ministris altaris praeceptum est de oblationibus et decimis vivere, nec terrenis possessionibus acquirendis concessum est inhiare: Unde: Nolite possidere aurum neque argentum, etc. (Matth. X); et post pauca: Dignus est enim operarius cibo suo (I Cor. IX.) Et alibi: Qui altario serviunt cum altario participant (Num. XVIII). Non enim oportet, ut 470Dqui semper astare debet officio divino, inhiet terreno lucro, cui Deus haereditas est. Quid ergo deesse potest illi qui omnia habentem habet.

VERS. 2.-- Haereditas. Pro operatione bona, ut quod mente conceperit, opere probet, unde: Coepit Jesus facere et docere (Act. I).

VERS. 3.-- Dabunt. Quia in corporalibus solatiis et spiritualibus emolumentis devote doctoribus nostris communicare debemus, unde: Communicet is qui catechizatur verbo, ei qui se catechizat, in omni opere bono (Gal. VI).

Ventriculum. Venter receptaculum ciborum, omnes hominum labores, et momentanea gulae blandimenta sunt, quorum finem condemnat: et mentibus 471ADeo sacratis ostendit totum quod voramus in successum projici, unde: Esca ventri, et venter escis: Deus autem et hunc et hanc destruet.

VERS. 4.-- Primitias. Primitias frugum laborantem agricolam oportet primum de fructibus accipere.

VERS. 6.-- Si exierit. Quasi urbem et domum deserit, qui gratis Deo servit, et sine sumptu Evangelium ponit.

VERS. 8.-- Excepto. (AUG., quaest. 38.) Quod LXX, etc., usque ad eum servari oportet quod parentibus exhibitum est.

VERS. 9.-- Quando ingressus. (AUG., quaest. 29.) Quoniam portentorum inspectores interdicuntur, etc., usque ad sic illae inspectiones prodigorum a significationibus miraculorum discernendae sunt.

471BVERS. 10.-- Ariolos. Qui circumeunt aras nefandis precibus, et divinationem in extis animalium quaerunt.

Auguria. Quasi avigeria, quae in vocibus avium gerantur. Vel avigaria, ab avium garritu.

VERS. 11.-- Pythones. Ventriloquos, de quorum ventre daemones loquuntur, a Pythone sic dictos, id est Apolline, quem deum divinationis credebant esse.

VERS. 15.-- Prophetam de. Licet historialiter de prophetis accipi possit, qui post Moysen in populo Israel repleti sunt Spiritu Dei, melius tamen de Domino prophetarum accipitur, de quo turbae ab ipso satiatae dixerunt: Hic est vere propheta qui venturus est in mundum (Joan. VI). Et alibi: Propheta magnus 471Csurrexit in nobis (Luc. VII).

CHAPTER XVIII.

VERS. 1.-- They shall not have. Both in the Old and in the New Testament it is enjoined upon the ministers of the altar to live from the oblations and the tithes, nor is it granted them to gape after the acquiring of earthly possessions. Whence: Do not possess gold nor silver, etc. (Matth. X); and a little after: For the workman is worthy of his food (I Cor. IX.) And elsewhere: They who serve the altar partake with the altar (Num. XVIII). For it is not fitting that 470Dhe who ought always to stand by the divine office should gape after earthly gain, whose inheritance God is. What therefore can be lacking to him who has him that has all things.

VERS. 2.-- Inheritance. For good working, that what he has conceived in mind he may prove in deed; whence: Jesus began to do and to teach (Act. I).

VERS. 3.-- They shall give. Because we ought devoutly to communicate with our teachers in bodily comforts and in spiritual profits; whence: Let him that is catechized in the word communicate to him that catechizeth him, in every good work (Gal. VI).

The maw. The belly is the receptacle of foods; all the labours of men and the momentary flatteries of the gullet are such, and their end he condemns; and to minds 471Aconsecrated to God he shows that the whole of what we devour is cast forth into the outcome; whence: Meat for the belly, and the belly for meats: but God shall destroy both the one and the other.

VERS. 4.-- Firstfruits. It behoves the labouring husbandman to receive first, out of the fruits, the firstfruits of the crops.

VERS. 6.-- If he shall go forth. He as it were forsakes city and house who serves God freely, and sets forth the Gospel without charge.

VERS. 8.-- Except. (AUG., quaest. 38.) What the LXX, etc., as far as that ought to be kept for him which was rendered to his parents.

VERS. 9.-- When thou art entered in. (AUG., quaest. 29.) Since inspectors of portents are forbidden, etc., as far as thus those inspections of the prodigal are to be distinguished from the significations of miracles.

471BVERS. 10.-- Soothsayers. They who go about the altars with unspeakable prayers, and seek divination in the entrails of animals.

Auguries. As it were avigeria, things which are carried on (gerantur) in the voices of birds. Or avigaria, from the chattering (garritus) of birds.

VERS. 11.-- Pythons. Ventriloquists, out of whose belly demons speak, so called from Python, that is, from Apollo, whom they believed to be the god of divination.

VERS. 15.-- A prophet of. Although it may be taken historically of the prophets who after Moses were filled with the Spirit of God among the people of Israel, yet it is better taken of the Lord of the prophets, of whom the crowds filled by him said: This is of a truth the prophet that is to come into the world (Joan. VI). And elsewhere: A great prophet 471Cis risen up among us (Luc. VII).

CAPUT XIX.

VERS. 2.-- Tres. Fidem, spem et charitatem, ad quas omnes poenitentes debent confugere.

Tres civitates refugii ultra Jordanem constituit Moyses homicidis qui non sponte homicidium commiserunt, alias tres circa Jordanem jubet separari (Num. XXXV; Josue. XX), mystice insinuans quod ante perceptionem baptismi, qui vult a peccato salvari et ab hostibus spiritualibus liberari, sanctae Trinitatis fidem debet sincera mente confiteri, et post baptismum in fide, spe et charitate viriliter operari. Sicque religiose conversans in mundo, qui senario conditus est numero, post perfectionem bonorum 471Doperum requiem aeternam exspectat in sabbatismo.

VERS. 3.-- In tres. Ut qui salvandus est facilius evadat. Praedicatores quoque verbo et exemplo debent peccatoribus consulere et aequaliter in tota terra Ecclesiae fidem Trinitatis omnibus insinuare, ut viam veritatis et portam fidei agnoscant patere sibi, et se merito damnandos si intrare noluerint.

Sed abiisse. Exemplo enim ostendit qualis homicida salvandus sit.

(GREG., Past. part. II, cap. X, tom. III.) Ad silvam cum amico imus, etc., usque ad quae non malitiose perpetratur.

(AMBR., lib. de Fuga saeculi, cap. 2.) Congrue 472Aprovisum liquet, etc., usque ad percussus Levitico gladio moriatur sensus carnis, ut vivat anima vestra.

VERS. 9.-- Addes tibi. In libro Jesu Nave, sex civitates refugii decernuntur talibus homicidis (Josue XX). Cades in Galilaea montis Nepthali, Sichem in monte Ephraim, et Cariatharbe, quae est Hebron, in monte Juda. Et trans Jordanem Bosor de tribu Ruben, et Ramoth in Galaad de tribu Gad, et Gaulon in Basan, de tribu Manasse.

(AMBR., ubi supra.) Sex autem civitatum refugia sunt, etc., usque ad vel interdictorum declinatione veneremur.

VERS. 11.-- Si quis autem odio habens proximum. Qui malitia aliquem percutit, et in mortem animae ducit, reus est aeternae mortis, unde: Si quis scandalizaverit unum de pusillis qui in me credunt, etc. 472BEt alibi: Qui odit fratrem homicida est, et non habebit vitam aeternam (Matth. XVIII; I Joan. III). Hic ab altari divellitur, tanquam sacramentis Dominicis indignus, nec veniam nisi per condignam poenitentiam promeretur. Qui autem non sponte occidit, habet urbes refugii, Ecclesiam scilicet catholicam, ubi se angustia poenitentiae coercens, per omne tempus praesentis vitae bonis operibus studeat, et si spem in morte summi pontificis, scilicet Redemptoris sui, posuerit, tandem merebitur salvari.

Qui fraternum odium retinet in corde, non potest fructuosam poenitentiam agere, nec Deum sibi placare.

VERS. 14.-- Non assumes et transferes terminos, etc. Unde: Ne transgrediaris terminos quos posuerunt 472Cpatres tui (Prov. XXII), id est, praecedentium patrum regulas et definitiones vivendi.

VERS. 15.-- Non stabit testis unus. Hoc etiam historialiter servare debemus. Et contra impios vel haereticos, cum testimoniis Scripturarum indigemus, duos testes, id est, Vetus et Novum Testamentum adhibemus, vel tres, id est, Evangelium, prophetas, apostolum, et sic stat verbum.

VERS. 21.-- Animam pro anima. Qui enim animam errare fecit, venia dignus non est.

Dentem pro dente (Exod. XXI; Lev. XXIV; Matth. V). Verborum virtutem. Dentes enim verba significant secundum illud: Filii hominum, dentes eorum arma (Psal. LVI). Et alibi: Dentes ejus lacte, id est 472Dlege, candidiores (Gen. XXIX).

Manum, etc. Operationem, ut ea, scilicet. sunt ei utilia aut nociva quae in alio pervertit.

CHAPTER XIX.

VERS. 2.-- Three. Faith, hope and charity, to which all penitents ought to flee.

Moses appointed three cities of refuge beyond the Jordan for homicides who committed homicide not willingly; three others about the Jordan he bids to be set apart (Num. XXXV; Josue. XX), mystically intimating that before the receiving of baptism he who wishes to be saved from sin and to be freed from spiritual enemies ought with sincere mind to confess the faith of the holy Trinity, and after baptism to work manfully in faith, hope and charity. And thus living religiously in the world, which was founded in the number six, after the perfection of good 471Dworks he awaits eternal rest in the sabbath-rest.

VERS. 3.-- Into three. That he who is to be saved may escape the more easily. Preachers also ought by word and by example to take counsel for sinners, and equally in the whole earth of the Church to intimate to all the faith of the Trinity, that they may acknowledge the way of truth and the gate of faith to lie open to them, and themselves to be deservedly damned if they should be unwilling to enter.

But to have gone away. For by an example he shows what kind of homicide is to be saved.

(GREG., Past. part. II, cap. X, tom. III.) We go to the wood with a friend, etc., as far as which is not perpetrated maliciously.

(AMBR., lib. de Fuga saeculi, cap. 2.) It is plain that provision was fittingly 472Amade, etc., as far as struck with the Levitical sword let the sense of the flesh die, that your soul may live.

VERS. 9.-- Thou shalt add to thee. In the book of Joshua the son of Nun six cities of refuge are decreed for such homicides (Josue XX). Cades in Galilee of mount Nephtali, Sichem in mount Ephraim, and Cariatharbe, which is Hebron, in mount Juda. And beyond the Jordan, Bosor of the tribe of Ruben, and Ramoth in Galaad of the tribe of Gad, and Gaulon in Basan, of the tribe of Manasse.

(AMBR., ubi supra.) Now there are refuges of six cities, etc., as far as or let us venerate them by the declining of things forbidden.

VERS. 11.-- But if any man hating his neighbour. He who out of malice strikes anyone, and leads him into the death of the soul, is guilty of eternal death; whence: If any man shall scandalize one of these little ones that believe in me, etc. 472BAnd elsewhere: He that hateth his brother is a murderer, and shall not have eternal life (Matth. XVIII; I Joan. III). This man is torn away from the altar, as unworthy of the Lord's sacraments, nor does he earn pardon save through worthy penance. But he who kills not willingly has cities of refuge, namely the Catholic Church, where, constraining himself with the strait of penance, let him give himself to good works through the whole time of the present life; and if he shall have placed his hope in the death of the high priest, that is, of his Redeemer, he shall at length deserve to be saved.

He who keeps fraternal hatred in his heart cannot do fruitful penance, nor appease God toward himself.

VERS. 14.-- Thou shalt not take and remove thy neighbour's landmarks, etc. Whence: Pass not beyond the bounds which thy fathers 472Chave set (Prov. XXII), that is, the rules and the definitions of living of the fathers who went before.

VERS. 15.-- One witness shall not stand. This too we ought to keep historically. And against the impious or heretics, when we have need of the testimonies of the Scriptures, we bring forward two witnesses, that is, the Old and the New Testament; or three, that is, the Gospel, the prophets, the apostle — and so the word stands.

VERS. 21.-- Life for life. For he who has made a soul to err is not worthy of pardon.

Tooth for tooth (Exod. XXI; Lev. XXIV; Matth. V). The force of words. For teeth signify words, according to that: The sons of men, their teeth are weapons (Psal. LVI). And elsewhere: His teeth than milk, that is, 472Dthan the law, are whiter (Gen. XXIX).

A hand, etc. Working: that those things, namely. are useful or hurtful to him which he perverts in another.

CAPUT XX.

VERS. 1.-- Quia Dominus. Mystice. (AUG., quaest. 30.) LXX: Quoniam Dominus, etc., usque ad ut etiam ipsos acturos ostenderet.

VERS. 5.-- Quis est homo. (ID., quaest. 31.) Non est melior in bello qui jam aedificia dedicavit, etc., usque ad sed haec instituta sunt propter virorum animos explorandos.

Quis est homo. (ISID.) Docet non posse quemquam professionem contemplationis vel militiae spiritalis 473Aarripere, etc., usque ad: ut ei placeat cui se probavit (II Tim. II).

Melius est professionem vel persecutionem devitare, quam in ea succumbere. Ad disputationem quoque contra haereticos venire non debet, nisi doctus et perfectus, ne scandalizet alios superatus.

VERS. 16.-- Nullum omnino permittes. Nulli vitio in nostra conversatione parcendum, ne coram oculis Domini voluptatibus corrupti et abominabiles appareamus, et exsultantes inimici de ruina nostra dicant: Euge, euge animae nostrae, devorabimus eum (Psal. XXXIV).

CHAPTER XX.

VERS. 1.-- Because the Lord. Mystically. (AUG., quaest. 30.) The LXX: Since the Lord, etc., as far as that he might show that they themselves also were about to act.

VERS. 5.-- What man is. (ID., quaest. 31.) He is not the better in war who has already dedicated buildings, etc., as far as but these things were instituted for the trying of the minds of men.

What man is. (ISID.) He teaches that no one can take up the profession of contemplation or of spiritual 473Awarfare, etc., as far as: that he may please him to whom he has approved himself (II Tim. II).

It is better to shun a profession or a persecution than to succumb in it. To a disputation also against heretics one ought not to come, unless learned and perfect, lest, being overcome, he scandalize others.

VERS. 16.-- Thou shalt permit none at all. No vice is to be spared in our manner of life, lest before the eyes of the Lord we appear corrupted by pleasures and abominable, and our enemies, exulting over our ruin, say: Well done, well done to our soul; we will devour him (Psal. XXXIV).

CAPUT XXI.

VERS. 1.-- Quando inventum, etc. Cadaver, corpus 473Bsine anima, et anima sine Deo, qui est ejus vita. In terra quam Dominus Deus tuus daturus est tibi, hoc est in Ecclesia. Caedis reus, actor seditionis; Majores natu, vita et sapientia majores. Judices, id est sacerdotes, quorum officium est de singulis judicare, id est, discernere. Metientur a loco cadaveris, id est, cum summa discretione investigabunt actorem seditionis. Tollent vitulam de armento, id est, carnem Christi, quam de patribus sumpsit. Quae non traxit jugum, quia peccatum non fecit, nec inventus est dolus in ore ejus. Nec terram scidit vomere (I Petr. II), nullam scilicet seditionis maculam admisit.

Et ignorabitur caedis reus. Diabolus qui invisibilis, et ideo ignorari dicitur, scitur enim quia misit in cor, ut traderet eum Judas (Joan. XIII).

473CMajores natu, etc. Apostoli et doctores qui Scripturae attestatione perceperunt nullam nationem sic promptam ad caedem ut Judaeorum gentem, qui et prophetarum et Domini sanguinem fuderunt.

VERS. 4.-- Et ducent eam ad vallem asperam, etc. Mortem scilicet despectam et turpissimam, unde: Morte turpissima condemnemus eum. Quae nunquam arata est (Sap. II), quia mors Christi absque culpa fuit. Nec sementem recepit (Matth. XIII), id est, zizania peccati, quae inimicus homo superseminavit. Possumus per vallem asperam gentilitatem accipere, quae aspera erat; saxosa, quia saxa adorabat; in qua caesa est cervix vitulae, quia pro salute gentium mors Christi celebrata est. Cervix robur carnis quod in morte deposuit. Lavabunt manus super vitulam, 473Did est, purgabunt opera per passionis Christi confidentiam.

VERS. 6.-- Lavabuntque manus suas, etc. Id est, opera sua in passione Christi demonstrabunt esse munda. Licet enim ejusdem passionis sacramenta quotidie iterentur, ab omni tamen fraude Judaeorum corda et opera habent mundissima.

VERS. 8.-- Propitius esto. Pro officio suo benedicunt populo, ut non reddatur vindicta pro scelere, sed salus pro redemptione.

VERS. 10.-- Si egressus fuerit ad pugnam. (ISID.) Dicant Judaei, quomodo haec servent? quid causae sit mulierem decalvari et ungues circumcidi? quid si sine capillis et unguibus inventa fuerit?

474AVERS. 11.-- Mulierem pulchram. Sicut Paulus qui de Menandro sumpsit: Corrumpunt bonos mores colloquia mala (I Cor. XV). Et de Arato: In ipso vivimus, movemur, et sumus: ipsius enim et genus sumus (Act. XVII).

Alii putant hanc mulierem specie decoram significare rationabilem disciplinam apud Gentiles inventam: ab ea enim oportet recidi omnem superstitionis immunditiam, ut ad studium veritatis assumatur. Nihil enim mundum habent disciplinae gentilium, quia nulla apud eos disciplina est, cui non sit aliquid immunditiae vel superstitionis admistum.

VERS. 13.-- Uno mense, id est, triginta diebus, ut post fidem Trinitatis et opus legis, mereatur Ecclesiae sociari; ter enim decem triginta efficiunt.

474BEt postea. Cum nec in capite, id est sensibus, nec in manibus, id est operibus, aliquid superfluum aut immundum erit.

VERS. 15.-- Si habuerit homo uxores duas, etc. Christus sibi duas uxores, populum scilicet Judaeorum et gentium, conjugii copulavit nomine.
