Odiosa est synagoga, semper contentiosa, dura cervice, incircumcisa corde; dilecta gentilis Ecclesia. Sed non potest filium dilectae praeferre filio odiosae, id est Ecclesiam de gentibus et filios ejus praeferre apostolis et aliis primitivis, qui sunt filii synagogae. Agnoscit odiosae primogenitum, dat ei duplicia, quia primitivis synagogae filiis dedit legem et prophetas, et notitiam nominis sui, et primo eis Evangelium praedicavit. Hic enim est primogenitus liberorum ejus.

474CUnam dilectam. Animalitatem, quae dulcis videtur et delectabilis: omnia voluptuosa cupit et nullo moerore afficit.

Alteram odiosam. Rationalitatem, quae non indulget corpori, nec servit voluptati, sed abdicat occulta dedecoris.

VERS. 16.-- Primogenitum. Apostoli et apostolici viri, qui locum primogeniti obtinent, quorum Ecclesia sequitur auctoritatem.

VERS. 17.-- Sed filium. Hoc ad litteram faciendum est, ne sequatur quis animi sui motum, vel amorem, vel odium, sed naturam.

Cuncta duplicia. Apostoli cuncta duplicia susceperunt, qui doctrina et operatione praediti sunt.

Alii dabit ut vivat, non ut abundet, sicut Abraham 474Dfilium ancillae super nationes praefecit: filio Sarae, non solum temporalia, sed spiritualia accumulavit (Gen. XII).

VERS. 18.-- Si genuerit. Melior est obedientia quam victimae, et auscultare magis quam offerre adipem arietum, quoniam quasi peccatum ariolandi est repugnare, etc. (I Reg. XV.) Inobedientem ergo filium et vitiosum Moyses jubet obrui lapidibus: Evangelium quoque duris increpationibus, quasi lapidibus tales arguit: Genimina viperarum, quis docebit vos fugere a ventura ira (Luc. III)?

VERS. 22.-- Appensus. Cruciatum ejus vidente populo, unde: Peccantes coram omnibus argue, ut caeteri metum habeant (II Tim. V).

475AVERS. 23.-- Non permanebit. Hoc est non debet relinqui in eadem damnatione, sed revocari doctorum exhortatione, ut nulli, dum vivit, reconciliatio negetur.

Non permanebit. Non est de die in diem differenda poenitentia, sed statim agenda, ut per poenitentiam infructuosam ab oculis Dei abscondantur delicta, unde: Beati quorum tecta sunt peccata (Psal. XXXI).

Maledictus a Deo. (AUG., lib. XIV contra Faustum.) Christus nos redemit de maledicto legis, factus pro nobis maledictum (Gal. III). (Adam namque in ligno praevaricationis corruit: ideo sibi et posteris suis maledictionem meruit, de qua dicit: Maledictus omnis qui pendet in ligno.) Maledictam namque, id est, exsecrabilem et turpissimam mortem suscepit.

475B(ID., ubi supra, cap. 3, 4.) Maledictus a Deo est qui pendet, etc. Quidam haeretici ad calumniam passionis Christi hanc sententiam transferunt, etc., usque ad bene ergo maledictum dicitur, quod odit Deus, hoc pependit in ligno.

Contaminabis. Poenitentiae lacrymis fetorem peccatorum abluere debemus, ne aliis scandalum et nobis germinet cruciatum.

CHAPTER XXI.

VERS. 1.-- When there shall be found, etc. A carcass, a body 473Bwithout a soul, and a soul without God, who is its life. In the land which the Lord thy God will give thee, that is, in the Church. Guilty of the slaughter, the author of sedition; The elders, those greater in life and in wisdom. The judges, that is, the priests, whose office it is to judge concerning individuals, that is, to discern. They shall measure from the place of the carcass, that is, with the utmost discretion they shall investigate the author of the sedition. They shall take a heifer of the herd, that is, the flesh of Christ, which he took from the fathers. Which hath not drawn the yoke, because he did no sin, neither was guile found in his mouth. Nor hath it cut the earth with the ploughshare (I Petr. II), that is, it admitted no stain of sedition.

And the guilty of the slaughter shall be unknown. The devil, who is invisible and is therefore said to be unknown; for it is known that he put it into the heart of Judas to betray him (Joan. XIII).

473CThe elders, etc. The apostles and the teachers who by the attestation of Scripture have perceived that no nation was so ready for slaughter as the people of the Jews, who shed the blood both of the prophets and of the Lord.

VERS. 4.-- And they shall lead her to a rough valley, etc. Namely to a despised and most shameful death; whence: Let us condemn him to a most shameful death. Which hath never been ploughed (Sap. II), because the death of Christ was without fault. Nor hath it received seed (Matth. XIII), that is, the cockle of sin, which the enemy man oversowed. We can take the rough valley as the Gentile world, which was rough; stony, because it adored stones; in which the neck of the heifer was struck off, because for the salvation of the Gentiles the death of Christ was celebrated. The neck is the strength of the flesh, which he laid down in death. They shall wash their hands over the heifer, 473Dthat is, they shall purge their works through confidence in the passion of Christ.

VERS. 6.-- And they shall wash their hands, etc. That is, they shall show their works to be clean in the passion of Christ. For although the sacraments of that same passion are repeated daily, yet from all the fraud of the Jews they have hearts and works most clean.

VERS. 8.-- Be merciful. According to their office they bless the people, that vengeance be not rendered for the crime, but salvation for the redemption.

VERS. 10.-- If he shall have gone forth to battle. (ISID.) Let the Jews say, how do they keep these things? what is the reason that the woman is shaved and her nails pared? what if she be found without hair and without nails?

474AVERS. 11.-- A beautiful woman. As Paul, who took from Menander: Evil communications corrupt good manners (I Cor. XV). And from Aratus: In him we live, and move, and are: for we are also his offspring (Act. XVII).

Others think that this woman comely in appearance signifies the reasonable discipline found among the Gentiles: for from her must be cut away every uncleanness of superstition, that she may be taken up unto the study of truth. For the disciplines of the Gentiles have nothing clean, because there is no discipline among them to which somewhat of uncleanness or of superstition is not mingled.

VERS. 13.-- One month, that is, thirty days, that after the faith of the Trinity and the work of the law she may deserve to be joined to the Church; for thrice ten make thirty.

474BAnd afterwards. When neither in the head, that is, in the senses, nor in the hands, that is, in the works, will there be anything superfluous or unclean.

VERS. 15.-- If a man have two wives, etc. Christ joined to himself two wives, namely the people of the Jews and of the Gentiles, under the name of wedlock. The synagogue is hateful, ever contentious, stiff-necked, uncircumcised in heart; the Gentile Church is beloved. But he cannot prefer the son of the beloved to the son of the hateful, that is, prefer the Church of the Gentiles and her sons to the apostles and the other first men, who are the sons of the synagogue. He acknowledges the firstborn of the hateful, he gives him a double portion, because to the first sons of the synagogue he gave the law and the prophets, and the knowledge of his name, and to them first he preached the Gospel. For he is the firstborn of his children.

474COne beloved. Animality, which seems sweet and delightful: it desires all voluptuous things and afflicts with no sorrow.

The other hateful. Rationality, which does not indulge the body, nor serve pleasure, but renounces the hidden things of dishonesty.

VERS. 16.-- The firstborn. The apostles and apostolic men, who hold the place of the firstborn, whose authority the Church follows.

VERS. 17.-- But the son. This is to be done to the letter, that no one follow the motion of his own mind, or love, or hatred, but nature.

All things double. The apostles received all things double, who were endowed with doctrine and with working.

To the other he will give that he may live, not that he may abound; as Abraham set the son of the handmaid 474Dover the nations: upon the son of Sara he heaped not only temporal but spiritual things (Gen. XII).

VERS. 18.-- If he beget. Obedience is better than victims, and to hearken rather than to offer the fat of rams; for it is as the sin of divining to rebel, etc. (I Reg. XV.) Moses therefore bids the disobedient and vicious son to be overwhelmed with stones: the Gospel too rebukes such men with hard reproofs, as with stones: Ye offspring of vipers, who shall teach you to flee from the wrath to come (Luc. III)?

VERS. 22.-- Hanged up. His torment, with the people looking on; whence: Them that sin reprove before all, that the rest may have fear (II Tim. V).

475AVERS. 23.-- It shall not remain. That is, he ought not to be left in the same condemnation, but to be called back by the exhortation of teachers, so that reconciliation be denied to none while he lives.

It shall not remain. Penance is not to be deferred from day to day, but to be done at once, that through unfruitful [infructuosam; read fructuosam, "fruitful"] penance offences may be hidden from the eyes of God; whence: Blessed are they whose sins are covered (Psal. XXXI).

Cursed of God. (AUG., lib. XIV contra Faustum.) Christ redeemed us from the curse of the law, being made a curse for us (Gal. III). (For Adam fell in the tree of transgression: therefore he merited a curse for himself and for his posterity, of which he says: Cursed is every one that hangeth on a tree.) For he underwent a cursed, that is, an execrable and most shameful death.

475B(ID., ubi supra, cap. 3, 4.) He that hangeth is cursed of God, etc. Certain heretics transfer this sentence to the calumny of the passion of Christ, etc., as far as well therefore is that called cursed which God hates: this hung upon the tree.

Thou shalt defile. With the tears of penance we ought to wash away the stench of sins, lest it breed scandal for others and torment for ourselves.

CAPUT XXII.

VERS. 1.-- Non videbis. Moraliter. Frater tuus doctor Ecclesiae, cujus discipulum vel doctum, qui significatur per bovem, vel indoctum, qui significatur per ovem, si videris errare, noli negligere, sed exhortare 475Cut fratri, id est, magistro possis reconciliare. Duc in domum tuam (I Cor. XI), id est, adhibe curam. Et si non est propinquus, id est Ecclesiae tuae, sed alterius doctor.

VERS. 3.-- Similiter. Gentili, scilicet, cujus utilitatem et immunditiam non debemus despicere.

Vestimento. Plebe qua ornatur doctor. Ad litteram quoque omnia haec et similia servanda sunt.

VERS. 5.-- Non induetur mulier, etc. (AUG., quaest. 32.) Alia editio, etc., usque ad non debet aliquid femineum vel molle in doctrina sua habere.

Mulier. Cujus naturaliter diversus color, motus incestus, vires, cui non permittitur docere, nec dominari in virum, nec usurpet officium praedicandi (I Cor. XIII).

475DContra naturam est virum muliebria facere, comam crispare, torquere capillos, unde Apostolus: Vir, si comam nutrierit, ignominia est illi. Mulieri autem capilli pro velamine dati sunt; non ergo induetur vir veste feminea, operatione scilicet fluxa et dissoluta.

(RAB.) Per viam imus, etc., usque ad sed ex ea allegoricum sensum in mente retinemus.

VERS. 8.-- Domum novam, id est Ecclesiam.

Murum tecti, id est virtutum custodiam.

Ne effundatur sanguis in domo tua, hoc est ne alio peccante reus tenearis.

(GREG., lib. VIII Moral., cap. 39.) Ne effundatur. Per incuriam atque desidiam. Unde qui virtutes sine 476Ahumilitate congregat, quasi pulverem in ventum portat.

VERS. 9.-- Vineam, doctrinam, vel plebem doctoribus commissam, ubi vinum spiritalis gratiae abundare debet.

Altero semine, id est haeretico. Semen bonum verbum Dei; semen nequam quod inimicus seminavit (Matth. XIII): cavendum ergo ne immisceatur aliquid erroris, et corrumpatur semen Dei.

VERS. 10.-- Non arabis. (ISID.) In bove et asino arat, qui recipit Evangelia cum Judaeorum observantia, quae praecessit in umbra. In bove quoque bene operantium vita, in asino stultorum socordia: quasi, fatuum sapienti in praedicatione non socies, ne per eum qui rem implere non valet ei obsistas qui praevalet. 476BStultus vero et sapiens bene conjunguntur, ut unus praecipiat et alter obediat, non ut aequali potestate verbum Dei annuntient.

Allegorice lineis vestibus lanam vel purpuram miscet, qui inordinate vivit, et profectionibus diversi generis, ut si sanctimonialis habeat ornamenta uxoris, vel uxor gerat speciem virginis. Hoc autem figurabatur in vestibus, quod nunc declaratur in moribus.

VERS. 12.-- Funiculos. Ad differentiam scilicet, sicut signum circumcisionis Israelitas separat a caeteris. Superstitiosi vero magistri captantes auram populi, et lucra ex mulierculis, dilatabant fimbrias et eis alligabant spinas, ut vel ambulantes vel sedentes pungerentur, et sic ad serviendum Deo monerentur, 476Cunde: Dilatant phylacteria sua et magnificant fimbrias.

VERS. 13.-- Si duxerit. (AUG., quaest. 33.) Apparet quomodo subditas viris feminas, etc., usque ad qua fuerat alter plectendus.

VERS. 15.-- Pater et mater ejus. Doctores Ecclesiae qui quasi pater et mater fovent plebem: patres auctoritate, matres pietate.

VERS. 18.-- Et verberabunt. Non mirum, si vir, qui falso accusat, jubetur caedi, et multam reddere; puella vero, si culpabilis sit, lapidari: quia per feminam initium peccati, quae virum seduxit et dupliciter peccavit, prius in Deum, postea in virum, vir tantum in Deum

VERS. 19.-- Centum siclis, etc. Perfectione doctrinae 476Det sollicitudinis, quam impendat subjectae plebi, vel poenitentiae. Centenarius enim perfectus est, quinquagenarius poenitentiae consecratus.

VERS. 23.-- Si puellam virginem desponderit. In hoc capitulo, sicut in duobus praecedentibus et duobus succedentibus, corporalem condemnat fornicationem.

VERS. 26.-- Nihil patietur. Sed ad incoeptam fidem revocabitur per gratiam baptismi liberata ab hostibus.

VERS. 29.-- Non poterit. (AUG., quaest. 34.) Merito quaeritur utrum ista poena sit, ut non eam possit dimittere per omne tempus, quam inordinate et illicite violavit; si enim intelligimus eam non posse, id est non debere dimitti per omne tempus, quia uxor effecta est, occurrit quod Moyses permisit 477Adare libellum repudii et dimittere. In his autem quae illicite vitiant noluit licere, ne videatur ad ludibrium fecisse, et potius finxisse quod eam duxerit. Hoc quoque de illa jussit, cui vir calumniatus fuerit de virginalibus non inventis.

VERS. 30.-- Non. Pater noster Christus est, cujus Ecclesia uxor est; non licet homini dignitatem istius sponsi transferre in se, nec sponsam ejus violare, ut amorem ejus commutet in se, ex dono virtutum vel ex praedicatione laudem suam quaerendo non Dei; cui dicere convenit: Non nobis, Domine, non nobis, sed nomini tuo da gloriam (Psal. CIII).

CHAPTER XXII.

VERS. 1.-- Thou shalt not see. Morally. Thy brother is the teacher of the Church, whose disciple — whether the learned, who is signified by the ox, or the unlearned, who is signified by the sheep — if thou see him err, do not neglect him, but exhort him 475Cthat thou mayest be able to reconcile him to his brother, that is, to his master. Bring him into thy house (I Cor. XI), that is, apply care. And if he be not near, that is, of thy church, but a teacher of another.

VERS. 3.-- In like manner. To the Gentile, namely, whose usefulness and uncleanness we ought not to despise.

With the garment. The people, wherewith the teacher is adorned. To the letter also all these things and the like are to be kept.

VERS. 5.-- A woman shall not be clothed, etc. (AUG., quaest. 32.) Another edition, etc., as far as he ought not to have anything womanish or soft in his teaching.

A woman. Whose colour is naturally diverse, her movement unchaste, and her strength; to whom it is not permitted to teach, nor to have dominion over the man, nor let her usurp the office of preaching (I Cor. XIII).

475DIt is against nature for a man to do womanish things, to curl his hair, to twist his locks; whence the Apostle: If a man nourish his hair, it is a shame unto him. But to the woman her hair is given for a covering; a man therefore shall not be clothed with a woman's garment, that is, with loose and dissolute working.

(RAB.) We go along the way, etc., as far as but out of it we retain the allegorical sense in the mind.

VERS. 8.-- A new house, that is, the Church.

A wall of the roof, that is, the guard of the virtues.

Lest blood be shed in thy house, that is, lest thou be held guilty when another sins.

(GREG., lib. VIII Moral., cap. 39.) Lest it be shed. Through carelessness and sloth. Whence he who gathers virtues without 476Ahumility, as it were carries dust into the wind.

VERS. 9.-- A vineyard, the doctrine, or the people committed to the teachers, where the wine of spiritual grace ought to abound.

With other seed, that is, with the heretical. The good seed is the word of God; the wicked seed is that which the enemy sowed (Matth. XIII): care must therefore be taken lest anything of error be mingled in, and the seed of God be corrupted.

VERS. 10.-- Thou shalt not plough. (ISID.) He ploughs with an ox and an ass who receives the Gospels together with the observance of the Jews, which went before in shadow. In the ox also is the life of those who work well, in the ass the slothfulness of the foolish: as if to say, join not the fool to the wise man in preaching, lest through him who is not strong to fulfil the thing thou hinder him who prevails. 476BBut the fool and the wise are well joined together, so that the one may command and the other obey, not that they should announce the word of God with equal power.

Allegorically he mixes wool or purple with linen garments who lives disorderly, and with departures of divers kinds, as if a nun should have the ornaments of a wife, or a wife bear the appearance of a virgin. Now this was figured in garments which is now declared in morals.

VERS. 12.-- Little cords. For a distinction, namely, as the sign of circumcision separates the Israelites from the rest. But superstitious masters, catching at the breeze of popular favour and at gains from silly women, enlarged their fringes and bound thorns to them, that whether walking or sitting they might be pricked, and so be admonished to serve God; 476Cwhence: They enlarge their phylacteries and they magnify their fringes.

VERS. 13.-- If a man take. (AUG., quaest. 33.) It appears how women subject to their husbands, etc., as far as wherewith the other should have been punished.

VERS. 15.-- Her father and mother. The teachers of the Church, who as father and mother cherish the people: fathers by authority, mothers by tenderness.

VERS. 18.-- And they shall beat him. It is no wonder if the man who falsely accuses is bidden to be beaten and to pay a fine, but the girl, if she be guilty, to be stoned: because through the woman was the beginning of sin, who seduced the man and sinned doubly, first against God, afterwards against the man; the man only against God

VERS. 19.-- A hundred sicles, etc. By the perfection of doctrine 476Dand of solicitude which he should spend upon the people subject to him, or of penance. For the hundredfold number is perfect, the fiftyfold consecrated to penance.

VERS. 23.-- If a man have betrothed a maid that is a virgin. In this chapter, as in the two preceding and the two following, he condemns bodily fornication.

VERS. 26.-- She shall suffer nothing. But she shall be called back to the faith begun, being freed from her enemies through the grace of baptism.

VERS. 29.-- He shall not be able. (AUG., quaest. 34.) It is deservedly asked whether this be the penalty, that he cannot put her away for all time whom he disorderly and unlawfully violated; for if we understand that she cannot, that is, ought not, be put away for all time, because she has been made his wife, there occurs the fact that Moses permitted 477Aa bill of divorce to be given and the wife to be put away. But in those things which unlawfully corrupt he willed that it should not be lawful, lest he should seem to have done it for a mockery, and rather to have feigned that he took her. This he commanded also concerning her whom her husband had calumniated concerning the tokens of virginity not found.

VERS. 30.-- He shall not. Our Father is Christ, whose wife is the Church; it is not lawful for a man to transfer the dignity of that bridegroom to himself, nor to violate his bride, so as to change her love unto himself, seeking his own praise and not God's out of the gift of virtues or out of preaching; to whom it is fitting to say: Not to us, O Lord, not to us, but to thy name give glory (Psal. CIII).

CAPUT XXIII.

VERS. 1.-- Non intrabit. Omnes qui molliter vivunt, 477Bnec virile opus perficiunt, non possunt permanere in congregatione sanctorum nec digni sunt introitu regni coelorum, quod violentiam patitur, et violenti diripiunt illud.

Mamzer. Degener sensu vel moribus; vel quem haereticorum pravitas in perceptione sacramentorum degenerem fecit; vel ritus gentilitatis aut Judaismi maculavit. Is in Ecclesiam recipietur, cum integra fide decalogum legis secundum Evangelii institutionem servaverit, et carnalem conversationem Spiritu sancto roboratus abdicaverit; unde: Si vis ad vitam ingredi, serva mandata (Matth. XIX). Et alibi: Ego sum ostium: per me si quis intrat, salvabitur (Joan. X).

VERS. 3.-- Ammonites et Moabites. (AUG., quaest. 33.) Alia editio, etc., usque ad feminis imputaverunt, 477Cquas conservare maluerunt.

Moabites. Haereticos semper ab Ecclesia esse pellendos significat, quasi, Haereticum hominem post unam et alteram correctionem devita.

VERS. 4.-- Quia noluerunt nobis occurrere cum, etc. Tradunt Hebraei quod hae gentes propinquae sibi exeuntibus de Aegypto occurrerunt cum pane tantum, cum scirent nos in deserto maxime siti laborare, et ideo huic maledictioni subjecti sunt.

Mystice designant haereticos, qui fugientibus de Aegypto hujus mundi ad veram terram promissionis offerunt panem pollutum, non aqua sapientiae dulcoratum, et ideo ab Ecclesia alienati sunt.

VERS. 5.-- Vertitque. Quia saepe persecutio causa majoris prosperitatis est. Quia diligentibus Deum omnia 477Dcooperantur in bonum (Rom. VIII).

VERS. 7.-- Non abominaberis Idumaeum. Idumaeus sanguineus vel terrenus carnem (cujus est terrena materia) significat: Aegyptius, id est tenebrosus, corpus morbidum. Carnem nostram non debemus abominari, sed fovere et necessaria illi praebere, curam tamen ejus in desideriis non facere: Frater, inquit, tuus est, quia ex carne sumus et anima. Aegyptium, id est corpus non abominemur, in cujus terra advenae fuimus; anima enim, quae coelestem habet originem, quasi advena moratur in corpore, unde Apostolus: Dum sumus in corpore, peregrinamur a Domino (II Cor. V). Qui nati fuerint ex eis, etc., id est paulatim fient spirituales.

478APer Idumaeum et Aegyptium significatur praesens vita, cui quamvis renuntiantes Aegyptiam terram, id est conversationem pristinam et concupiscentiam mundi declinemus; dum tamen hujus mundi necessitatibus subdimur, non omnino Aegyptiam nationem exstinguimus: sed quodammodo separati, quotidiano victu et vestitu contenti sumus; tertia generatione in Ecclesiam intrabunt, quia saecularis potest sanctis Dei conjungi, cum perfecte in Patrem et Filium et Spiritum sanctum crediderit, vel cum spe, fide, et charitate ornatus fuerit.

VERS. 9.-- Quando. Spiritales Christi milites instruit, quibus alibi dicitur: Omnis qui in agone contendit, ab omnibus se abstinet (I Cor. IX).

Si. (AUG., lib. de bono conjugali, cap. 20.) Multa 478Bponit lex in sacramentis et umbris futurorum, etc., usque ad non quia corpus mortuum peccatum est, sed significat peccatum animae a justitia desertae.

Mystice (GREG., lib. IX Moral., cap. 40.) Si fuerit inter vos homo, qui nocturno pollutus, etc. Nocturnum somnium tentatio occulta, etc., usque ad et ad fidelium merita reparetur praesumenda.

VERS. 12.-- Habebis. Corporalem enim munditiam diligit Deus: ideo praecepit nihil foedum remanere in castris.

Mystice. (GREG., lib. XXXI Mor., cap. 32.) Habebis locum, etc. Naturae corruptibilis pondere gravatae a nostrae mentis utero quaedam cogitationum superflua quasi ventris gravamen erumpunt. Sed portare sub balteo paxillum debemus, ut ad reprehendendos nosmetipsos 478Csemper accincti acutum circa nos stimulum compunctionis habeamus, quod terram nostrae mentis poenitentiae dolore confodiat, et quod a nobis fetidum erumpit, abscondat.

VERS. 13.-- In balteo. Balteo enim renes accinguntur, ideo per balteum, malae voluntatis contritio designatur, unde Job: Balteum regum dissolvit, et praecinxit fune renes eorum (Job. II).

Humo operies. Terrena fragilitate considerata, sordes debemus operire.

VERS. 15.-- Non trades servum (AUG., quaest. 36.) LXX: Non trades puerum domino suo qui appositus est tibi a domino suo. Non quod dominus eum apposuerit, etc., usque ad hoc explanat dicens: In vobis habitabit in omni loco ubi placuerit ei (I Reg. XXVII).

478DVERS. 37.-- Non erit meretrix. (AUG., quaest. 37.) LXX: Non erit meretrix, etc., usque ad unde quid nobis videretur, ibi tractavimus.

Non offeres mercedem prostibuli. (AUG. quaest. 38.) LXX: Non offeres, etc., usque ad dicendum fuit, quod Domino abominatio sit.

Pretium canis, rapacis. Dona iniquorum non probat Altissimus. Qui offert sacrificium ex substantia pauperum, quasi qui victimat filium in conspectu patris (Eccli. XXXIV).

VERS. 19.-- Non foenerabis fratri tuo. Usuram et avaritiam removet, et charitatem impendere jubet.

Et pecunia, quae ad usuram dari prohibetur, quia 479Avitium cupiditatis est exigere velle, quod cognoscis te non commodasse. Hanc Dominus habuit, et inde pauperibus erogandam tradidit, et nos ad liberalitatem largitatis invitavit. Altera est quam ad usuram dare debemus; unde: Nonne oportuit te pecuniam committere nummulariis ut ego veniens exigerem quod meum est cum usura? (Matth. XXV).

VERS. 20.-- Sed alieno. Infideli vel iniquo cui pecuniam damus ad usuram, cum pro verbo praedicationis exigimus fidem, poenitentiam et operationem bonam.

VERS. 21.-- Non tardabis reddere. Unde Salomon: Ruina est homini post vota tractare vel tardare (Proverb. X).

VERS. 24.-- Ingressus vineam proximi. In ecclesia 479Balterius episcopi, potest alter aliquos corrigere vel confirmare, totam plebem non licet ei regere, vel magna negotia tractare.

Foras. Extra ecclesiam hanc gratiam efferre non licet, quia in una domo carnes agni edi jubentur.

VERS. 25.-- Si intraveris. Sunt quaedam loca Scripturae quae historialiter fidem imbuunt, quaedam moraliter sanctam conversationem instruunt: quaedam non secundum historiam, sed secundum allegoriam veneranda mysteria ostendunt. Caute ergo in segete proximi intrandum est, ut discernendo carpas cibum, non praecipitandum succisae messis incurras judicium.

CHAPTER XXIII.

VERS. 1.-- He shall not enter. All who live softly, 477Band do not accomplish a manly work, cannot remain in the congregation of the saints, nor are they worthy of entrance into the kingdom of heaven, which suffereth violence, and the violent bear it away.

Mamzer. Degenerate in sense or in morals; or one whom the depravity of heretics has made degenerate in the receiving of the sacraments; or whom the rite of heathenism or of Judaism has stained. He shall be received into the Church when with entire faith he shall have kept the decalogue of the law according to the institution of the Gospel, and, strengthened by the Holy Spirit, shall have renounced carnal converse; whence: If thou wilt enter into life, keep the commandments (Matth. XIX). And elsewhere: I am the door: by me if any man enter in, he shall be saved (Joan. X).

VERS. 3.-- The Ammonite and the Moabite. (AUG., quaest. 33.) Another edition, etc., as far as they imputed it to the women, 477Cwhom they chose rather to preserve.

The Moabite. It signifies that heretics are ever to be driven from the Church, as if to say, A man that is a heretic, after the first and second correction, avoid.

VERS. 4.-- Because they would not meet us with, etc. The Hebrews hand down that these nations, being near of kin to them, met those going forth out of Egypt with bread only, though they knew that we laboured in the desert most of all with thirst, and therefore they were made subject to this curse.

Mystically they designate heretics, who to those fleeing out of the Egypt of this world unto the true land of promise offer polluted bread, not sweetened with the water of wisdom, and therefore they are estranged from the Church.

VERS. 5.-- And he turned. Because often persecution is the cause of a greater prosperity. For to them that love God all things 477Dwork together unto good (Rom. VIII).

VERS. 7.-- Thou shalt not abhor the Idumaean. The Idumaean, the bloody or earthly one, signifies the flesh (whose matter is earthly); the Egyptian, that is, the dark one, the sickly body. We ought not to abhor our flesh, but to cherish it and to furnish it with what is necessary, yet not to make provision for it in its desires: He is thy brother, he says, because we are of flesh and soul. Let us not abhor the Egyptian, that is, the body, in whose land we were sojourners; for the soul, which has a heavenly origin, dwells in the body as a sojourner; whence the Apostle: While we are in the body, we are absent from the Lord (II Cor. V). They that shall be born of them, etc., that is, little by little they shall become spiritual.

478ABy the Idumaean and the Egyptian is signified the present life; and although in renouncing it we shun the Egyptian land, that is, our former converse and the concupiscence of the world, yet while we are subject to the necessities of this world we do not altogether extinguish the Egyptian nation: but, in a manner set apart, we are content with daily food and clothing; in the third generation they shall enter into the Church, because a man of the world can be joined to the saints of God when he shall have believed perfectly in the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit, or when he shall have been adorned with hope, faith and charity.

VERS. 9.-- When. He instructs the spiritual soldiers of Christ, to whom it is said elsewhere: Every one that striveth for the mastery refraineth himself from all things (I Cor. IX).

If. (AUG., lib. de bono conjugali, cap. 20.) Many things 478Bthe law sets in sacraments and in shadows of things to come, etc., as far as not because a dead body is sin, but it signifies the sin of a soul deserted by justice.

Mystically (GREG., lib. IX Moral., cap. 40.) If there be among you a man who, defiled by a nocturnal, etc. A nocturnal dream is a hidden temptation, etc., as far as and let it be repaired unto the merits of the faithful, which are to be presumed.

VERS. 12.-- Thou shalt have. For God loves bodily cleanness: therefore he commanded that nothing foul should remain in the camp.

Mystically. (GREG., lib. XXXI Mor., cap. 32.) Thou shalt have a place, etc. Certain superfluities of thoughts burst forth from the womb of our mind, weighed down by the burden of a corruptible nature, as it were the heaviness of the belly. But we ought to carry a paddle under our girdle, so that, being always girt for the reproving of ourselves, 478Cwe may have about us the sharp goad of compunction, which may dig into the earth of our mind with the pain of penance, and may hide what bursts forth foully from us.

VERS. 13.-- In the girdle. For with a girdle the reins are girt; therefore by the girdle is designated the contrition of an evil will; whence Job: He looseth the belt of kings, and girdeth their loins with a cord (Job. II).

Thou shalt cover it with earth. Considering our earthly frailty, we ought to cover our filth.

VERS. 15.-- Thou shalt not deliver up the servant (AUG., quaest. 36.) The LXX: Thou shalt not deliver up the boy to his master, who was set beside thee by his master. Not that the master set him there, etc., as far as he explains this, saying: He shall dwell among you in every place that shall please him (I Reg. XXVII).

478DVERS. 37.-- There shall be no harlot. (AUG., quaest. 37.) The LXX: There shall be no harlot, etc., as far as whence we treated there what might seem to us.

Thou shalt not offer the hire of a strumpet. (AUG. quaest. 38.) The LXX: Thou shalt not offer, etc., as far as it had to be said that it is an abomination to the Lord.

The price of a dog, of a ravening one. The Most High approves not the gifts of the unjust. He that offereth sacrifice of the substance of the poor is as one that sacrificeth the son in the presence of his father (Eccli. XXXIV).

VERS. 19.-- Thou shalt not lend to thy brother upon usury. He removes usury and avarice, and bids charity be spent.

And money, which is forbidden to be given for usury, because 479Ait is a vice of covetousness to wish to exact what thou knowest thou hast not lent. This the Lord had, and thence delivered it to be laid out upon the poor, and invited us to the liberality of largess. There is another which we ought to give for usury; whence: Oughtest thou not therefore to have committed my money to the bankers, that at my coming I might exact my own with usury? (Matth. XXV).

VERS. 20.-- But to the stranger. To the unbeliever or the unjust, to whom we give money for usury when for the word of preaching we exact faith, penance and good working.

VERS. 21.-- Thou shalt not delay to pay. Whence Solomon: It is ruin to a man to deliberate or to delay after vows (Proverb. X).

VERS. 24.-- Going into thy neighbour's vineyard. In the church 479Bof another bishop one may correct or confirm certain persons; it is not lawful for him to rule the whole people, or to handle great affairs.

Without. It is not lawful to carry this grace outside the church, because in one house the flesh of the lamb is commanded to be eaten.

VERS. 25.-- If thou go in. There are certain places of Scripture which historically imbue with faith, certain which morally instruct in holy converse; certain which show the mysteries to be venerated not according to history but according to allegory. Warily therefore must one go into the standing corn of one's neighbour, that by discerning thou mayest pluck food, and mayest not, by that which must be hurried, incur the judgment of the reaped harvest.

CAPUT XXIV.

VERS. 1.-- Si acceperit. (RAB. in Deut., tom. II.) 479CHomo uxorem accepit, etc., usque ad tanquam indigna et errore polluta abominabilis permansit; unde: Vobis oportebat primum loqui verbum Dei, sed quoniam, etc. (Act. XIII.)

(AUG., lib. I de sermone Domini in monte.) Multae erant in lege causae dimittendi uxores, etc., usque ad ut qui habent uxores, sint quasi non habentes (I Cor. VII).

Scribet. Ad duritiam cordis vestri scripsit Moyses praeceptum istud, malens scilicet indulgere dissidium, quam committi homicidium.

VERS. 4.-- Ne peccare. Polluit terram suam, qui concupiscentiis carnalibus inquinat corpus suum. Ideo necesse est ambitionem praesentis vitae citius domari, ne valeat dominari. Et cum terrena actio semel 479Dfuerit repulsa, non est repetenda: ne post tergum respiciat, qui in aratro manum posuerat.

VERS. 5.-- Cum acceperit. Quasi, quicunque carnalibus desideriis se obligat ad regimen Ecclesiae, et ad spiritalem militiam se non habilem esse cognoscat: Quia nemo militans Deo implicat se saecularibus negotiis, ut ei placeat cui se probavit (II Tim. II); unde: Nemini cito manum imposueris.

(ISID.) Tollit molam superiorem qui peccatum flenti dicit: Non habebis veniam de commissis. Tollit inferiorem qui peccatum palpando dicit: Age mala quantum vis, Deus pius est et ad veniam paratus; utiliter utraque mola habetur, si spes sit cum emendante formidine, et formido cum spe.

480A(GREG., lib. XXXIII Moral., cap. 16.) Non accipies loco pignoris inferiorem, etc. Accipere aliquando offerre dicimus, etc., usque ad et incassum metuit qui non confidit.

VERS. 7.-- Auferes. (AUG., quaest. 39.) Assidue hoc Scriptura dicit, etc., usque ad quod exponens ait: Qui furabatur jam non furetur (Ephes. IV).

VERS. 8.-- Plagam leprae. Nota propter peccatum hanc infirmitatem hominibus accidere, sicut Oziae qui sacerdotium usurpavit.

VERS. 10.-- Cum repetes. (AUG., quaest. 41.) Ad opus misericordiae pertinet ut pignorator in domum non intret, etc., usque ad non habens ubi dormiat.

Nonne. Qui Evangelium annuntiat, de Evangelio vivat (I Cor. IX). Dignus est enim operarius mercede 480Bsua (Luc. X).

VERS. 16.-- Non occidentur. (AUG., quaest. 42). Etiam lex dicit, etc., usque ad non tres vel quatuor intelligi voluit.

VERS. 17.-- Non pervertes. (AUG., quaest. 43). LXX: Non declinabis judicium advenae et orphani et viduae. Non pignorabis vestimentum viduae. Cur non ait, etc., usque ad continentia in eis laudatur, non desolatio commendatur.

Viduae. Vidua anima ante diabolo juncta, cui conversae fidei vestimentum non est auferendum.

VERS. 19.-- Non reverteris. Invidendo gentibus, qui sunt advenae testamentorum Dei.

VERS. 20.-- Si fruges collegeris. Etsi superbi colligant et improbi, ille misericordiam facit qui hoc 480Canimo dimittit, ut egentes habeant. Praeterea cum haec populo praecipiuntur; qui non indigent, admonentur ne ista quaerant. Si autem quaesierunt, tanquam praedones pauperum judicandi sunt.

Vineam tuam. Vineam de qua colligitur vinum, quod laetificat cor hominis (Psal. CIII).

CHAPTER XXIV.

VERS. 1.-- If he shall take. (RAB. in Deut., tom. II.) 479CA man took a wife, etc., as far as she remained abominable, as unworthy and defiled with error; whence: To you it behoved us first to speak the word of God, but because, etc. (Act. XIII.)

(AUG., lib. I de sermone Domini in monte.) There were in the law many causes of putting away wives, etc., as far as that they who have wives be as though they had none (I Cor. VII).

He shall write. For the hardness of your heart Moses wrote this precept, choosing rather to allow a separation than that a murder be committed.

VERS. 4.-- Lest he sin. He pollutes his land who defiles his body with carnal concupiscences. Therefore it is necessary that the ambition of the present life be tamed the sooner, lest it prevail to have dominion. And when an earthly action has once 479Dbeen repelled, it is not to be sought again: let not him look back who had put his hand to the plough.

VERS. 5.-- When he shall have taken. As if to say, whosoever binds himself with carnal desires, let him acknowledge that he is not fit for the rule of the Church, nor for the spiritual warfare: because no man being a soldier to God entangleth himself with secular business, that he may please him to whom he hath approved himself (II Tim. II); whence: Impose hands hastily upon no man.

(ISID.) He takes away the upper millstone who says to one weeping for his sin: Thou shalt have no pardon for what thou hast committed. He takes away the lower who, soothing the sin, says: Do evil as much as thou wilt, God is merciful and ready to pardon; both millstones are usefully had, if hope be with amending fear, and fear with hope.

480A(GREG., lib. XXXIII Moral., cap. 16.) Thou shalt not take the lower stone for a pledge, etc. Sometimes we say "to take" for "to offer," etc., as far as and he fears in vain who does not trust.

VERS. 7.-- Thou shalt take away. (AUG., quaest. 39.) Scripture says this continually, etc., as far as which, expounding, he says: He that stole, let him now steal no more (Ephes. IV).

VERS. 8.-- The plague of leprosy. Note that this infirmity befalls men on account of sin, as it did Ozias, who usurped the priesthood.

VERS. 10.-- When thou shalt demand back. (AUG., quaest. 41.) It belongs to the work of mercy that the taker of a pledge should not enter into the house, etc., as far as having nowhere to sleep.

Is it not. He that announceth the Gospel, let him live of the Gospel (I Cor. IX). For the labourer is worthy of his hire 480B(Luc. X).

VERS. 16.-- They shall not be put to death. (AUG., quaest. 42). The law also says, etc., as far as he did not wish three or four to be understood.

VERS. 17.-- Thou shalt not pervert. (AUG., quaest. 43). The LXX: Thou shalt not turn aside the judgment of the stranger and of the fatherless and of the widow. Thou shalt not take the widow's garment in pledge. Why does he not say, etc., as far as continence is praised in them, desolation is not commended.

Of the widow. The widow is the soul formerly joined to the devil, from whom, when she is converted, the garment of faith is not to be taken away.

VERS. 19.-- Thou shalt not return. By envying the Gentiles, who are strangers to the testaments of God.

VERS. 20.-- If thou hast gathered the fruits. Even though the proud and the wicked gather, he does mercy who leaves this behind with the 480Cmind that the needy may have it. Moreover, when these things are enjoined upon the people; they who are not in need are admonished not to seek these things. But if they have sought them, they are to be judged as robbers of the poor.

Thy vineyard. The vineyard from which is gathered the wine that maketh glad the heart of man (Psal. CIII).

CAPUT XXV.

VERS. 1.-- Si fuerit causa. (AUG., quaest. 45). LXX: Si autem fuerit altercatio inter homines, etc., usque ad sic et peccata quae non in Deum, sed in hominem videntur admitti, quaedam sunt digna morte, quaedam verbere.

VERS. 3.-- Quadragenarium. Quadragenarius denario quater ducto perficitur. Nos vero de quatuor 480Delementis secundum corpus consistimus: praecepta decalogi per denarium accipiuntur. Jubet ergo judicium vel vindictam, in qua carnis peccatum plectitur, secundum praecepta ordinari, et omnia praecepta in decalogo vult intelligi.

(ISID.) In quadragenario hoc tempus figuratur, etc., usque ad illic sine peccato invenimur.

VERS. 4.-- Os bovis triturantis. (ISID.) In bove vita operantium exprimitur, de quo dicitur: Dignus est enim operarius mercede sua (Luc. X). Vult ergo Scriptura praestari carnalia iis a quibus accipimus spiritualia, qui occupati in doctrina non possunt sibi providere necessaria.

VERS. 5.-- Quando. (AUG., quaest. 46.) LXX Si 481Aante habitaverunt fratres, etc., usque ad aut adoptio fecit alterum patrem quem posset habere Joseph.

VERS. 5.-- Mortuus fuerit. Passus est et ascendit in coelum adhuc synagoga sterili permanente.

Sed accipiet, etc. Qui regere valet, cui nolenti in faciem mulier exspuit. Qui enim donis spiritalibus non curat prodesse aliis, hujus etiam bonis Ecclesia exprobrans quasi in faciem jactat salivam.

VERS. 9.-- Calceamentum. De quo dicitur: Calceati pedes in praeparatione Evangelii pacis (Ezech. VI). Si ut nostram, sic curam proximi gerimus, utrumque pedem calceamento munimus; qui vero suam cogitans utilitatem proximorum negligit, quasi unius pedis calceamentum cum dedecore amittit.

481BDe pede, id est mercede praedicationis privatum ostendet. Doctorum enim pedes calceati sunt, quia exemplis praecedentium patrum muniti gressus praedicationis eorum imitati sunt. Utroque pede calceatus est, qui sibi et aliis prodest.

VERS. 11.-- Et unus contra alterum. Catholicus haereticusque quorum perpetua contentio, quia dispar intentio: alter enim falsitatem, alter defendit veritatem, et resistere oportet errori, ne sibi stultus sapiens videatur.

Miseritque. Per falsam ignominiam vel actionem abominandam voluerit catholici conversationem maculare vel doctrinam diffamare.

Verenda. Infirmiora membra Ecclesiae, scilicet volent blandiendo decipere vel insultando deridere.

481CVERS. 13.-- Non habebis in sacculo. In Levitico idem interdicitur. Salomon quoque ait: Pondus magnum et pusillum, et mensurae duplices, immundae sunt apud Deum (Prov. XX).

(ISID.) Non habebis in sacculo. Spiritualiter quoque studendum est, ne diversa pondera in corde habeamus, id est, districtionis regulam nobis mollientes, eos quibus verbum Dei praedicamus districtioribus praeceptis quasi gravioribus ponderibus obruamus. Quod cum facimus, diverso pondere praeceptorum Domini mercedem frugemque appendimus, unde: Abominatio est Domino pondus duplex (Prov. XX).

VERS. 17.-- Memento quae. Tradunt Hebraei quod 481Dleprosos et semine fluentes, et alios extra castra positos interfecerit Amalec, et hos dicunt esse extremos agminis Domini. Amalec lingens sanguinem. Hi sunt daemones qui nobis per baptismum de spiritali Aegypto exeuntibus adversantur, et in virtutibus extremos, et in itinere hujus vitae lassatos peccata suggerendo student occidere. Hos cum Dominus facultatem dederit, omnino debemus subvertere et suggestiones eorum omni modo ejicere atque delere, ne si aliquas reliquias, sicut Saul, servaverimus, quasi inobedientes cum eis subvertamur.

CHAPTER XXV.

VERS. 1.-- If there be a cause. (AUG., quaest. 45). The LXX: But if there be a strife between men, etc., as far as so also of the sins which seem to be committed not against God but against man, some are worthy of death, some of stripes.

VERS. 3.-- Forty. The number forty is completed by ten drawn four times. But we, according to the body, consist of four 480Delements: the precepts of the decalogue are taken by the number ten. He bids therefore that the judgment or the vengeance, in which the sin of the flesh is punished, be ordered according to the precepts, and he wills all the precepts to be understood in the decalogue.

(ISID.) In the number forty this time is figured, etc., as far as there we are found without sin.

VERS. 4.-- The mouth of the ox that treadeth out. (ISID.) In the ox is expressed the life of those that work, of whom it is said: For the labourer is worthy of his hire (Luc. X). Scripture wills therefore that carnal things be supplied to those from whom we receive spiritual things, who, being occupied in teaching, cannot provide themselves with the things needful.

VERS. 5.-- When. (AUG., quaest. 46.) The LXX If 481Abrothers have dwelt together before, etc., as far as or adoption made another father whom Joseph could have.

VERS. 5.-- Shall have died. He suffered and ascended into heaven while the synagogue still remained barren.

But he shall take, etc. He who is strong to rule, in whose face, when he is unwilling, the woman spits. For he who cares not to profit others by spiritual gifts — the Church, upbraiding him too for his goods, casts as it were spittle in his face.

VERS. 9.-- The shoe. Of which it is said: Shod feet in the preparation of the Gospel of peace (Ezech. VI). If we bear the care of our neighbour as we bear our own, we fortify both feet with a shoe; but he who, thinking of his own advantage, neglects that of his neighbours, as it were loses with disgrace the shoe of one foot.

481BFrom the foot, that is, he shall show him deprived of the reward of preaching. For the feet of teachers are shod, because, fortified by the examples of the fathers who went before, they have imitated their steps of preaching. He is shod on both feet who profits himself and others.

VERS. 11.-- And one against the other. The Catholic and the heretic, whose contention is perpetual, because their intention is unlike: for the one defends falsehood, the other truth; and it behoves us to resist error, lest the fool seem wise to himself.

And she shall put forth. He shall have wished by false ignominy or by an abominable action to stain the converse of the Catholic or to defame his teaching.

The privy parts. The weaker members of the Church, whom, namely, they will wish to deceive by flattering or to deride by insulting.

481CVERS. 13.-- Thou shalt not have in thy bag. In Leviticus the same is forbidden. Solomon also says: A great weight and a small, and double measures, are unclean before God (Prov. XX).

(ISID.) Thou shalt not have in thy bag. Spiritually also we must take care that we have not diverse weights in the heart, that is, that, softening the rule of strictness for ourselves, we overwhelm those to whom we preach the word of God with stricter precepts, as with heavier weights. And when we do this, we weigh out the reward and the fruit of the Lord's precepts with a diverse weight; whence: A double weight is an abomination to the Lord (Prov. XX).

VERS. 17.-- Remember what. The Hebrews hand down that 481DAmalec slew the lepers and those with an issue of seed, and others set outside the camp, and these they say are the hindmost of the Lord's army. Amalec is "licking blood." These are the demons who withstand us as we go forth by baptism out of the spiritual Egypt, and who strive to slay, by suggesting sins, those who are hindmost in virtues and wearied in the journey of this life. These, when the Lord shall have given the power, we ought utterly to overthrow, and in every way to cast out and destroy their suggestions, lest, if we have kept back any remnants, as Saul did, we be overthrown with them as disobedient.

CAPUT XXVI.

VERS. 1.-- Cumque intraveris, etc. Sicut Israelitae 482Ain terra promissionis suas primitias jubentur offerre pro gratiarum actione, quia erepti sunt de Aegypto sic nos per spiritalem Jordanem, id est baptismum, terram Ecclesiae intrantes ponamus in cartalo conversationis nostrae primitias operationis bonae, et offeramus Deo in altari fidei catholicae, ut sacerdos noster Christus suscipiat, ibique confiteamur Domino dicentes quod Syrus, qui interpretatur sublimis, vel humectans, id est diabolus, persequebatur patrem nostrum, id est protoplastum, ut deceptum per superbiam, gulam et avaritiam, paradisi possessione privaret, unde exsul in Aegypto hujus mundi peregrinatus est in parvo numero, quia multi idololatriam secuti sunt, et pauci fideles remanserunt. Sed postquam lege data plures surrexerunt, scientiam 482Bet fidem unius Dei accipientes, afflixerunt eos Aegyptii, id est daemones, onera peccatorum gravissima imponentes. Sed respexit Deus humani generis afflictionem et laborem, et misit Filium suum, qui manus ejus et brachium dicitur, ut eriperet de potestate tenebrarum, et transferret in regnum lucis. Sic tradidit nobis terram lacte et melle manantem, id est, potentem Ecclesiam, ubi lac innocentiae et favus doctrinae, unde nutriantur parvuli, et mel dulcis et multiplicis sapientiae, unde satientur perfecti.

VERS. 10.-- Quam Dominus Deus dedit, etc. Quasi non offero nisi quod dedit: omne enim bonum a Deo est.

Domini Dei tui. Qui quasi sub oculis servabit, et 482Creddet in illa die justus judex.

VERS. 11.-- Epulaberis, spiritali convivio, de quo dicitur: Comedite, amici, et inebriamini, charissimi (Cantic. V).

Et Levites, verus Dei minister assumptus ex hominibus, et constitutus pro hominibus in his quae ad Deum sunt.

VERS. 12.-- Quando compleveris: hoc legis implesse Tobiam, sed qui longe erant a templo tertio anno collectas trium annorum decimas dabant pauperibus et Levitis qui secum morabantur.

CHAPTER XXVI.

VERS. 1.-- And when thou shalt have entered, etc. Just as the Israelites 482Ain the land of promise are bidden to offer their first-fruits for a giving of thanks, because they were snatched out of Egypt, so let us, entering through the spiritual Jordan — that is, baptism — into the land of the Church, place in the basket of our manner of life the first-fruits of good working, and let us offer them to God upon the altar of the catholic faith, that our priest Christ may receive them; and there let us confess to the Lord, saying that the Syrian — who is interpreted "lofty," or "moistening," that is, the devil — was pursuing our father, that is, the first-formed man, that, having deceived him through pride, gluttony and avarice, he might deprive him of the possession of paradise; whence as an exile he sojourned in the Egypt of this world in a small number, because many followed idolatry, and few remained faithful. But after the law was given and more arose, receiving the knowledge 482Band the faith of the one God, the Egyptians — that is, the demons — afflicted them, laying upon them the most grievous burdens of sins. But God looked upon the affliction and the labour of the human race, and sent his Son, who is called his hand and his arm, that he might snatch them out of the power of darkness and carry them over into the kingdom of light. So he handed over to us a land flowing with milk and honey, that is, the mighty Church, where there is the milk of innocence and the honeycomb of doctrine, whereby little ones are nourished, and the honey of a sweet and manifold wisdom, whereby the perfect are filled.

VERS. 10.-- Which the Lord thy God hath given, etc. As if to say, I offer nothing but what he has given; for every good thing is from God.

Of the Lord thy God. Who will keep them as it were beneath his eyes, and 482Cwill render them on that day, a just judge.

VERS. 11.-- Thou shalt feast, with a spiritual banquet, of which it is said: Eat, O friends, and be inebriated, my dearly beloved (Cantic. V).

And the Levite, the true minister of God, taken from among men and appointed for men in the things that pertain to God.

VERS. 12.-- When thou shalt have completed: that Tobias fulfilled this of the law; but those who were far from the temple gave in the third year the tithes gathered of three years to the poor and to the Levites who dwelt with them.

CAPUT XXVII.

VERS. 2.-- Lapides. Doctores debent omnem asperitatem deponere, et ideo splendidi apparere habentes 482Dlegem scriptam in cordibus suis.

VERS. 4.-- Quando ergo. (ISID.) Quomodo hoc factum fuerit, etc., usque ad qui altaris constructione digni sunt.

Qui juxta montem Garizim incedunt electi ad benedicendum, figurant eos qui non metu poenae, sed coelestis promissionis amore succensi, veniunt ad salutem. Qui juxta montem Hebal incedunt, in quo maledictiones prolatae sunt; hi sunt qui non amore benedictionum vel promissionum, sed suppliciorum timore, legis praecepta faciunt. Omnes autem circa arcam incedunt, quia ab Ecclesiae sinu non recedunt. Sed nobiliores, qui ex charitate, quam qui poenae timore serviunt. Solus vero Jesus potest hujusmodi 483Amentes cognoscere, et alios in monte Garizim ad benedicendum, alios in monte Hebal ad maledicendum statuere, non ut maledictionem accipiant, sed ut maledictiones scriptas et peccatoribus constitutas caveant, et timore supplicii poeniteant.

VERS. 13.-- Et pronuntiabunt. Quia sacerdos verbum instanter et valenter populis praedicare debet, ut sciant, si mandata Dei praeterierint, quae pericula maneant; si obedierint, quae sequantur beneficia, scilicet in praesenti vita solatium, et in futura praemium aeternum. Maledictiones autem et benedictiones historialiter pertinent ad eos qui felicitatem terrenam amant vel infelicitatem formidant. Sed maxime Christianis cavendum, ne per inobedientiam spiritualium patiantur penuriam; perfectis enim 483Bviris terrenorum saepe contingit indigentia, cum semper adsit virtutum copia, unde: Usque in hanc horam esurimus et sitimus et nudi sumus, etc. (I Cor. IV).

Maledictus. Non solum idololatria simulacrorum prohibetur, sed omnis haereticorum error. Hi enim artifices vanitatis et superstitiosi dogmatis, qui de corde suo, quos volunt, fingunt sibi deos.

VERS. 16.-- Omnis populus. Omnes debent laudare bona, et detestari mala, tam praelati quam subditi.

VERS. 17.-- Maledictus qui transfert terminos. Hic est qui non contentus fide catholica et doctrina, superstitiones et sectas inducit.

VERS. 20.-- Maledictus qui dormit cum. Omnis 483Cincestus abominatio est, sed specialiter haereticos atque schismaticos percutit, qui sponsam Christi (qui omnium pater et creator est) errore corrumpunt. Tropologice vero prohibet ne quis actionem pravam patris sui imitando diffamet, et ignominiam ejus revelet.

VERS. 21.-- Maledictus. Maledictus est, qui gentilium stultitiam sequitur. Vir aut mulier, non per fragilitatem, sed perseveranter tali commistione inhaerens, reus est mortis.

VERS. 22.-- Dormit, id est maculat, prave docendo, aut prava exempla demonstrando, alterius animam. Pater enim noster unus est Deus, et mater Ecclesia, nos autem omnes fratres aut sorores sumus.

483DVERS. 23.-- Cum socru sua. Socrus, pravae actionis concupiscentia, quia omnis peccati concupiscentia mater est. Diligenter ergo attendamus ut concupiscentiae resistamus, ne cupita impleamus.

VERS. 24.-- Maledictus. Quocunque modo hominem occidere peccatum est. Mirum ergo videtur quare maledictum tantum dixit, qui clam percusserit. Sed forte clam percutere est corde odisse, unde: Qui odit fratrem suum, homicida est (Joan. III). Et alibi: Ne oderis fratrem tuum in corde tuo (Lev. XIX).

VERS. 26.-- Maledictus qui non permanet. Haec sententia generaliter et praeterita et praesentia et dicenda legis mandata complectitur, tanto gravior quanto generalior. Quis enim potest sine trangressione 484Aomnibus sermonibus legis permanere, cum et Paulus dicat: Quicunque ex operibus legis sunt, sub maledicto sunt? (Galat. III.) Nemo hanc maledictionem evadere potuit, nec legislator, nisi Christus, unde: Christus nos redemit de maledicto legis, factus pro nobis maledictum, etc.

CHAPTER XXVII.

VERS. 2.-- Stones. Teachers ought to lay aside all roughness, and therefore to appear shining, having 482Dthe law written in their hearts.

VERS. 4.-- When therefore. (ISID.) How this was done, etc., as far as who are worthy of the building of the altar.

Those who go beside Mount Gerizim, chosen for blessing, figure those who come to salvation not out of fear of punishment, but kindled by love of the heavenly promise. Those who go beside Mount Ebal, on which the curses were pronounced — these are they who perform the precepts of the law not out of love of the blessings or of the promises, but out of fear of punishments. But all of them go about the ark, because they do not withdraw from the bosom of the Church. Yet nobler are they who serve out of charity than they who serve out of fear of punishment. But Jesus alone can know 483Aminds of this sort, and can station some on Mount Gerizim for blessing, others on Mount Ebal for cursing — not that they should receive the curse, but that they should beware of the curses that are written and appointed for sinners, and out of fear of punishment repent.

VERS. 13.-- And they shall pronounce. Because the priest ought urgently and mightily to preach the word to the peoples, that they may know what perils remain if they pass by the commandments of God, and what benefits follow if they obey — namely, in the present life comfort, and in the life to come an eternal reward. Now the curses and the blessings belong, according to the history, to those who love earthly happiness or dread earthly unhappiness. But Christians must above all take heed lest through disobedience they suffer a want of spiritual things; for to perfect 483Bmen there often comes an indigence of earthly things, while there is always at hand an abundance of virtues, whence: Even unto this hour we hunger and thirst and are naked, etc. (I Cor. IV).

Cursed. Not only the idolatry of images is forbidden, but every error of heretics. For these are the artificers of vanity and of superstitious dogma, who out of their own heart fashion for themselves what gods they will.

VERS. 16.-- All the people. All ought to praise good things and to detest evil things, prelates as well as subjects.

VERS. 17.-- Cursed be he that removeth landmarks. This is he who, not content with the catholic faith and doctrine, brings in superstitions and sects.

VERS. 20.-- Cursed be he that lieth with. Every 483Cincest is an abomination, but it strikes especially heretics and schismatics, who by error corrupt the spouse of Christ (who is the father and creator of all). But tropologically it forbids that anyone, by imitating the depraved deed of his father, defame him and reveal his shame.

VERS. 21.-- Cursed. Cursed is he who follows the folly of the Gentiles. A man or a woman cleaving to such intercourse not through frailty but perseveringly is guilty of death.

VERS. 22.-- Lieth, that is, defiles another's soul by teaching depravedly, or by displaying depraved examples. For our father is one, God, and our mother the Church, and we are all brothers or sisters.

483DVERS. 23.-- With his mother-in-law. The mother-in-law is the concupiscence of a depraved deed, because concupiscence is the mother of every sin. Let us therefore diligently take heed that we resist concupiscence, lest we fulfil the things we have desired.

VERS. 24.-- Cursed. In whatever manner, to kill a man is a sin. It seems strange, therefore, why he called cursed only him who shall have struck in secret. But perhaps to strike in secret is to hate in the heart, whence: He that hateth his brother is a murderer (Joan. III). And elsewhere: Thou shalt not hate thy brother in thy heart (Lev. XIX).

VERS. 26.-- Cursed be he that abideth not. This sentence embraces generally both the past and the present and the yet-to-be-spoken commandments of the law, and is the graver the more general it is. For who can, without trangressione, 484Aabide in all the words of the law, since Paul also says: Whosoever are of the works of the law, are under a curse? (Galat. III.) No one could escape this curse, not even the lawgiver, save Christ, whence: Christ hath redeemed us from the curse of the law, being made a curse for us, etc.

CAPUT XXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Si autem audieris. Sicut inobedientibus praedicit maledictiones et supplicia, sic obedientibus promittit benedictiones et praemia.

VERS. 4.-- Fructus ventris. Diversos ordines notat subditorum. Alii enim doctoribus corporalia praebent adjumenta; alii laborare possunt quod sibi fuerit praeceptum; alii simplicitate contenti per innocentiam 484Bvitae student Deo placere: unusquisque proprium donum habet ex Deo.

VERS. 5.-- Benedictae reliquiae. Bonae intentionis praemium, etsi voluntas non habeat effectum, quia pax hominibus bonae voluntatis, etc. (Luc. II.)

VERS. 6.-- Ingrediens. Ingreditur ille per ostium, qui per fidem catholicam quam juxta voluntatem Dei intus meditatur et tractat: egreditur quando idem coram hominibus ad laudem Dei manifestat.

VERS. 7.-- In conspectu tuo. Ut gaudeas: Laetabitur justus cum viderit vindictam (Psal. LVII).

VERS. 8.-- Benedicetque. Bonis operibus tribuens incrementum et felicem successum.

Coelum. Prophetas et apostolos, de quibus dicitur, Coeli enarrant gloriam Dei: quos Dominus aperit, 484Ccum per eos doctrinae abundantiam tribuit, ut carnalium corda hoc imbre irrigata proferant germina bonorum operum.

Faenerabis. Id est tanta erit abundantia tibi, quod poteris dare pecuniam gentibus, illis vero non tanta. Vel, doctrina ecclesiastica intelligitur.

Faenerabis gentibus. Foenerat Ecclesia gentibus multis, quando divina charismata expendit nationibus. Nec foenus accipit, quia non praesentem remunerationem, sed futuram inquirit. Excellit enim omnibus, et caput est orbis, non cauda. Semper supra est, et non infra; quia licet pressuras et persecutiones malorum in mundo patiatur, fortior tamen adversariis est, fide et virtute praeeminet universis. Unde, constituet te Dominus in caput, etc.

484DVERS. 14.-- Non aectinaveris. (AUG., quaest., 48.) LXX: Non praeteribis ab omnibus verbis, etc., usque ad nec propter ista colendi sunt.

VERS. 15.-- Quod si audire nolueris. Sicut custodientibus mandata promisit benedictionem, sic negligentibus pronuntiat maledictionem.

VERS. 22.-- Percutiat te Dominus. (AUG. in Enchirid., cap. 98.) Quis tam impie desipiat, ut dicat Deum malas hominum voluntates, quas voluerit, quando voluerit, ubi voluerit, in bonum non posse convertere? Sed cum facit, per misericordiam facit: Cum non facit, per judicium non facit, qui cui vult miseretur, et quem vult obdurat.

Et aere corrupto. Vita polluta ex carnali immunditia. 485ACarnalis vita polluta ex carnali immunditia. Carnalis vita per aerem exprimitur, quia aeris spiramine humanum corpus vivificatur.

VERS. 23.-- Sit coelum. Elementa, quibus fructus nutriuntur, comparat durissimis metallis, quia peccata hominum commutant ordinem rerum, et quae ad solatium condita sunt humano generi, unde: Pugnavit pro eo orbis terrarum contra insensatos.

Coelum est aeneum praevaricatoribus legis, cum nullam a sanctis doctoribus merentur clementiam, sed durissimam disciplinam, quoniam ipsi non cor carneum et mollem animum, sed lapideum habent et ferreum. Sicut duro populo lex in lapide dabatur, ut ex munere acceptor figuraretur.

VERS. 24.-- Det Dominus imbrem terrae tuae pulverem: 485Bquasi, sicut pulvis et cinis non faciunt fructum, sic doctrina tibi infusa non excitet germina virtutum, sed aeternum praenuntiet interitum. Pulvis enim bonorum operum sterilitatem, cinis designat combustionem; quia paleae peccatorum comburentur igni inexstinguibili.

VERS. 25.-- Et dispergaris. Sicut Judaei captivati per latitudinem orbis dispersi sunt quia contra Deum collecti sunt, sic generatio malorum a spiritibus immundis dispersa per poenarum genera perturbatur, unde: Inimici tui peribunt et dispergentur omnes qui operantur iniquitatem (Psal. XCI).

VERS. 27.-- Percutiat te Dominus ulcere Aegypti. Ulcera dolorosa et purulenta malitia. Vesicae, inflata superbia. Scabies et prurigo, ira fervida et furoris 485Cinsania. Haec in praesenti congrua poena peccatis infligitur: post hanc vitam (quod signatur in posterioribus) fetorem scelerum in aeternis cruciatibus sustinebunt.

Percutiat te Dominus. Haeretici et schismatici clarescente Evangelio in toto orbe erroris caecitate involuti, amentiam suam exercentes catholicae fidei contradicunt. Ideo Ecclesiae calumniam sustinent et a veritate opprimuntur.

Amentes et caeci fuerunt, cum Christi miracula videntes, in Beelzebub ea fieri dicebant, et Christum Deum esse negabant (Matth. VII), nec veritatem videbant, id est intelligebant.

VERS. 29.-- Et palpes. Tenebrae factae sunt cum crucifigeretur Jesus usque in horam nonam, sicut 485Dtenebrae erant in cordibus Judaeorum.

Non dirigas: nec Christo praedicante crediderunt, nec per poenitentiam dirigi voluerunt.

VERS. 30.-- Uxorem accipias, et alius, etc. In uxore plebs haereticis sociata accipitur, cum qua diabolus fornicatur, dum in errorem seducitur. Animalia haereticis rapiuntur, cum subjecti ab eis liberantur: cum ipsi vita et doctrina privantur. Substantiam quoque, quam Judaei sibi docendo et scribendo singulariter praeparaverunt, Ecclesia sibi convertit in usum.

VERS. 33.-- Fructus terrae tuae. Tota caeremoniarum utilitas ad Ecclesiam translata est. Haec abundantia fruitur, Judaei egestate pereunt, unde per 486AIsaiam dicitur: Servi mei comedent, et vos esurietis (Isai. LXV).

VERS. 35.-- In genibus et in suris. Fortitudinem, scilicet et statibilitatem auferat. Vel in genibus percutiatur, cujus novissima opera damnantur.

A planta pedis. Sicut ab initio pravae voluntatis usque ad perfectionem operis malitiam exercuisti, unde a planta pedis usque ad verticem non est in eo sanitas (Isai. I). Hoc in ultima captivitate completum est.

VERS. 36.-- Ducetque te. Haeretici vel mali catholici, qui contemnunt praecepta Dei, a daemonibus et vitiis variis captivantur. Sicut servus, ita et dominus, et rex sicut populus, magister sicut discipulus.

486BEt regem. Osee regem Samariae Salmanasar rex Assyriorum cum decem tribubus duxit in captivitatem: Nabuchodonosor, Joachin, et Sedeciam regem Juda cum populo captivavit.

VERS. 38.-- Sementem, etc. Nobis quoque cavendum est, ne perdamus panem qui confirmat cor hominis, et vinum quod laetificat, et oleum quod exhilarat faciem.

VERS. 43.-- Advena. Aperte significatur abjectio Judaeorum et electio gentium, unde: Auferetur a vobis regnum Dei, et dabitur genti facienti fructus ejus, et alibi: Sic erunt novissimi primi, et primi novissimi.

(RAB. in Deut., tom. II.) Advena qui tecum versatur, etc. Creator omnium per incarnationis suae mysterium, etc., usque ad et sanguinem testamenti 486Cpollutum duxerit.

VERS. 66.-- Et erit vita. Nihil pejus inter maledicta, quae Judaeis merito superbiae acciderunt, quam videre vitam suam, id est Filium Dei pendentem, et non credere ei. Maledicta autem cum ex prophetia dicuntur: non de malo voto sunt, sed praedicuntur. Potuit autem sic sonare Judaeis: Videbis vitam tuam pendentem, et non credes vitae tuae, id est inter minas et dolos hostium vitam tuam videbis pendere ex incerto.

CHAPTER XXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- But if thou shalt hear. Just as he foretells curses and punishments to the disobedient, so he promises blessings and rewards to the obedient.

VERS. 4.-- The fruit of thy womb. He marks the diverse orders of subjects. For some furnish bodily helps to the teachers; others can labour at what has been enjoined upon them; others, content with simplicity, are eager to please God through innocence 484Bof life: every one has his own gift from God.

VERS. 5.-- Blessed are thy remnants. The reward of a good intention, even if the will has no effect, because peace to men of good will, etc. (Luc. II.)

VERS. 6.-- Coming in. He comes in through the door who, through the catholic faith, ponders and handles within according to the will of God: he goes out when he makes the same manifest before men to the praise of God.

VERS. 7.-- In thy sight. That thou mayest rejoice: The just shall rejoice when he shall see the revenge (Psal. LVII).

VERS. 8.-- And he shall bless. Granting increase and happy success to good works.

The heaven. The prophets and apostles, of whom it is said, The heavens shew forth the glory of God: whom the Lord opens 484Cwhen through them he grants an abundance of doctrine, that the hearts of carnal men, watered by this shower, may put forth the buds of good works.

Thou shalt lend. That is, so great will thy abundance be that thou wilt be able to give money to the nations, but they not so great. Or, ecclesiastical doctrine is understood.

Thou shalt lend to nations. The Church lends to many nations when she spends the divine graces upon the peoples. Nor does she take interest, because she seeks not a present recompense, but a future one. For she excels all, and is the head of the world, not the tail. She is always above, and not beneath; because although she suffers the pressures and persecutions of the wicked in the world, yet she is stronger than her adversaries, and by faith and virtue is pre-eminent over all. Whence, the Lord shall set thee at the head, etc.

484DVERS. 14.-- Thou shalt not turn aside aectinaveris. (AUG., quaest., 48.) LXX: Thou shalt not pass beyond all the words, etc., as far as nor are they to be worshipped on account of these things.

VERS. 15.-- But if thou wilt not hear. Just as he promised a blessing to those who keep the commandments, so he pronounces a curse upon those who neglect them.

VERS. 22.-- May the Lord strike thee. (AUG. in Enchirid., cap. 98.) Who would be so impiously senseless as to say that God cannot turn the evil wills of men — which he will, when he will, where he will — unto good? But when he does it, he does it through mercy: when he does not do it, he does not do it through judgment, who hath mercy on whom he will, and whom he will he hardeneth.

And with corrupted air. A life polluted by carnal uncleanness. 485AA carnal life polluted by carnal uncleanness. Carnal life is expressed by air, because the human body is quickened by the breath of the air.

VERS. 23.-- Let the heaven be. He compares the elements by which fruits are nourished to the hardest metals, because the sins of men change the order of things, and those things which were established for the comfort of the human race, whence: The whole world fought for him against the senseless.

The heaven is of brass to the transgressors of the law, when they merit no clemency from the holy teachers, but the harshest discipline, since they themselves have not a heart of flesh and a soft mind, but a stony and an iron one. Just as to a hard people the law was given on stone, that from the gift the receiver might be figured.

VERS. 24.-- May the Lord give dust for the rain of thy land: 485Bas if to say, just as dust and ashes bear no fruit, so let the doctrine poured into thee not stir up the buds of virtues, but foretell an everlasting destruction. For the dust signifies the barrenness of good works, the ashes the burning; because the chaff of sins shall be burned with unquenchable fire.

VERS. 25.-- And be dispersed. Just as the Jews, taken captive, were dispersed throughout the breadth of the world because they gathered themselves against God, so the generation of the wicked, dispersed by unclean spirits, is troubled through kinds of punishments, whence: Thy enemies shall perish, and all shall be scattered that work iniquity (Psal. XCI).

VERS. 27.-- May the Lord strike thee with the ulcer of Egypt. Ulcers are painful and festering malice. Blisters, inflated pride. Scab and itch, burning anger and the 485Cmadness of fury. These are inflicted in the present as a fitting punishment for sins: after this life (which is signified in the hinder parts) they shall bear the stench of their crimes in eternal torments.

May the Lord strike thee. Heretics and schismatics, wrapped in the blindness of error while the Gospel grows bright in the whole world, exercising their madness, contradict the catholic faith. Therefore they endure the calumny of the Church and are crushed by the truth.

Senseless and blind they were, when, seeing the miracles of Christ, they said that these were done in Beelzebub, and denied that Christ was God (Matth. VII), nor did they see the truth, that is, understand it.

VERS. 29.-- And thou shalt grope. Darkness came when Jesus was crucified, until the ninth hour, just as 485Dthere was darkness in the hearts of the Jews.

Mayest thou not direct: neither did they believe when Christ preached, nor were they willing to be directed through penance.

VERS. 30.-- Mayest thou take a wife, and another, etc. By the wife is taken the people joined to heretics, with whom the devil commits fornication while it is seduced into error. The beasts are snatched from the heretics when their subjects are freed from them: when they themselves are deprived of life and of doctrine. The substance too, which the Jews prepared for themselves singularly by teaching and by writing, the Church turns to her own use.

VERS. 33.-- The fruit of thy land. The whole usefulness of the ceremonies has been carried over to the Church. She enjoys abundance, the Jews perish of want, whence through 486AIsaiah it is said: My servants shall eat, and you shall be hungry (Isai. LXV).

VERS. 35.-- In the knees and in the calves. Let him take away strength, namely, and statibilitatem. Or let him be struck in the knees, whose last works are condemned.

From the sole of the foot. Just as from the beginning of a depraved will unto the completion of the deed thou hast exercised malice, whence from the sole of the foot unto the top of the head there is no soundness in him (Isai. I). This was fulfilled in the last captivity.

VERS. 36.-- And he shall lead thee. Heretics, or bad catholics, who despise the precepts of God, are taken captive by demons and by divers vices. As the servant, so also the master; and the king as the people, the teacher as the disciple.

486BAnd the king. Shalmaneser king of the Assyrians led Hosea king of Samaria into captivity with the ten tribes: Nebuchadnezzar took captive Joachin, and Zedekiah king of Judah, together with the people.

VERS. 38.-- Seed, etc. We too must take heed that we lose not the bread which strengthens the heart of man, and the wine which gladdens it, and the oil which makes the face cheerful.

VERS. 43.-- The stranger. The casting off of the Jews and the choosing of the Gentiles is openly signified, whence: The kingdom of God shall be taken away from you, and shall be given to a nation yielding the fruits thereof, and elsewhere: So shall the last be first, and the first last.

(RAB. in Deut., tom. II.) The stranger that dwelleth with thee, etc. The creator of all through the mystery of his incarnation, etc., as far as and shall have esteemed the blood of the testament 486Cpolluted.

VERS. 66.-- And thy life shall be. Nothing is worse among the curses which befell the Jews by the desert of their pride, than to see their own life, that is, the Son of God, hanging, and not to believe in him. But when curses are spoken out of prophecy: they are not from an evil wish, but are foretold. And it could sound thus to the Jews: Thou shalt see thy life hanging, and thou shalt not believe thy life — that is, amid the threats and the treacheries of enemies thou shalt see thy life hanging in uncertainty.

CAPUT XXIX.

VERS. 1.-- Haec sunt verba, etc. (AUG., quaest. 49 in Deut.) LXX: Haec sunt verba testamenti, etc., usque ad ad Abraham de circumcisione vel ad Noe.

486DVERS. 3.-- Tentationes magnas, etc. (AUG., quaest. 50.) Et non dedit Dominus Deus vobis cor scire, etc., usque ad quoniam judicia Dei quamvis occulta, tamen justa sunt.

VERS. 6.-- Panem non comedistis, etc. (AUG.) Hinc apparet Israelitas, etc., usque ad principium belli, sed principium vini.

VERS. 18.-- Ne forte sit. (AUG., quaest. 52.) LXX: Nunquid aliquis est inter vos, etc., usque ad non conjugem sibi concupiverat alienam.

VERS. 19, 20.-- Ebria sitientem, et Dominus, etc. Id est, ne quis plenus malitia et idololatria seducat eum qui sitit discere et scire veritatem, sicuti faciunt haeretici.

CHAPTER XXIX.

VERS. 1.-- These are the words, etc. (AUG., quaest. 49 in Deut.) LXX: These are the words of the testament, etc., as far as to Abraham concerning circumcision, or to Noah.

486DVERS. 3.-- The great temptations, etc. (AUG., quaest. 50.) And the Lord God hath not given you a heart to know, etc., as far as since the judgments of God, however hidden, are nevertheless just.

VERS. 6.-- You have not eaten bread, etc. (AUG.) Hence it appears that the Israelites, etc., as far as not the beginning of war, but the beginning of wine.

VERS. 18.-- Lest perhaps there be. (AUG., quaest. 52.) LXX: Is there any among you, etc., as far as he had not coveted another's wife for himself.

VERS. 19, 20.-- The drunken the thirsty, and the Lord, etc. That is, lest anyone full of malice and of idolatry seduce him who thirsts to learn and to know the truth, as the heretics do.

CAPUT XXX.

487A

VERS. 11.-- Mandatum hoc, quod ego, etc. (AUG., quaest. 54.) LXX: Quia mandatum hoc, etc., usque ad sine qua si foris manibus fiant, et in corde non fiant, praecepta Dei non implentur.

VERS. 14.-- Juxta te est sermo, etc. Id est mandata Dei moderata et contemperata sunt humanis viribus. Potest hoc de incarnatione Christi accipi, qui assumpta humanitate super terram visus est, et inter homines conversatus.

CHAPTER XXX.

487A

VERS. 11.-- This commandment, which I, etc. (AUG., quaest. 54.) LXX: For this commandment, etc., as far as without which, if they be done outwardly with the hands and be not done in the heart, the precepts of God are not fulfilled.

VERS. 14.-- The word is nigh thee, etc. That is, the commandments of God are measured and tempered to human strength. This can be taken of the incarnation of Christ, who, having assumed humanity, was seen upon earth and conversed among men.

CAPUT XXXI.

VERS. 7.-- Vocavitque. Verba Moysi ad Josue significant quod Christus terram viventium singulis secundum propria distribuit merita, et omnia promissa 487BDei impleat. Ipse enim introducit populum in terram promissam. Eo namque duce coelestem ingredimur patriam, qui ait: Nemo venit ad Patrem nisi per me (Joan. XIV). Et: Nemo ascendit in coelum nisi qui descendit de coelo, Filius hominis, qui est in coelo (Joan. III); qui enim unitur et per ipsum ascendit.

VERS. 10.-- Post septem annos. In quibus praesens vita signatur, quae septenario numero volvitur; vel tempus veteris legis propter sabbatum. Post septem ergo annos in anno remissionis lex in conventu legi praecipitur: quia completa legis observantia, lex spiritualis succedit. Unde: Ubi venit plenitudo temporis, misit Deus Filium suum natum ex muliere, etc. (Ephes. IV).

487CVERS. 24.-- Postquam ergo scripsit Moyses. Deuteronomium commendatur Levitis, quia Evangelium Christi per verorum sacerdotum ministerium in memoria reconditur Ecclesiae, quae est arca Domini, ubi coelestes thesauri reconduntur. Ibi duae tabulae testamenti, libri scilicet legis et Evangelii.
